ELEVEN EDIT — ARCHIVE
===================================================================
Created: 2026-07-21

WHAT THIS IS
  Material moved out of the live documents because it no longer changes what
  anyone would DO next. It is kept for provenance, for deeper dives, and so
  that no finding ever disappears without a trace.

HOW IT WORKS — ONE DIRECTION ONLY
  Things move IN. Nothing moves back out. This file is NOT read during normal
  work; the Primer and Technical Reference are complete without it.
  Every block moved out left a one-line pointer behind in the live doc.

WHAT IS **NOT** IN HERE, AND NEVER WILL BE
  Anything that stops a future session from repeating work stays live, however
  old it reads. Specifically these are NOT archivable:
    - Section 20, Confirmed Dead Ends — every line prevents a re-derivation.
    - The retraction notes: Sec 6's "typeA=0x07 is WRONG" warning and Sec 23's
      lowest-variant header. They exist precisely because someone could
      otherwise rebuild the old claim from first principles.
    - Any WITHDRAWN block quoted in place in the live docs.
    - Unresolved conflicts (currently C11, C12).
  The test for archiving is: does this change what I would DO next? If it only
  explains WHY something is the way it is, it can come here. If it would stop
  someone repeating a mistake, it stays.


===================================================================
PART 1 — CHANGE LOG, 2026-07-16 to 2026-07-18 (detail)
===================================================================
Moved from Technical Reference Section 24. These entries narrate the chain-map
decode, the TFX cross-check behind it, Phase 7a-1 (stereo/mono), Phase 8
(To Amp volume), and the original six-document consolidation. Every FACT they
established is live in Technical Reference Sections 1-23; what follows is the
account of how it was arrived at, and the reasoning that was discarded.

2026-07-18 (final+) — Eight more TFX files matching the nav-pass session log
  produced a mid <-> TFX-model-id bridge, which names 26 models automatically
  from the CMD 0x20 table. Section 23 now carries a consolidated model table
  covering every effect in the unit; only four rows remain inferred rather
  than confirmed. Method note recorded: patch filenames are unnecessary — a
  session log plus matching TFX files is sufficient and self-naming.
  Corrections from the bridge: TFX 0x22 is Orange Phaser (previously listed as
  a Roto Speaker candidate); TFX 0x55/0x56 confirmed as Dyn3 Compressor, fully
  vindicating the earlier retraction of the "parser artifact" flag.

2026-07-18 (final) — Five more TFX files (Fuchs series) completed the
  distortion set: 1D Tri-Knob Fuzz, 1E Black Op, 1F Green JRC, 57 White Boost,
  5B DC Distortion — all confirmed by patch filename. Also added Flanger (45)
  and two EQ/compressor sets. Corrected an earlier caveat: FX model 55/56 is
  real, not a parser artifact (verified against raw hex). A MultiChorus was
  observed in an FX slot, positively confirming the Sec 23 slot-legality mask.

2026-07-18 (last) — Nine TFX files parsed. Section 23 gained a TFX block
  parameter-set table (~15 models) and the finding that the TFX model ID
  encodes mono vs stereo instantiation — which explains both the duplicate
  names in the CMD 0x20 table and the post-amp handle reassignment on a
  stereo/mono toggle. TFX model numbering is NOT the CMD 0x20 numbering.

2026-07-18 (latest) — Chain map fully decoded after a TFX cross-check.
  Field 1 of each triplet is a BACK-LINK holding the previous block's SLOT ID,
  and slot IDs are fixed per block type in every patch (AMP 0x00, LOOP 0x01,
  VOL 0x02, WAH 0x03, MOD 0x04, REVERB 0x05, DELAY 0x06, DIST 0x07, FX1 0x08,
  FX2 0x09, INPUT 0x0B). This closes the FX1-vs-FX2 identification gap and
  replaces mid-range guessing as the way to identify blocks. It also REVERSES
  the same-day instruction "field 1 is a meaningless allocation index, do not
  use it" — it is the most useful field in the message.
  THIRD correction to Section 4 in one day (after the "rig container" and FX
  Loop position claims). All three had the same cause: inferring structure
  from too few samples without checking an independent source. The TFX Wst
  keys were available throughout and settled it immediately. LESSON: when a
  structure is inferred from one data source, check a second before writing
  it into this document as fact.

2026-07-18 (earlier) — Bypass solved, and a long-standing blocker found to be
  our own bug. CMD 0x11 paramLo 0x01 is per-block bypass; 0x06/0x14 remain
  amp/cab. The reason amp/cab bypass never worked is that our REQU format had
  the block handle hardcoded as 0x07 (Sec 3) — the DO-NOT-RETRY entry claiming
  the hardware ignores those queries has been WITHDRAWN as false. New Section
  23 holds the complete 65-entry effect model table from CMD 0x20, which also
  corrected two Section 4 claims made earlier the same day: chain map triplet 1
  is the INPUT block (mid 0x37 = "Guitar In"), not a rig container, and the FX
  Loop mid values are routing variants, not a pre/post-amp position code.

2026-07-18 — Phase 7a-1 complete. Stereo/Mono is now a working two-way
  control, not a read-only indicator. Send format confirmed
  (F0 13 0B 0F 00 0D [val] F7, val 0x01=Mono / 0x00=Stereo); echo confirmed
  unreliable and suppressed; front-panel broadcast decode confirmed; badge
  made clickable with a 300 ms lockout. Sections 6 and 10 rewritten, C10
  added. New USB-MIDI FRAMING block added to Section 2 — the root cause of
  a full day of failed builds was treating CIN framing bytes as payload, and
  that trap applies to every future command derived from a USB capture.
  Section 4 gained the stereo/mono handle-reassignment hazard, the FX Loop
  four-position placement rule, and — from an 8-patch nav pass with editor
  chain order recorded for each — the solved chain map structure: handles are
  sequential in chain order, mid is the effect model ID (AMP always 0x00),
  LOOP mid encodes side of amp, bypass state is NOT in the map, and the field
  we called "typeA" is a meaningless allocation index (renamed allocIdx).

2026-07-17 — Phase 8 complete. To Amp 1/2 volume knobs fully working:
  CMD 0x36 format confirmed (HW knob capture + Avid USB capture analysis);
  v0 encoding confirmed as (v127+64)%128; TFX body offsets confirmed (52/60);
  HW knob -> SW display tracking; SW drag -> HW response; TFX initial readback.
  CMD 0x37 (To Amp source): format confirmed from Avid capture; CMD 0x3A
  query-first requirement discovered (assert without it); query+write sequence
  implemented; no assert with query. SW sends do not move HW source — root
  cause unknown; dropdowns removed from GUI pending investigation.

2026-07-16 — This consolidation. Merged six source documents into one
  reference. Adopted Reference 07-14 as the structural backbone; folded in
  Findings 07-14/15/16 (all 33 amp tone tables, cab/mic/axis/breakup wiring,
  Bright toggle, stereo/mono, bypass findings, CMD 0x11 Format A/B) and the
  still-valid low-level facts from the June MIDI-era and July node-usb-era
  research (patch load/save, 7-bit codec, EHB bank format, rename, CC map).
  All conflicts recorded in Section 22 rather than silently resolved.

PART 2 — SESSION LOG ENTRIES, PROMOTED AND VERIFIED
===================================================================
These were marked [FOLDED IN], confirmed present in the main documents, and
moved here. The session log retains a one-line index of each.

Read these if you need the working detail behind a fact — the capture that
produced it, the wrong turns taken, and the reasoning at the time. The
conclusions are already in the Technical Reference.

-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-20 (late)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 3, C11, Sec 24; code shipped]
AREA:        Bypass — Format B false positive when a block handle is 0x04
FINDING:     MOD bypass state was never stored — "state unknown yet" on every
             click. Bypass routing detected Format B by testing
             data[6] === 0x04, but a block's runtime handle CAN be 0x04 (MOD,
             in certain chain orders). A Format A message from that block was
             read as Format B, every field parsed one byte off, paramLo 0x01
             never matched, state never stored.
FIX:         Before treating data[6] === 0x04 as a Format B marker, check
             whether 0x04 is a live handle in currentChain. If it is, data[6]
             IS the handle and the message is Format A. Covers every block,
             not just MOD — WAH, DIST or any other would have failed the same
             way once its handle landed on 0x04.
TESTED:      Build confirmed 2026-07-20 — "Bypass: MOD -> ACTIVE" now appears
             in the log and the click toggles correctly.
LESSON:      Same failure mode as C1: treating a byte whose VALUE is 0x04 as a
             structural marker when 0x04 is also a legal datum. Third time this
             family of bug has cost us a day. When a constant doubles as data,
             the discriminator must come from context, never from the value.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-20
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4, 6, 20, 21, 23, 24,
             C11; Primer DO NOT RETRY / HARDWARE SAFETY]
AREA:        Documentation sweep — superseded text removed, no new findings
FINDING:     A review of the 7/18-7/20 docs found no wrong findings, but
             seven places where a claim overturned during that week was still
             standing in a section nobody had reopened. Two were dangerous:
               - Sec 6's CMD 0x21 entry still described the old
                 "[typeA][mid][third], typeA=0x07=amp" model. That is the
                 exact belief that produced the wrong-handle defect; a future
                 session reading Sec 6 alone would have rebuilt the bug.
               - Sec 23's output-table header still asserted the C1
                 Chorus/Vibrato claim that Sec 4 had explicitly retracted,
                 then contradicted itself by extending the table with the
                 rule it had just declared unusable.
             The rest: Sec 4 and Sec 20 still called bypass disabled (shipped
             7/19); Sec 21 still listed chain-map typeA identification and the
             Stereo/Mono send path as open (both solved); Sec 6 still flagged
             CMD 0x37 as never-send and 0x3A/0x3C as unknown; the FORMAT B
             provenance doubt raised 7/19 had no conflict number.
ACTION:      All corrected in place. C11 added for FORMAT B. Change-log entries
             added for the 7/20 GUI work and for this sweep.
LESSON:      Fast sessions supersede facts faster than they sweep them, and
             the superseded copy is always in a section you did not open.
             When a finding overturns something, grep the WHOLE document for
             the old claim before closing the session.
NEXT:        The [FOLDED IN] entries below are all verified present in the
             main docs and are safe to delete whenever Charlie wants the log
             trimmed. Left in place pending his say-so.

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4, 23, 24]
AREA:        FX1 vs FX2 slot identity — RESOLVED, question closed
FINDING:     Resolved by TFX cross-check. Chain map field 1 is a back-link
             carrying the previous block's SLOT ID, and slot IDs are fixed
             per block type. FX1 = 0x08, FX2 = 0x09. Full detail in Tech Ref
             Sec 4. Supersedes the earlier "open question" entry and the
             earlier claim that field 1 was a meaningless allocation index.

DATE:        2026-07-19
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4, 24; code shipped]
AREA:        CMD 0x21 handler rewritten; amp handle was wrong on most patches
FINDING:     The old handler searched the chain map for a first byte of 0x07
             and took that triplet's handle as the amp, on the (documented,
             wrong) belief that field 1 was a type tag. Field 1 is a BACK-LINK
             carrying the PREVIOUS block's slot ID, and 0x07 is DIST's slot ID
             — so it actually found "the block after DIST". That coincides
             with the amp only when the amp immediately follows the
             distortion. Checked against 14 real patches: WRONG on 11.
             On those it addressed FX2, LOOP or REVERB instead, so every amp
             write (tone, gate, amp out, cab, mic, amp select) went to the
             wrong block, and incoming amp broadcasts were discarded by the
             instId guard as "not ours".
             Survived because the test patch set all had AMP after DIST.
FIX:         Handler now decodes back-links to recover slot IDs and stores the
             whole chain (position, slotId, name, modelId, handle) in
             currentChain. currentParamHi is derived from the block whose slot
             ID is 0x00. No call sites changed — currentParamHi keeps its name
             and meaning, it simply gets the right value now.
VERIFIED:    Decoder logic replayed over all 42 unique chain maps in the
             session logs: amp handle correct 42/42, all slots recognised.
AUDIT:       Other TFX decoders checked for the same fixed-position hazard.
             decodeAmpKey / tone / gate / ampOut anchor off a searched 6dls
             marker; decodeCabMicValues and decodeMonoStereo search by key —
             all safe. decodeToAmpVolumes uses fixed body offsets 52/60, which
             appear to be in the fixed global header rather than the variable
             keyed region, but this was NOT fully verifiable (decoded bulk body
             is not byte-identical to a saved TFX). Confirm when To Amp work
             resumes — note To Amp is already on the known-broken list.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-19 PASSED. session-2026-07-19-091431.log —
             8 chain maps, amp handle correct 8/8, all slots recognised, amp
             control working on B2 Singtall (previously dead) and A1 unchanged.
             The log also captured six live reorders, all tracked correctly,
             with the amp handle preserved throughout — an independent
             confirmation of the reorder handle-preservation finding.
             Stale comment in protocol.js repeating the old "typeA" model also
             corrected, to stop it re-infecting future work.

DATE:        2026-07-19
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 3, 24; Primer; code shipped]
AREA:        Bypass implemented — amp, cab, and per-block state capture
SCOPE:       Amp and cab bypass are now clickable and live. Per-block bypass
             is CAPTURED AS STATE ONLY, deliberately with no UI: the chain row
             is still fixed-order HTML, so wiring clicks to chain-dist,
             chain-fxloop etc. would address whichever block happens to sit in
             that position — exactly the defect fixed earlier today, but in the
             UI layer. Per-block UI waits for a chain row rebuilt from
             currentChain.
CHANGES:     transport.js  sendBypassWrite / sendAmpBypass / sendCabBypass and
                           requestAmpCabBypass (first use of REQU PARAM).
             sysex-handler.js  bypass routing for paramLo 0x01/0x06/0x14 on ANY
                           handle, placed BEFORE the amp-only instId guard;
                           the four "disabled until chain reorder" stubs
                           removed; requestAmpCabBypass fired after each map.
             state.js      blockBypass{} keyed by slot ID, cabBypassActive,
                           bypass paramLo/v0 constants; cleared on disconnect.
             ui.js         click handlers on chain-amp / chain-cab; the two
                           previously-DEAD display functions now called, and
                           extended to render an unknown state.
             protocol.js   KLUDGE REMOVED — decodeCabMicValues no longer
                           hard-codes ampActive=true. It returns null (unknown)
                           and the real value is queried once a handle exists.
             index.html    tooltips corrected (they claimed "read only"),
                           pointer cursor only when state is known.
NOTE:        No optimistic display on click. The hardware broadcasts the new
             state immediately and that is what updates the UI, so a failed
             send leaves the display truthful rather than lying. Different
             choice from the Stereo/Mono badge, which had to be optimistic
             because its echo is unreliable.
NOTE:        The real REQU response is FORMAT A shaped (data[6]=handle), not
             the data[6]=0x04 "FORMAT B" this file's older comments describe.
             The bypass router handles both, but FORMAT B's provenance is
             doubtful and may itself be a USB-framing misread. Worth revisiting
             before relying on it elsewhere.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-19 PASSED — session-2026-07-19-100225.log.
             - First ever parameter REQU from this app succeeded:
                 OUT F0 13 0B 0F 01 11 51 06 F7
                 IN  F0 13 0B 0F 12 11 51 06 40 00 00 00 00 F7
             - All ELEVEN bypass targets (ten blocks + cab) observed in the
               BYPASSED state, proving nothing defaults to active any more.
             - Amp/cab bypass worked on six different amp handles (0x04, 0x12,
               0x21, 0x51, 0x60, 0x75) and at chain positions including LAST.
             - Ignored CMD 0x11 lines fell from 104 to 14; the remainder are
               non-bypass params from other handles, correctly dropped.
             - Zero errors, zero unknown-handle bypass events.

DATE:        2026-07-19
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4, 23; Primer; code shipped]
AREA:        Chain row — true order, hover text, stereo connectors, real bypass
FOUND:       index.html contained an inline click handler on every chain label
             that flipped slot-on/slot-off LOCALLY and sent nothing ("TODO
             Phase 5+: send bypass when wired"). So any slot could be clicked
             into a colour that did not match the hardware, and on amp/cab it
             briefly lied before our real broadcast corrected it. REMOVED, with
             a comment warning against reinstating a cosmetic toggle.
CHANGES:     protocol.js  MODEL_NAMES (all 65 mids, for hover text) and
                          MODEL_OUT_STEREO (30 mids, for the connectors).
             state.js     SLOT_ID_TO_DOM mapping.
             ui.js        renderChainRow() — MOVES the existing slot divs into
                          chain order rather than rebuilding, so each div keeps
                          its data-panel and its listeners and the ▼ mapping
                          follows the block for free. Sets hover text on BOTH
                          the label and the ▼ for a larger target. Sets each
                          connector to a double arrow when the preceding block
                          outputs stereo. Plus refreshBlockBypassDisplays() and
                          a delegated click handler doing REAL per-block bypass.
             sysex-handler.js  calls renderChainRow() after every chain map and
                          repaints slots when block bypass state arrives.
             index.html   fake handler removed.
DESIGN:      Ten fixed slots, always all present, reordered — not a variable
             list. Slots are moved, never rebuilt. No optimistic repaint on
             click; the hardware broadcast repaints, so a failed send leaves
             the display truthful.
KNOWN GAP:   MODEL_OUT_STEREO covers 30 of ~52 usable models. An unknown mid
             draws a SINGLE connector and logs a warning naming the mid and
             model, so gaps surface during use rather than being drawn wrong.
             Simulated over real chain maps: most rows fully resolved, a few
             show one or two unknown connectors as expected.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-19 PASSED (session-2026-07-19-133235.log +
             screenshot). Order matches the Avid editor across ~30 chain
             states including many live reorders made in the Avid editor while
             our app was running; hover text correct; per-block bypass real and
             audible on all ten blocks.
SECOND PASS: Two issues found and fixed the same session.
             (a) Connectors used one/two arrow GLYPHS, so the row re-flowed as
                 patches changed. Replaced with stacked horizontal lines at a
                 FIXED 16px width, matching the Avid editor's look.
             (b) An unknown model drew a SINGLE SOLID line — indistinguishable
                 from a confident "mono". That was a real honesty flaw, and it
                 is what made the feature read as broken. Unknown now draws a
                 DASHED line.
             Coverage raised 30 -> 49 of 52 models by applying the lowest-
             variant rule (Tech Ref Sec 23). The rule was validated by it
             independently producing EP Tape Echo 0x1D = STEREO, matching the
             Avid rendering in a screenshot taken BEFORE the rule was applied.
             Only FX Loop variants 0x2E/0x2F/0x33 remain unresolved.
CAUTION:     19 of the 49 entries are DERIVED, not observed. If a connector
             disagrees with the Avid editor, report the block and model id —
             it is more likely the rule meeting an uncovered case than a
             rendering bug.

DATE:        2026-07-19
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4; Primer; code shipped]
AREA:        Chain row drag-to-reorder
CHANGES:     transport.js  sendChainOrder(newOrder) — builds a complete 41-byte
                           CMD 0x21 with dir=0x00, re-using each block's model
                           id and handle and re-linking the back-links.
             ui.js         HTML5 drag on the slot containers, drop indicator,
                           computeReorder(), enforceLoopPlacement(),
                           wireChainDrag(); bypass click suppressed for the
                           synthetic click that trails a drag.
             index.html    grab/grabbing cursors, drag opacity, drop-edge
                           marker; hint text now "drag blocks to reorder".
VERIFIED BEFORE BUILD:
             - Message construction reproduces ALL FOUR real Avid reorder sends
               from Drag_Test_1.pcapng BYTE FOR BYTE.
             - Loop snapping reproduces Avid's observed result on the one case
               we captured (VOL dropped between AMP-CAB and LOOP lands to the
               RIGHT of the loop).
             - Loop placement legal in all edge cases tried.
DESIGN:      No optimistic reorder. We send, the hardware broadcasts what it
             actually adopted, renderChainRow() draws that. Necessary because
             the firmware may return different model ids and handles than we
             sent when a move changes a block's channel configuration.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-19 PASSED — session-2026-07-19-141945.log.
             21 drags performed in our app, all adopted correctly; 8 FX Loop
             snaps, all landing legal; 12 further chain renders from moves made
             in the Avid editor while our app was running, all tracked.
             Zero errors. Zero accidental bypass toggles — 8 deliberate bypass
             writes, none within a second of a drag, so the guard against the
             synthetic post-drag click works.
NOTE:        Our drags are identifiable in a log by the sendChainOrder line;
             moves made in the Avid editor or on the front panel produce a
             chain render with no preceding send.
RESOLVED:    The stranded-loop question. Avid drags the loop ALONG WITH the amp
             when adjacent (so the amp cannot pass position 9 with the loop on
             its right until the loop is parked elsewhere). We deliberately do
             NOT copy that — dragging the amp leaves the loop, snapping it if
             stranded. Charlie prefers the freer behaviour. Recorded in Sec 4
             as an intentional divergence.

DATE:        2026-07-20
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: code shipped]
AREA:        GUI layout — effect panel opened in the wrong place
FINDING:     Clicking a chain ▼ hid the amp panel, which let the auto-advance
             (roller) strip slide UP into the vacated space, and the effect
             panel then appeared BELOW it. Cause was purely DOM ORDER inside
             #main:  panel-ampcab > roller-strip > panel-other.
FIX:         Moved #panel-other to sit immediately AFTER #panel-ampcab and
             BEFORE the roller strip, so it takes the amp panel's slot. Also
             copies the amp panel's rendered offsetHeight onto #panel-other
             before hiding it — the amp panel is content-sized, so without
             that the column still shrinks and everything below shifts.
             Comment left in the markup explaining the ordering requirement,
             since the bug is invisible until a panel is opened.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-20 PASSED — panel occupies the amp panel's
             slot and the auto-advance strip no longer moves.

DATE:        2026-07-20
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer WORKING / NOT YET / CODE FILE MAP;
             Technical Reference Sec 15; code shipped]
AREA:        Phase 4a — GUI Redesign Round 2
FINDING:     Full GUI cleanup pass. All changes confirmed in build tests
             by Charlie. Details:

  STATUS BAR / TOPBAR:
    - Bridge connection dot + label removed from topbar; merged into
      status bar right as "● Eleven Rack (Java bridge) — Connected".
      midi-label JS updated to show "Connected" not the device name.
    - Avid Editor status block removed from topbar entirely; avid-status-
      badge element kept hidden for JS compatibility.
    - avid-warn ("AVID EDITOR: OPEN") moved to status bar LEFT so it
      no longer crowds the bridge text on the right.
    - "Eleven Edit" label, status-msg, and log-path-label all hidden in
      status bar (elements kept for JS).

  TFX / BANK TOOLBAR:
    - Scan Bank hidden (code kept). Open Captures Folder hidden (moved
      to Settings). Change Folder hidden (replaced by Settings).
    - "Capture Current Patch" + "Save to Current Slot" + "Save to Slot…"
      + "Rename Patch…" — all four hidden and replaced by single
      SAVE ▾ dropdown with three items:
        · Save to Rack… — name-prompt modal (pre-filled, max 16 chars,
          slot label shown); on confirm: rename via CMD 0x05, commit to
          current slot (full 4-step sequence), then auto-captures TFX to
          disk with the new name. Implemented in saveToRackAndDisk(name)
          in capture-scan.js.
        · Save to Disk — same name-prompt modal (slot line hidden);
          on confirm: updates currentPatchName + display, then
          captureCurrentPatchNow() for TFX only, no slot commit.
        · Save to Different Slot… — greyed, coming soon.
    - saveCurrentPatchToSlot() now accepts optional nameOverride param;
      updates currentPatchName and display before sending so TFX capture
      picks up the new name. Max 16 chars, trimmed.
    - About button moved to far right next to ⚙ gear button.
    - btoolbar font-size now a single line (font-size: 12px on #btoolbar;
      .bt-btn uses font-size: inherit). Matches roller-strip pattern.

  SETTINGS PANEL (new):
    - Gear ⚙ button added far right of TFX/Bank toolbar.
    - Settings panel opens in the amp panel slot (same DOM mechanic as
      effect panels). ✕ close button top-right; gear highlights amber
      when open; chain ▼ clicks also close it.
    - Captures Folder row: Set Folder… (native picker, saves to
      settings.json 'capturesDir'), Open Folder, path display.
    - Avid Editor Folder row: Set Folder… (saves to settings.json
      'avidDir'), path display.
    - Open Logs Folder button.
    - Paths populated from disk on each panel open.
    - New IPC handlers in main.js: browse-avid-dir, get-avid-dir,
      get-logs-dir. Exposed in preload.js.

  LOGS:
    - Log files moved from userData root to userData/logs/ subfolder.
      Folder created automatically. get-logs-dir returns the subfolder.

DATE:        2026-07-20
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 4; Primer; code shipped]
AREA:        Per-block bypass never queried — yesterday's pass was a FALSE
             POSITIVE caused by the Avid editor being open alongside
FINDING:     Clicking any of the nine non-amp chain slots did nothing. Cause:
             requestAmpCabBypass() only ever queried paramLo 0x06 and 0x14 on
             the AMP block. Nothing in the app ever asked the other nine blocks
             for their bypass state, and the hardware does not volunteer it.
             So blockBypass[slotId] stayed undefined for those nine and the
             click handler bailed out by design ("state unknown yet").
             IT LOOKED LIKE IT WORKED on 7/19 because the Avid editor was
             running at the same time: ITS queries produced broadcasts that we
             picked up and stored. Verified after the fact — the 7/19 log
             contains ZERO paramLo 0x01 queries sent by us.
             Charlie diagnosed this; I had recorded the 7/19 test as passing.
LESSON:      Running the Avid editor alongside makes our app look more capable
             than it is, because we adopt broadcasts triggered by ITS queries.
             ANY readback feature must be tested with the Avid editor CLOSED
             before it is called working.
FIX:         requestAmpCabBypass() replaced by requestAllBypass(), which walks
             currentChain and queries every block: paramLo 0x01 for the nine
             ordinary blocks plus 0x06 and 0x14 on the amp. Called after
             renderChainRow() on each chain map, so currentChain is populated.
             Costs 11 queries per chain map — the same thing the Avid editor
             does at every patch load.
TESTED:      Build test 2026-07-20 PASSED. All ten blocks now report state and
             all nine non-amp slots toggle correctly.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-21
STATUS:      FOLDED — promoted to Tech Ref Sec 23 (DIST CMD 0x11 param map)
AREA:        DIST paramLo numbers — all five models
FINDING:     Confirmed from Distorion_Captures_1.pcapng (722 CMD 0x11 OUT writes
             across 6 DIST patches, 5 distinct models captured):
               3-knob (Tri-Knob Fuzz, Black Op Distortion, Green JRC Overdrive):
                 Driv = 0x02, Tone = 0x03, Levl = 0x04
               4-knob (White Boost, DC Distortion):
                 Driv = 0x02, Treb = 0x03, Bass = 0x04, Levl = 0x05
               Bypass = 0x01 (all models — already confirmed globally)
             3-knob mapping confirmed by Black Op sequential knob drag.
             4-knob Levl=0x05 confirmed by exclusive presence on 4-knob handles.
             4-knob Treb=0x03/Bass=0x04 ordering is inferred from sequential
             pattern — high confidence but not independently verified without TFX.
EVIDENCE:    Distorion_Captures_1.pcapng; model order confirmed by Charlie:
             Black Op → DC Dist → Green JRC → Tri-Knob → White Boost → Black Op.
             Model change = CMD 0x21 (dir=0x00), same as chain reorder.
CONFLICTS:   Tech Ref Sec 23 currently has param NAMES but no paramLo numbers
             for DIST. These are additive — no conflict.

             If passes, promote paramLo numbers into Tech Ref Sec 23 and mark
             confirmed. Also confirm 4-knob Treb/Bass ordering at same time.

===================================================================
PART 3 — NAVIGATION / FLICKER INVESTIGATION, 2026-07-22 to 2026-07-23
===================================================================
Moved from the session log 2026-07-23. This is the build-by-build narrative of
a single investigation: why our app made the rack's front panel flicker twice
on a patch change when the Avid editor did not. It ran from build A through
build H over two days and ended with patch navigation rebuilt.

Every CONCLUSION is live in the Technical Reference — the CMD 0x02 recall form
(Sec 6), the CMD 0x36 and CMD 0x0D read paths (Sec 5), the 35-bit multi-byte
value format (Sec 7), Stereo/Mono and To Amp corrected to per-patch (Sec 10,
18, C13), the paramLo-equals-slider-id rule (Sec 11), the navigation dead ends
(Sec 20), and the Sync/Speed interlock (Sec 21). The query-budget finding is
in the Primer.

What follows is the working detail: what was tried, what each build changed,
which hypotheses were wrong, and the raw observations behind the conclusions.
Read it if you need to know WHY, or to re-open a question that was closed.

-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (later)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 3 (0x06/0x14 amp-handle rule), Sec 23
             (REVERB map note), Sec 24; Primer WORKING / CODE FILE MAP]
AREA:        CMD 0x11 bypass routing — paramLo 0x06 handle collision
FINDING:     paramLo 0x06 = AMP BYPASS, but ONLY on the amp block's handle.
             The bypass router matched 0x06 (and 0x14 cab) on ANY handle. No
             other block used 0x06 until now, so it stayed latent. Eleven SR
             REVERB Pre-Delay is paramLo 0x06 on the REVERB handle (0x61 in the
             capture) — so every Pre-Delay turn was read as an amp-bypass event
             (GUI showed AMP going bypassed) and the Pre-Delay READBACK was
             swallowed here instead of reaching the knob (hence "no initial
             readback"). Hardware amp state never actually changed, matching
             Charlie's both-programs test.
FIX:         Amp-bypass (0x06) and cab-bypass (0x14) now count as bypass only
             when the message arrives on currentParamHi (the amp handle);
             otherwise they fall through to per-block routing, where the reverb
             handler updates Pre-Delay. Block bypass (0x01) unchanged.
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-07-22-114841.log — IN F0 13 0B 0F 02 11 61 06 7F ...
             logged as "Bypass: AMP -> ACTIVE"; amp real handle 0x5B, reverb
             handle 0x61. Same bug FAMILY as the 0x04-handle case (Archive):
             a value that is a control code in one context is data in another —
             disambiguate by handle/context, never by the value alone.
ALSO:        Pre-Delay now displays milliseconds over 0-200 (was 0-10 like the
             other knobs). Mapping ASSUMED LINEAR, max 200 ms — needs a quick
             hardware confirm of the value shown at full knob.
NEXT:        Re-test solo (Avid closed): open Eleven SR REVERB, confirm all 5
             controls read back on open incl Pre-Delay; sweep Pre-Delay and
             confirm NO amp-bypass flicker and the ms readout tracks; confirm
             real amp bypass (chain-slot click) still works. If good, fold the
             0x06-only-on-amp-handle rule into Tech Ref (bypass section) and
             confirm the ms scale.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22
STATUS:      provisional — change written, awaiting hardware test
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Patch recall method — Program Change vs absolute-slot SysEx
FINDING:     Avid sends NO Program Change at any time. Every patch recall,
             sequential or jump, is a single
               F0 13 0B 0F 00 02 <tail truncated in capture>
             on the Vendor-Specific port. Arbitrary bank jumps cannot be
             relative, so the truncated tail must carry the absolute slot.
             This matches patch-identity form 4 already in the Tech Ref:
               F0 13 0B 0F 00 02 [0x00] [raw_slot] F7
             Our app used C0 nn (Program Change), inherited from pre-Java-
             bridge builds. Hypothesis for the double flicker seen on the HW
             front panel: a Program Change triggers the Rack's own native
             recall behaviour ON TOP OF the patch load, whereas Avid's
             absolute-slot SysEx does not. Our post-change readback burst is
             NOT the cause — logs show exactly one load per navigation, one
             bulk dump, no write-back.
CAPTURE
CAVEAT:      This USBPcap setup drops the FINAL 4-byte USB-MIDI group of every
             message. Proven by a name response reading "Bassguy 5" when the
             true name is "Bassguy 59" — the closing group (39 00 F7) is gone.
             Trailing arguments are therefore invisible in all three captures.
             Re-check this before trusting any byte-level tail taken from them.
EVIDENCE:    Avid_Edit_Patch_Changes_.pcapng (Avid, A1-A2-A3-A4 sequential)
             Eleven_Edit_Patch_Changes.pcapng (ours, same walk)
             Avid_Editor_JumpList_Patch_Changes.pcapng (Avid, A1-B2-C3-D4 jumps)
             session-2026-07-22-154654.log (ours, same session as capture)
CONFLICTS:   Tech Ref Sec 20 lists "SysEx for patch load via the standard MIDI
             DIN port — silently ignored" as a dead end. Not in conflict: our
             SysEx goes out the bridge OUT port that already carries working
             CMD 0x01/0x11 traffic, not the DIN port.
CHANGE:      patch-nav.js sendPC() now emits F0 13 0B 0F 00 02 00 <slot> F7,
             guarded by USE_SYSEX_RECALL. Set that flag false to revert to
             Program Change instantly. No other call site touched — goToSlot,
             auto-roll and keyboard nav are unchanged.
UNCONFIRMED: raw_slot encoding. Our slots are 0-based (A1=0, A2=1); the Tech
             Ref example shows raw_slot 04. Verify against the slot echoed
             back in the 0E 02 02 announcement after a recall.
NEXT:        Test A1 to A2/A3/A4. Watch the front panel for one flicker vs two,
             and confirm the echoed slot matches the slot requested. If the
             display does not move at all, the encoding is wrong, not the
             method — try the raw_slot variant before abandoning.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (later)
STATUS:      provisional — recall CONFIRMED; flicker cause still open
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Patch recall — SysEx result, and the bulk request as flicker suspect
CONFIRMED:   Absolute-slot SysEx recall works.
               F0 13 0B 0F 00 02 00 <slot> F7
             raw_slot is plain 0-based with NO translation: A2 = 0x01,
             A4 = 0x03, D4 = 0x0F. Hardware echoes the requested slot back in
             both 0x03 and 0x02 announcements (e.g. 0F 02 02 00 0F for D4).
             Sequential nav and a non-adjacent jump to D4 both landed
             correctly. Program Change is no longer used for navigation.
             SIDE EFFECT: announcements now arrive with direction byte 0x0F
             rather than 0x0E, matching the Avid capture exactly. Our 0x02 /
             0x03 handlers accept both — worth noting before anything is
             written that keys on 0x0E.
RULED OUT:   Program Change is NOT the cause of the double front-panel
             flicker. It persists unchanged after the switch to SysEx recall.
             Also previously ruled out: a second load or a write-back from us
             (logs show exactly one recall per navigation).
NEW
HYPOTHESIS:  The CMD 0x01 bulk TFX request is the second flicker. Avid never
             requests CMD 0x01 at any point. Its post-recall sequence is
             21, 05, 07, 0A, 08, 03, 50, 34, then the 11 bypass sweep. Ours
             leads with 01 — a 1196-1361 byte dump, the only large transfer
             in the sequence, arriving a beat after the recall.
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-07-22-175415.log (SysEx recall, slot echoes, flicker
             still present), Avid_Edit_Patch_Changes_.pcapng and
             Avid_Editor_JumpList_Patch_Changes.pcapng (no CMD 0x01 anywhere).
CHANGE:      transport.js requestPatchStateAfterNav() gains REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV,
             shipped set to FALSE for this diagnostic. While false, everything
             decoded from the bulk body is stale or blank: amp name, tone
             knobs, gate, amp out, cab/mic, stereo/mono, to-amp volumes.
             Expected. Set true to restore.
NEXT:        Nav A1-A2-A3-A4 with the flag false and watch the panel. If the
             second flicker is gone, the bulk request is confirmed as the
             cause. The fix is then NOT to drop the bulk — we need that data —
             but to work out how Avid obtains it without CMD 0x01. Its
             08, 50, 34 and 0A queries are unimplemented on our side and are
             the obvious place to look.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (night)
STATUS:      provisional — flicker cause CONFIRMED; amp paramLo map NOT solved
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Flicker cause = CMD 0x01 bulk request; attempt at amp paramLo map
CONFIRMED:   The CMD 0x01 bulk TFX request is the second front-panel flicker.
             With REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV=false the flicker is GONE, navigation and
             a jump to D4 both correct, and all bulk-derived readouts blank as
             expected. Evidence: session-2026-07-22-180229.log.
             Cause chain now fully resolved: not Program Change, not a double
             load, not a write-back — it is our own bulk request.
CONFIRMED:   CMD 0x11 value encoding. Five 7-bit bytes form a 35-bit
             offset-binary number N; raw = (N - 0x400000000) >> 3, signed.
             0x40 00 00 00 00 = 0 (centre), 0x7F 7F 7F 7F 7F = +2147483647.
             Display value = 64 + raw/2147483647*63 (0-127). Verified against
             TFX-derived raws in session-2026-07-22-154654.log.
             NOTE: captures lose the 5th byte, costing ~16 counts of 2.1e9 —
             negligible, but the byte IS real; do not drop it when sending.
CONFIRMED:   Avid reads the amp as a sweep of paramLo 0x01..0x19 against the
             amp's runtime handle from CMD 0x21 (handle was 126 / 13 / 27 on
             the three patches). 39 CMD 0x11 queries per patch change total:
             25 amp params + the 11 per-block bypass flags + 3 duplicates
             (0x06, 0x14, 0x15 queried twice — bypass and param overlap).
             No CMD 0x01 anywhere in any Avid capture.
NOT SOLVED:  The amp paramLo -> control map. Correlating the 25 values against
             our decoded readouts over three patches gave only two consistent
             hits: paramLo 0x08 = Mid (99) and 0x0B = Vol Norm (35) on Bassguy
             59. Neighbours are close but wrong — 0x07 reads 106 vs our Treble
             109, 0x09 reads 91 vs our Bass 85, 0x0A reads 46 vs our Vol
             Bright 43 — off by 3-6 counts with no consistent offset. Gate,
             amp out, cab and mic do not appear in the 25 at all.
TWO
EXPLANATIONS: (a) paramLo is amp-model-specific, as already documented for
             DIST. The three test patches use three different amps (Tweed
             Bass, Tread Vin, SL100 Drive), so slot 0x09 need not mean the
             same knob on each.
             (b) Our TFX tone decode is a few counts off on some knobs. It
             locates the amp by scanning the body for a key and reading at
             fixed offsets — a search, not a fixed layout. If so the param
             values are the more trustworthy source and our present readouts
             are slightly wrong.
             Captures alone cannot separate these. NO MAP GUESSED.
NEXT:        Knob-sweep, one amp model: turn each amp knob on the hardware and
             record which (handle, paramLo) broadcasts. Six knobs. Then repeat
             on a second amp with the same knob count — that single comparison
             decides whether numbering is universal or per-model, which sets
             the size of the whole job. It also settles (b) for free: if swept
             values disagree with our TFX readouts, the TFX decode needs
             fixing regardless of the flicker work.
INTERIM:     Set REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV back to TRUE for normal use until the map
             exists. The SysEx recall is an independent confirmed win and
             stays either way.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, later)
STATUS:      diagnostic build issued — awaiting results
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Probe of Avid's five undecoded queries + clean amp param sweep
NOTE ON
OLD SWEEPS:  Previously captured Wireshark knob-sweeps ARE still usable.
             The truncation drops only the final 4-byte group, which on a CMD
             0x11 response is the 5th value byte. Handle, paramLo and the top
             four value bytes all survive, and a sweep only needs to show
             WHICH paramLo moves. Do not discard those captures.
RATIONALE:   Before hand-sweeping ~30 amp models, check whether the amp
             parameter map can be obtained directly. Avid sends CMD 0x03,
             0x08, 0x0A, 0x34 and 0x50 after every recall and we have never
             decoded any of them; all five are short messages, so capture
             truncation destroyed their whole payload. Our own logs are not
             truncated. If one returns a parameter descriptor for the loaded
             amp, the entire per-model mapping job disappears.
CHANGE:      protocol.js   — REQU_PROBE_03/08/0A/34/50 and PROBE_DUMP_CMDS.
             sysex-handler.js — raw full-hex dump of any response whose CMD is
                            in PROBE_DUMP_CMDS, before normal dispatch; no
                            existing handler is bypassed or altered.
             transport.js  — PROBE_UNKNOWN_QUERIES fires the five queries
                            after each nav; PROBE_AMP_PARAM_SWEEP sweeps
                            paramLo 0x01..0x19 against currentParamHi and logs
                            every reply in full, giving untruncated values to
                            correlate. Both run AFTER the normal state pull so
                            they cannot disturb it. Both default true.
             REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV stays FALSE for this build, to keep the log
             readable and the flicker out of the way.
TEST:        Nav across three or four patches on DIFFERENT amp models, then
             send the log. Looking for: any of the five returning per-amp
             parameter information, and a clean 25-value sweep per amp.
NEXT IF NO
DESCRIPTOR: fall back to knob-sweep on two amps of equal knob count to decide
             universal vs per-model numbering before committing to all 30.

DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, final)
STATUS:      STABLE BUILD RESTORED — flicker work paused, not abandoned
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Rollback to known-good, plus two hardware-confirmed facts
ROLLBACK:    REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV back to TRUE, PROBE_UNKNOWN_QUERIES and
             PROBE_AMP_PARAM_SWEEP both FALSE. App behaves as it did before
             the flicker investigation, except patch recall is now SysEx
             instead of Program Change (confirmed good, kept).
             The double front-panel flicker RETURNS with this build. That is
             the accepted trade for correct readouts.
CAUSE OF
"SKETCHY":   Tone-stack readback failing in the diagnostic build was a direct
             consequence of REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV=false, not a pre-existing
             fault. Tone values are decoded from the bulk body; with the bulk
             request suppressed they only refreshed when some other event
             happened to trigger a bulk (hardware save, bank load). Restoring
             the flag restores the readouts. No underlying app defect implied.
             Secondary factor, still true and still unfixed: in the sweep the
             amp identity arrived AFTER the tone values, because identity
             comes from the bulk. Any future CMD 0x11 replacement must query
             paramLo 0x0F first and wait for it before sweeping tone.
CONFIRMED
BY HARDWARE
TEST:        To Amp 1 and To Amp 2 volumes are PER-PATCH, not global. User set
             both to specific values, saved to the rack, navigated away and
             back — settings returned with the patch. This CONTRADICTS the
             comment on sendToAmpVolume in transport.js which calls CMD 0x36
             "To Amp volume (global)". That comment is wrong and should be
             corrected when the area is next touched.
             Mono/Stereo is also PER-PATCH, not global.
             Consequence: both must be re-read on every patch change, so
             neither can be dropped from a bulk replacement, and neither has
             a known read path outside the bulk body. CMD 0x36 is write-plus-
             echo only; no query form exists anywhere in the code.
BULK
REPLACEMENT
COVERAGE:    Covered by CMD 0x11: amp identity 0x0F, gate 0x04/0x05, amp out
             0x03, tone knobs per-amp lo, cab 0x15, mic 0x16, axis 0x17,
             breakup 0x18.
             NOT covered, no known read path: Mono/Stereo, To Amp 1 volume,
             To Amp 2 volume.
             Until those three have a read path, the bulk request cannot be
             removed without losing readouts. This is the blocker.
OPEN LEADS:  Avid reads these somehow without ever requesting CMD 0x01. Its
             undecoded per-recall queries remain the best candidates:
             CMD 0x08 returns four 5-byte blocks and DOES vary by patch —
               A1 gave 00 7F 02 00 19 then 00 7F 02 00 64 x2 then 00 7F 00 00 64,
               while the other four patches gave 00 7F 00 00 64 in all four
               blocks. Four blocks, per-patch, small values — plausible fit
               for the To Amp pair plus two others. UNPROVEN.
             CMD 0x0A returned 04/00/00/04/04 across the five patches — a
               single byte that changes per patch. Plausible Mono/Stereo, but
               all five patches were STEREO so nothing was distinguished.
               UNPROVEN.
             CMD 0x50 four bytes, varies per patch, does not track the amp
               (lead800 and treadvint returned identical values). UNPROVEN.
NEXT WHEN
RESUMED:     Single-variable experiments, one at a time, on ONE patch:
             set To Amp 1 to a distinctive value and re-probe, watching CMD
             0x08 block 1; then toggle Mono/Stereo and re-probe, watching
             CMD 0x0A and 0x50. Change one thing, look at one field.
             Do NOT attempt the bulk replacement before all three unknowns
             have a proven read path.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, build B)
STATUS:      A/B differential test build issued
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Read path for To Amp 1/2 and Mono/Stereo — outer (rig) level
MODEL
CORRECTION:  Earlier framing of "per-patch vs global" was WRONG. Everything in
             question is per-patch. The real distinction is WHICH COMMAND
             carries the value, and there are two levels:
               INNER (amp block) — gate, amp out, tone stack, cab, mic.
                 Read via CMD 0x11 against the amp's runtime handle.
               OUTER (rig level) — rig volume, To Amp 1/2, Mono/Stereo.
                 Each has its own top-level command number.
             Rig Volume proves the outer level already works the way we need:
             CMD 0x07 has a dedicated read, F0 13 0B 0F 01 07 F7, fired on
             every nav, reliable, per-patch, never needed the bulk dump.
             So To Amp is not missing a read because the hardware lacks one —
             we have only ever WRITTEN CMD 0x36 and never tried reading it.
BUILD B:     REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV = TRUE  (app fully functional, readouts good)
             PROBE_UNKNOWN_QUERIES = TRUE
             PROBE_AMP_PARAM_SWEEP = FALSE (not needed here, keeps log short)
             New read candidates, all read-only and unable to alter a patch:
               F0 13 0B 0F 01 36 F7        (bare)
               F0 13 0B 0F 01 36 02 F7     (To Amp 1 slot)
               F0 13 0B 0F 01 36 03 F7     (To Amp 2 slot)
             0x36 added to PROBE_DUMP_CMDS so any reply is dumped in full.
TEST METHOD
(USER'S,
BETTER THAN
PROBING):    Build two ADJACENT patches identical in every respect except To
             Amp 1 and To Amp 2 — one pair at midpoint, one pair at 3 o'clock
             — saved to the rack in neighbouring slots. Navigate back and
             forth. Whatever carries the To Amp values MUST differ between the
             two logs; everything else that differs should be slot-related and
             can be discounted as a control. This avoids guessing command
             forms entirely.
             Then, separately, toggle Mono/Stereo on one patch and re-run,
             watching CMD 0x0A and 0x50.
DISCIPLINE:  Unsaved edits revert on nav, so ALWAYS save before testing
             readback. Use distinctly different values for To Amp 1 vs To Amp
             2 so a reply cannot be ambiguous.
FILE SYNC:   All files handed over are one consistent set built from the
             project baseline plus every edit to date. Take the newest set,
             discard earlier ones, never apply partially. Build A (the stable
             rollback) was superseded without being built — no action needed.
-------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, build C)
STATUS:      To Amp read CONFIRMED; Mono/Stereo command identified
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Outer (rig) level read paths
CONFIRMED:   CMD 0x36 READ FORM WORKS. Slot byte is REQUIRED; the bare form
             returns nothing.
               Request:  F0 13 0B 0F 01 36 <slot> F7   (02=ToAmp1, 03=ToAmp2)
               Response: F0 13 0B 0F 12 36 <slot> <v0> 00 00 00 00 F7
             Verified against user-set values on four patches (session log
             2026-07-22-221119). Decode val = v0>=0x40 ? v0-0x40 : v0+64,
             matching the existing write path, is correct.
             ACCURACY NOTE: where the 0x36 read and the bulk decode disagree,
             the BULK IS WRONG. Every disagreement is the bulk one step low
             and only on positive values — its raw-to-value conversion floors
             (raw 603979776 = 81.7, truncated to 81, true value 82; the rack
             displayed +3.3 matching 82). Switching to 0x36 removes the bulk
             dependency AND fixes a pre-existing off-by-one.
ELIMINATED:  Mono/Stereo is NOT carried by CMD 0x03, 0x08, 0x0A, 0x34, 0x50 or
             0x36. Proven, not merely unproven: patch P1 was visited three
             times with genuinely different mono state each time (STEREO,
             MONO, STEREO per the bulk decode) and all three probe blocks were
             byte-identical. Session log 2026-07-22-221644.
             Side notes from the same run: 0x0A varies by patch (04 on
             A1/B1/P1, 00 on P4) but is unrelated to mono; 0x34 and 0x50 track
             the amp; CMD 0x03's SECOND byte goes to 01 after an edit and back
             to 00 after a save — looks like a dirty/unsaved flag, worth
             following up separately as it could drive a "modified" indicator.
CONFIRMED:   Mono/Stereo IS CMD 0x0D. From Mono_Toggle.pcapng: the Avid editor
             writes F0 13 0B 0F 00 0D <val> F7 and the rack echoes
             F0 13 0B 0F 02 0D <val> F7. This is not new to us — we already
             have a 0x0D set command and a 0x0D handler. Only the READ form
             was ever missing.
IMPORTANT
SIDE FINDING: After EVERY mono/stereo toggle the rack re-broadcasts the chain
             map (CMD 0x21) with DIFFERENT block handles:
               04 20 75 06 28 76  ->  04 21 7A 06 2A 7B  ->  04 20 7F 06 28 01
             So a mono change invalidates currentParamHi. Any CMD 0x11 based
             readback MUST re-read the chain map after a mono change, or it
             will query stale handles. This affects the bulk-replacement
             design directly.
BUILD C:     Adds REQU_PROBE_0D = F0 13 0B 0F 01 0D F7 and 0x0D to
             PROBE_DUMP_CMDS. Everything else unchanged from build B:

DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, build D)
STATUS:      BULK REPLACEMENT IMPLEMENTED — awaiting test
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Targeted post-nav state pull replacing the CMD 0x01 bulk dump
CONFIRMED:   Mono/Stereo read works. 5/5 against hardware state across
             A1/B1/B2/B1/A1, and the read does NOT toggle the state.
               F0 13 0B 0F 01 0D F7 -> F0 13 0B 0F 12 0D <00 stereo|01 mono> F7
             Last blocker cleared: every bulk-derived readout now has a
             non-bulk read path.
CHANGE:      transport.js requestPatchStateAfterNav() rewritten with two modes
             selected by REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV (now defaulting FALSE):
               TRUE  = legacy bulk path, unchanged behaviour, second flicker.
               FALSE = targeted path, four phases:
                 1 structure — patch name 0x05, chain map 0x21. The chain map
                   sets currentParamHi/currentChain and its handler fires
                   requestAllBypass, so the 11 bypass flags come free.
                 2 amp identity — CMD 0x11 paramLo 0x0F, ALONE, with a longer
                   settle. Must resolve before phase 3 because tone paramLo
                   values are per-amp. This ordering was the exact fault in
                   the diagnostic build.
                 3 amp block — 0x03 amp out, 0x04/0x05 gate, 0x0E bright,
                   0x15/0x16 cab/mic, 0x17 axis, 0x18 breakup, then this
                   amp's tone knobs from AMP_TONE_PARAMS, deduped.
                 4 rig level — rig vol 0x07, To Amp 0x36 slots 02/03,
                   mono 0x0D.
             protocol.js gains REQU_TOAMP1_READ, REQU_TOAMP2_READ,
             REQU_MONO_READ as confirmed constants.
STALE
GUARD:       navSeqId increments per nav; every phase checks both that its id
             is still current AND that currentSlot has not moved. Any sequence
             that finds itself superseded abandons its remaining queries and
             logs "Nav pull abandoned — slot changed". This stops replies for
             an abandoned patch painting stale values during fast navigation
             or auto-roll — the same hazard the bulk path guarded with its
             slot check.
TIMING:      NAV_QUERY_GAP = 120 ms, deliberately conservative, with a 350 ms
             wait after the chain map and 250 ms after amp identity. About 20
             queries total. Avid fires ~39 per recall, so there is headroom to
             reduce this once the path is confirmed. Tune only after correct.
UNAFFECTED:  TFX capture on hardware save. Saves arrive as an unprompted CMD
             0x00 broadcast on a separate path; neither mode touches it.
PROBES:      PROBE_UNKNOWN_QUERIES and PROBE_AMP_PARAM_SWEEP both FALSE for
             this build to keep the log readable. Probe code retained.
TEST:        Nav across several patches on DIFFERENT amps. Check: second
             flicker gone; amp name, tone stack, gate, amp out, cab, mic,
             axis, breakup, rig vol, To Amp 1/2 and mono all correct and
             matching what the bulk path showed. Then try fast navigation and
             the auto-roller and watch for stale or blank readouts.
             If anything is wrong, set REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV = true to return to
             known-good behaviour immediately.
KNOWN
RESIDUAL:    A mono/stereo change re-issues the chain map with DIFFERENT block
             handles, invalidating currentParamHi. Not yet handled — after a
             user mono toggle the chain map should be re-read before any
             further CMD 0x11 queries. Deferred; note it before the next
             parameter work.

DATE:        2026-07-22 (night, build F)
STATUS:      Race condition found and fixed — awaiting retest
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Post-nav pull ordering; fixed sleeps replaced by reply waits
TEST RESULT
BUILD E:     Six navigations, all six amps identified correctly, no abandoned
             pulls, no stale reads, no errors. Speed acceptable. Front panel
             flicker gone. BUT the log exposed a real defect.
DEFECT:      SILENT MISSING READOUT. On A2 (Bassguy 59, tweed_bass) the tone
             queries actually sent were paramLo 0D 0C 0B 0A 09 07 — that is
             LEAD800's knob set, the amp from the PREVIOUS patch — instead of
             tweed_bass's 0D 0B 0C 0A 07 08.
             Consequences:
               0x09 queried spuriously -> "CMD 0x11 unhandled paramLo=0x09"
               0x08 (Vol Norm) NEVER QUERIED -> readout silently not updated
             The four "unhandled paramLo" lines in the build E log were the
             visible symptom; the missing read was the real damage, and it was
             invisible. Values that DID arrive were routed correctly, because
             routing happens at reply time when currentAmpKey is already
             correct — which is exactly why this hid so well.
ROOT CAUSE:  NAV_AMP_SETTLE (130 ms fixed) expired before the Amp Select reply
             arrived, so the per-amp tone list was built from the stale
             currentAmpKey. Raising the sleep would only make it rarer, not
             correct — the failure is a race, not a duration.
FIX:         Both critical waits are now event-driven, not timed.
             state.js gains two counters:
               ampSelectRxSeq — incremented when a CMD 0x11 paramLo 0x0F reply
                 is processed. Incremented BEFORE any early return, so an
                 unmatched v0 still releases the wait instead of stalling it.
               chainMapRxSeq  — incremented once a CMD 0x21 chain map has been
                 fully applied, i.e. currentChain and currentParamHi valid.
             transport.js polls each counter every 10 ms until it advances:
               NAV_CHAIN_TIMEOUT 900 ms  (replaces NAV_CHAIN_SETTLE 220)
               NAV_AMP_TIMEOUT   700 ms  (replaces NAV_AMP_SETTLE   130)
             Timeouts only bound a lost reply and log plainly when hit. The
             stale-slot guard is still checked inside both poll loops, so a
             fast nav still aborts promptly rather than waiting out a timeout.
             The chain map wait matters as much as the amp one: a stale
             currentParamHi addresses the WRONG BLOCK entirely, not merely the
             wrong parameter.
UNCHANGED:   NAV_QUERY_GAP 15 ms, NAV_RECALL_SETTLE 100 ms.
RETEST:      Navigate between patches with DIFFERENT amps, especially amps
             with different knob counts, and confirm: no "unhandled paramLo"
             lines at all, and every tone control on each amp gets a query.
             Check Vol Norm specifically on Bassguy 59. Then fast nav and the
             auto-roller.

DATE:        2026-07-23 (morning)
STATUS:      Build G verified clean; Speed knob defect isolated, cause OPEN
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Post-nav pull verification, chain reorder behaviour, Speed knob
BUILD G
VERIFIED:    ZERO "unhandled paramLo" lines across 12 navigations. The
             selector exclusion worked. No abandoned pulls, no timeouts, no
             stale reads. Speed acceptable. Targeted path is sound.
CONFIRMED —
CHAIN
REORDER:     Drag-and-drop of the chain causes the hardware to BROADCAST the
             full chain map UNPROMPTED (F0 13 0B 0F 02 21 ...) after every
             reorder — five broadcasts observed 06:16:42 to 06:17:12, none
             preceded by a request from us. Block handles CHANGE each time:
             first block went 38 -> 40 -> 44 -> 4D -> 50.
             Our 0x21 handler already consumes these and bumps chainMapRxSeq,
             so drag/drop self-heals currentParamHi. NO ACTION NEEDED.
             Same mechanism as the mono/stereo toggle noted 2026-07-22 — that
             one is still unhandled only because it is not accompanied by an
             unprompted rebroadcast in the case observed.
DEFECT —
SPEED KNOB:  On DC models, paramLo 0x11 (Speed) neither reads nor writes.
               READS  — all six replies were 00 00 00 00 00.
               WRITES — during a drag we sent v0 = 7F, 7D, 7B ... and the
                 hardware echoed 00 00 00 00 10 EVERY TIME, identical
                 regardless of what was sent.
             The display then shows 64 (centre) because the handler decodes
             v0 alone; under the 5-byte encoding 00 00 00 00 10 is near
             MINIMUM, not centre. So the display is also wrong, but that is a
             symptom, not the cause.
NOT THE
CAUSE:       Stale handle is RULED OUT. In the same drag, on the same handle
             0x39, Treble (0x0A) and Depth (0x10) both wrote and echoed
             correctly. The block was addressed properly throughout.
             User's hypothesis that it only breaks after changing model in an
             already-loaded patch is therefore NOT supported by this capture —
             the fault is specific to paramLo 0x11, not to the handle or the
             model-change path.
TWO
CANDIDATES:  (a) Sync is locking Speed. DC models carry a Sync selector we
                 have never implemented or read. If engaged, a free-running
                 rate would be ignored and forced to a tempo division, which
                 would look exactly like this.
             (b) Speed uses a different encoding. It is a rate, not a 0-127
                 knob. Our write places the value in the first byte only; if
                 the rack expects a rate spread across the lower bytes, our
                 writes are nonsense and 00 00 00 00 00 does not mean centre.
             This capture cannot separate them. NOTHING GUESSED.
NEXT TEST
(NO CODE):   With the app running, turn the SPEED KNOB ON THE RACK'S OWN FRONT
             PANEL through several positions and read the broadcasts — they
             reveal the true encoding and range. Then toggle SYNC on the
             hardware and turn Speed again.
               Speed responds only with Sync off  -> candidate (a)
               Broadcast values unlike our single-byte writes -> candidate (b)
             Same technique that settled recall, To Amp and Mono/Stereo.
             Cannot damage anything.

DATE:        2026-07-23 (morning, addendum)
STATUS:      User hardware observation — raises candidate (a) to likely
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Sync / Speed coupling on DC models
OBSERVATION
(USER,
DIRECT FROM
HARDWARE):   Sync and Speed are COUPLED. The Sync selector has several
             choices — user recalls 3 or 4 — and choosing certain ones MOVES
             THE SPEED KNOB to a specific position on its own.
             This is a first-hand hardware observation, not yet corroborated
             from the protocol, so it lives here and not in the Technical
             Reference. Promote it once the wire behaviour confirms it.
IMPLICATION: This makes candidate (a) from the previous entry the likely
             explanation of the Speed defect. If Sync owns Speed whenever a
             division is selected, then the hardware would ignore any free
             rate we write and report a value it derives itself — which is
             exactly the observed pattern: writes of 7F / 7D / 7B all echoing
             back an identical 00 00 00 00 10.
             It also means Speed may not be independently writable AT ALL
             while Sync is engaged, so "fixing" the Speed write could be the
             wrong goal. The correct model may be: read Sync first, and treat
             Speed as read-only, or as driven, whenever Sync is on a division.
CONSEQUENCE
FOR DESIGN:  Sync therefore is NOT merely a missing display control. It is a
             control that DRIVES another parameter, so implementing Speed
             correctly probably requires implementing Sync first, not after.
             This reorders the backlog: on DC models, do Sync before Speed.
STILL
REQUIRED:    The front-panel test from the previous entry stands, with one
             addition — note WHICH Sync choice is selected at each step, and
             which Sync choices move the Speed knob and where to. That gives
             the Sync value list AND the Speed positions it forces, in one
             pass.

-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-23 (build H)
STATUS:      Speed/Sync stopgap + live timing panel
             [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference / Primer]
AREA:        Sync/Speed interlock; runtime tuning of the post-nav pull
USER
OBSERVATION
(HARDWARE):  The interlock is BIDIRECTIONAL.
               Sync OFF + Speed at 0 behaves normally.
               Setting Sync to certain divisions FORCES Speed to a fixed spot.
               Turning the real Speed knob ON THE RACK resets Sync to OFF.
             Our software does the second half not at all: it writes Speed
             without clearing Sync, so the hardware forces Speed back — the
             erratic knob behaviour.
WIRE
EVIDENCE     From session 2026-07-23-063859, broadcasts on the amp handle:
(MEASURED):    SYNC  0x12 — 127 distinct values, ALWAYS trailing 00 00 00 10
               SPEED 0x11 — 126 distinct values, mostly trailing 7F 7F 7F 1F,
                            and 00 00 00 10 in another region
               DEPTH 0x10 — works fine, always trailing 00 00 00 10
             sendParamWrite() sends trailing 00 00 00 00, matching NONE of
             them. Depth tolerates it; Speed does not, and the rack answers by
             forcing Speed to 0.
             NOTE: both Sync and Speed are CONTINUOUS 0-127 values on the wire,
             not a 3-4 item list. The dropdown presentation must quantise them
             somewhere. The meaning of the trailing bytes is NOT established —
             do not invent a write format from this.
STOPGAP:     transport.js sendParamWrite() now refuses any paramLo listed in
             READ_ONLY_PARAM_LOS, currently [0x11 Speed, 0x12 Sync]. They still
             DISPLAY whatever the hardware broadcasts; only writes are
             suppressed, and each suppression is logged. Set the array to []
             to re-enable. This removes the erratic behaviour without
             pretending to solve the interlock.
DECISION:    NOT reverting to the pre-bulk-replacement code. The interlock is
             independent of the read-path work — the write path is identical
             in both builds, so the old code would fight the hardware in
             exactly the same way. Reverting would forfeit the recall fix, the
             flicker fix, the To Amp and Mono/Stereo read paths and the race
             fix, and would not address this bug.
NEW FILE:    timing-panel.js — a development panel, bottom right, that edits
             the nav timings live. The timings are plain `var` globals, so no
             rebuild is needed to try a value.
               TUNE: NAV_QUERY_GAP (main lever), NAV_RECALL_SETTLE.
               DO NOT TUNE: NAV_CHAIN_TIMEOUT / NAV_AMP_TIMEOUT — these bound
               a LOST reply only. The pull already proceeds the moment the real
               reply lands, so lowering them cannot speed anything up; it can
               only cause a false give-up. Shown greyed. Raise only if "did not
               arrive" appears in the log.
             The panel also reports the last pull's query count and elapsed ms,
             so tuning is measured rather than guessed. SHOW_TIMING_PANEL =
             false hides it entirely.
             index.html gains one script tag, loaded last.
TUNING
METHOD:      Drop NAV_QUERY_GAP a few ms at a time; after each change navigate
             several patches across DIFFERENT amps and run auto advance. Too
             small shows up as "unhandled paramLo" lines or a readout lagging a
             patch behind. Then step back up once. Avid's measured mean is
             14.3 ms; ~10 ms is the sensible floor.
OPEN:        The Sync/Speed interlock itself. Needs the trailing-byte meaning,
             the Sync value-to-division mapping, which divisions force Speed
             and to what, and the rule that a genuine Speed change clears Sync.
             Separate work; nothing should be written to either control until
             it is understood.

DESIGN
CONSEQUENCE: Fetch a block's parameters WHEN ITS PANEL OPENS, not on every
             nav. The distPanelOpen / reverbPanelOpen flags already point this

             permanent cost for values nobody is looking at, and would make us
             slower than Avid rather than faster.

             Studio..Early Reflect 2; Echo Room = load state 0x40, matching the
             knob-sweep lowest). Knob-turn order per Charlie's capture notes.
CONFLICTS:   Tech Ref Sec 23 has the reverb param NAMES but no paramLo numbers.
             Additive — no conflict. Type value table is new.
NEXT:        Test the built EXE with the Avid editor CLOSED (DIST readback
             discipline): open REVERB panel on a Spring patch and an Eleven SR
             patch, confirm knobs + Type dropdown reflect readback; turn each
             HW knob and confirm the panel tracks; switch models both ways.
             CONFIRM the 5-byte value payload (v0 00 00 00 00, copied from the
             DIST writer) is accepted by the reverb block — hardware knob
             broadcasts carried non-zero trailing bytes; DIST proved zeros work
             on write, but verify here. If all pass, promote paramLo numbers +
             Type table into Tech Ref Sec 23 and mark [FOLDED IN].-------------------------------------------------------------------

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 1, 20, 24, C12; Primer ENVIRONMENT /
             HARDWARE SAFETY / GRAPHICS]

FINDING:     Test environments, all on Avid USB driver v1.1.12:
               Win10 (VM + physical, several driver versions)
                 Eleven Edit works. Avid Editor runs.

             Plug-In Settings — that folder holds the graphics. This removes
             the Win11 blocker from the planned graphics enhancement.
