             --release 8 target makes the compiler version largely immaterial.
LESSON:      Neither half of C12 was a real contradiction. "Windows 11 works"
             was about Eleven Edit; "Win11 BSODs" was about the Avid Editor.
             Both true, about different subjects, and they only looked like a
             conflict once compressed into one-liners in separate sections.
             Before resolving a conflict by discarding a side, check the two
             statements are about the same thing.

===================================================================
PART 4 — TREMOLO FEATURE SET, 2026-07-23 (afternoon)
===================================================================
Moved from the session log 2026-07-24. The working detail behind the session
that solved the tremolo feature set and retired four wrong diagnoses in one
afternoon.

Every CONCLUSION is live in the Technical Reference — the tremolo controls and
14-zone Sync map (Sec 11), the trailing-byte resolution and endpoint sentinels
(Sec 7), the zero-anchored dB scales (Sec 18), FORMAT B deleted (Sec 3, C11),
and the retractions in Sections 20 and 21.

Read it for the reasoning: how the Sync zones were derived from two tempo
sweeps, why the dotted-note dip confirmed the ordering independently, and how
four separate wrong diagnoses were each traced back and retired.

-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-23 (afternoon)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN 2026-07-23 -> Technical Reference Sec 3, 7, 11, 18, 20,
             21, 22 (C11 resolved), 24; Primer Status / CURRENT APP STATE /
             WORKING / NOT YET IMPLEMENTED / CODE FILE MAP / KEY STATE
             VARIABLES / HOW TO WORK WITH CHARLIE / NEXT SESSION]
             Verified on hardware. Retained here only until the next sweep.
AREA:        Amp tremolo feature set — Sync 0x12, On/Off 0x13, panel layout
FOLLOW-UP
BUILD (same
session):    Hardware test passed: layout correct, Sync dropdown tracks all 14
             zones, Tremolo reads and writes both directions. Four issues
             found and fixed in a second build.
             1. SPEED KNOB DIVERGED FROM THE RACK. 623 suppressed Speed writes
                in session-2026-07-23-141511.log. The knob painted itself as
                it was dragged while sendParamWrite refused to transmit, so
                the display walked away from the hardware. This is the
                "inconsistency between Speed and the Sync dropdown" reported —
                Sync was honest about being read-only and Speed was not.
                FIX: new .knob-readonly style. Any tone knob whose paramLo is
                in READ_ONLY_PARAM_LOS renders at 0.65 opacity, is not
                draggable, appends "(rack only)" to its label and carries an
                explanatory tooltip. It still displays every broadcast.
                Emptying READ_ONLY_PARAM_LOS makes the knob live again with no
                other change — the styling is driven off that same array.
             2. STUCK KNOB DRAG — CONFIRMED PROGRAM DEFECT, NOT HARDWARE.
                All four drag handlers ended a drag only on a mouseup received
                by the window. Releasing the button outside the window, or the
                window losing focus mid-drag, never delivered it, so the knob
                kept following the mouse until the next click.
                FIX: mousemove now also ends the drag when e.buttons is 0, and
                a window blur listener resets it. Applied to all four handlers
                (initKnob, tone knobs, DIST, REVERB).
             3. Tremolo button label no longer appends ON/OFF; colour carries
                the state.
             4. SL100 Drive's 0x0E toggle is labelled MOD on the rack. Tech
                Ref Sec 11 recorded this but AMP_TONE_PARAMS said 'Bright' for
                all 19 amps. Table corrected, and the button now takes its
                text from the table instead of a hardcoded string. Also
                updateBrightVisibility now matches on paramLo 0x0E rather than
                the label 'Bright' — with the label corrected, the old
                label-based match would have hidden the button on that amp.
BUILD 3 —
INTERLOCK
SOLVED:      Avid_Shark_Sync_Test.pcapng (Avid alone, Wireshark) settles the
             Sync/Speed interlock that has been open since 7/23 morning.
             1. THE OLD DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG. Speed writes did not fail because
                sendParamWrite's trailing bytes were malformed. They failed
                because the rack IGNORES any Speed write while Sync is on a
                division. Avid sent ~40 Speed writes between t=95s and t=116s
                with v0 ranging 0x16-0x2D; the hardware echoed the SAME
                unchanged value (1F 7F 7F 7F 1F) back every single time. Avid
                never clears Sync first, so its own Speed knob is equally
                inert in that state — user confirmed visually before the
                capture was analysed.
             2. SYNC WRITE FORMAT CONFIRMED, and it is what we already emit.
                Avid picking OFF from its dropdown sends
                  00 11 <handle> 12 40 00 00 00 00
                and the rack echoes
                  02 11 <handle> 12 40 00 00 00 10
                That outgoing message is byte identical to
                sendParamWrite(0x12, 0).
             3. THE 0x10 IS A BROADCAST ARTEFACT. The final byte of every
                hardware broadcast is 0x10 higher than the corresponding
                write. Seen on both Sync and Speed. It is added by the rack on
                the way out and must NOT be included in writes. This retires
                the "trailing bytes are not understood" caveat in Sec 7 for
                these two controls.
             4. Speed's v1-v4 in broadcasts are genuine low-order bits of the
                same quantity, not a fixed signature: v0=0x00 appeared with
                both 00 00 00 10 and 7F 7F 7F 1F within one sweep, either side
                of centre. Display continues to use the single-byte decode,
                which is correct to the tenth (Finding 3).
DESIGN
ADOPTED
(USER):      Rather than greying Speed while Sync is engaged, MIRROR THE
             HARDWARE: turning Speed clears Sync to OFF first, exactly as the
             rack does when its own Speed knob is turned. The control stays
             live at all times and behaves as the front panel does. This is
             better than Avid, whose Speed knob simply does nothing in that
             state.
             IMPLEMENTATION: currentSyncZone tracks the live zone from every
             readback; syncClearedThisDrag latches per drag so the Sync-OFF
             write is sent once at the start of a Speed drag, not on every
             mousemove. The latch also clears when the hardware confirms Sync
             is OFF, so a rack-side clear cannot cause a duplicate.
             READ_ONLY_PARAM_LOS is now EMPTY — both 0x11 and 0x12 are
             writable. The array and the .knob-readonly styling remain in
             place: putting a paramLo back makes that knob read-only again
             with no other change.
             Sync dropdown is now ENABLED and writes the CENTRE of the chosen
             zone (syncV127FromIndex), so a rounding error either way still
             lands in the intended division.
BUILD 4 —
PRECISION
FIXES:       1. SPEED WOULD NOT REACH 10.0, settling back to 9.9. Cause: a CMD
                0x11 value is FIVE 7-bit bytes and v1..v4 are the LOW-ORDER
                bits, not padding. sendParamWrite always sent v0 + 00 00 00 00,
                i.e. the very BOTTOM of the requested step. Most controls are
                quantised to the byte and cannot tell; Speed carries real
                sub-step precision (v0=0x00 was seen with both 00 00 00 10 and
                7F 7F 7F 1F within one sweep) and reported exactly what it was
                asked for. Depth and Presence were checked and are clean,
                confirming this is a precision issue and not Speed-specific.
                FIX: endpoint sentinels, already documented in Sec 7 —
                  v127 >= 127  ->  3F 7F 7F 7F 0F
                  v127 <= 0    ->  40 00 00 00 00
                  otherwise    ->  v0 00 00 00 00  (unchanged)
                Writes carry 0F in the final byte where broadcasts carry 1F.
                Applied to ALL knobs by agreement: for controls already
                reaching 10.0 this asks for a value strictly closer to true
                maximum, so nothing visible changes.
             2. TO AMP 1/2 COULD NEVER DISPLAY 0.0 dB. The old scale,
                db = -12 + (v127/127)*24, puts zero at v127 = 63.5, which
                cannot exist — it showed +0.1 at 64 and -0.1 at 63.
                EVIDENCE, from existing logs, no new capture needed: with the
                rack reading 0.0 dB our log recorded
                  CMD 0x36 ToAmp1 volume: v0=0x00 (+0.1 dB)
                and v0=0x00 decodes to 64. So 64 is the hardware's 0 dB point.
                FIX: anchor at 64, with 64 steps below centre and 63 above, so
                all three anchors land exactly: 0 -> -12.0, 64 -> 0.0,
                127 -> +12.0. Also drops the '+' from a bare 0.0.
                LIKELY CLOSES the "Amp Out 0.2-0.4 dB midrange nonlinearity"
                in Sec 21: an offset scale is worst mid-range and vanishes at
                the ends, which is exactly the reported shape. It was never
                nonlinearity.
             3. AMP OUT — SAME DEFECT, NOW FIXED AND CONFIRMED.
                From session-2026-07-23-155947.log:
                  rack set to 0.0 dB  -> v127 = 98,  we showed +0.2
                  rack reading 4.8 dB -> v127 = 106, we showed +5.1
                106 is 8 steps above 98 and 4.8 / 8 = 0.6 exactly, so the step
                is 0.6 dB and the 0 dB anchor is v127 = 98.
                NEW: db = (v127 - 98) * 0.6.
                The old -60..+18 span implies 0.6142 per step, which would show
                4.9 where the rack shows 4.8 — so the documented range was
                rounded. Implied endpoints are now -58.8 dB at v127=0 and
                +17.4 dB at v127=127. WORTH A SPOT CHECK at both extremes; if
                either disagrees, the step is not exactly 0.6 and both anchors
                need re-deriving rather than nudging (see Primer, two-failed-
                fixes rule).
                Rig Vol (-24..0) and Gate Threshold (-90..-20) have no zero
                crossing and are unaffected.
                CONFIRMS the Sec 21 entry "Amp Out display has ~0.2-0.4 dB
                nonlinearity at midrange — likely log-pot response" was a
                MISDIAGNOSIS. There is no log-pot behaviour: the scale was
                linear all along, just anchored wrongly and with a step 2%
                too large. That entry should be deleted, not amended.
             4. FOCUS RING — TRIED AND REVERTED AT USER REQUEST. The ring left
                on dropdowns and buttons after a click is the browser's default
                focus indicator, not a deliberate "recently used" marker. It
                was briefly limited to keyboard focus (:focus-visible) with a
                blur() on the Sync dropdown. User asked for it back, so both
                the CSS and the blur calls were removed and the default
                behaviour restored. Recorded here only so the idea is not
                re-proposed as new: it was considered and declined, not missed.
VERIFIED ON
HARDWARE:    Speed now reaches and holds 10.0. To Amp 1 and 2 read 0.0 dB.
             Sentinel writes caused no regression on other knobs.
NEXT:        Hardware test, Avid closed, patches backed up first — this is the
             first time the app has written to either control.
              - Sync on a division, drag our Speed knob: Sync should drop to
                OFF and Speed should then track.
              - Pick a division from our dropdown: the rack should follow and
                Speed should jump to the tempo-derived value.
              - Turn the rack's Speed knob: Sync should drop to OFF in our GUI.
NOT A
DEFECT:      Sync did read OFF correctly (14:15:21 and 14:15:35). The sweep
             then climbed 0 -> 124 without returning, so it had no reason to
             show OFF again. Zone mapping tracked all fourteen zones exactly,
             including the boundary: val 13 -> zone 1, val 15 -> zone 2.
FINDING 1 —
TREMOLO
ON/OFF:      paramLo 0x13 on the amp handle. This control exists ONLY on the
             hardware front panel: Avid's editor has no such toggle and the
             per-amp XML does not describe it. Encoding is identical to
             Bright — 127 = ON, 0 = OFF, seen on the wire as
               ON   3F 7F 7F 7F 1F
               OFF  40 00 00 00 10
             The tremolo set is therefore a contiguous paramLo block:
               0x10 Depth   0x11 Speed   0x12 Sync   0x13 On/Off
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-07-23-131306.log. Eight 0x13 events 13:13:35 to
             13:14:35 = the four on/off presses reported; four 0x0E events
             13:15:29 to 13:15:40 = the two Bright presses. Distinct
             parameters, correct counts, correct order.
FINDING 2 —
SYNC MAP:    Sync (0x12) is a continuous 0-127 value quantised into 14 zones:
             OFF plus 13 note divisions. Zone width 10; first and last zones
             truncated to 4 so the total is exactly 128.
               0-3 OFF | 4-13 div 1 | ... | 114-123 div 12 | 124-127 div 13
             Division order: whole note, then dotted / plain / triplet for
             half, quarter, eighth and sixteenth.
EVIDENCE:    Two front-panel sweeps at different tempos, which agree:
             at 120 BPM the Speed broadcast changed at decoded 64, 74, 84, 94,
             104, 114, 124 (zones 7-13); at 32.1 BPM only at 114 and 124
             (zones 12-13). Slower divisions fall below the Speed floor and
             read 0 — hardware behaviour, not a decode fault.
             ORDER CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY: a dotted note is slower than the
             triplet before it, so displayed Speed must dip at zones 8 and 11.
             It does — 1.3, 0.8, 2.5, 5.0, 4.2, 7.5 at 120 BPM.
FINDING 3 —
SPEED
DECODE:      Speed's displayed value is the STANDARD single-byte offset decode
             of v0. Verified on nine messages against the rack's own display:
             0x50 -> 1.3, 0x4A -> 0.8, 0x60 -> 2.5, 0x1F -> 7.5, all to the
             tenth. The 35-bit multi-byte formula in Tech Ref Sec 7 returns
             6.28, 5.87, 7.52 and 2.56 for those same messages and is WRONG
             for this control. The trailing bytes carry precision below the
             displayed step, not a different encoding.
CONFLICTS:   Tech Ref Sec 7 — the 35-bit formula must be scoped or corrected.
             Sec 11 — Sync is no longer an unimplemented 'selector'.
             Sec 21 / Primer — "Sync dropdown needs a valid-value capture" is
             now satisfied.
FINDING 4 —
C11 SETTLED: FORMAT B does not exist on this connection. Across ~20 replies in
             one session, byte 6 is always the block handle (0x04-0x0D,
             0x12-0x1B), never a fixed 0x04 marker, and every reply is 14
             bytes — handle, paramLo and five value bytes, with no room for an
             extra marker. Recommend deleting FORMAT B from Sec 3 and closing
             C11. The handler accepts both shapes, so nothing breaks either
             way.
FINDING 5 —
RIG TEMPO:   CMD 0x50, four payload bytes, broadcast on tempo change.
             INVERSELY related to BPM — the value traced a V while the tempo
             traced a peak, twice, matching two sweeps. NOT decoded: the value
             range spans a ratio of about 98 where 10-500 BPM spans 50, so it
             is not a simple reciprocal. A tempo change also triggered 753
             rebroadcasts of paramLo 0x02 on handle 0x17 and 0x04 on handle
             0x1A — tempo-synced parameters in other chain blocks.
             The startup reply F0 13 0B 0F 12 50 01 3A 04 20 F7 is byte
             identical across sessions at different tempos, so it is NOT the
             tempo value.
             NEXT: a two-minute calibration capture — set tempo to exactly
             60.0, 120.0, 240.0 and 32.1 with a pause at each. Four known
             points pin the formula and settle whether the decimal rides in
             the same number. Deferred; tempo is a rig-level control, not part
             of the amp panel. The FINE button repurposes the scroll wheel to
             the decimal place (user, 7/23).
BUILD
CHANGES:     protocol.js  SYNC_DIVISIONS (14 entries, glyph + text),
                          syncIndexFromV127, syncV127FromIndex, ampHasTremolo.
             index.html   .scard-col is now a COLUMN of two fixed .amp-row
                          blocks instead of one wrapping row. That single
                          wrapping row is what made the tone knobs ride up
                          beside Volumes and Bright wrap under Gate at wide
                          window sizes. Amp controls now always start below
                          the gate row. New vertical stack right of the tone
                          knobs: BRIGHT / TREMOLO / Sync dropdown, top down.
             ui.js        updateBrightVisibility now gates all three
                          independently; updateTremButton, updateTremReadout,
                          populateSyncDropdown, updateSyncReadout; tremOn.
                          Bright visibility now matches on paramLo 0x0E rather
                          than the label 'Bright' — SL100 Drive names that same
                          toggle MOD and so was never showing its button.
             sysex-handler.js  0x12 and 0x13 routed BEFORE the tone-knob loop.
             transport.js Nav pull adds 0x12 and 0x13, only on the 15 amps
                          that have tremolo (+2 queries on those amps only).
             app-init.js  TREMOLO click handler. Refuses to act until a
                          readback has arrived, so a first click cannot invert
                          a guessed state.
WRITES:      TREMOLO writes are ENABLED (plain two-state control, same as
             Bright). Sync dropdown is DISPLAY ONLY — disabled in the UI, and
             0x12 remains in READ_ONLY_PARAM_LOS. Speed writes stay blocked.
NEXT:        Hardware test, Avid closed. Then, if Sync writes are wanted, the
             candidate format is v0 with trailing 00 00 00 10 — that was the
             trailing signature on all 242 Sync broadcasts without exception,
             and syncV127FromIndex already returns zone centres to write.
             Also outstanding in this area: sendToAmpVolume()'s "global"
             comment is wrong (To Amp 1/2 are per-patch) — not yet corrected.

===================================================================
PART 5 — 7/26 UI PASS + POST-SAVE HANDLE BUG (working detail)
===================================================================
Moved from the session log 2026-07-26 after fold-in and hardware test. The
build-by-build detail of the six-feature UI pass and the post-save "dead knobs"
investigation. Conclusions are live in Tech Ref Sec 4 (handles reassign on
save; knob-write throttle; handle-mismatch self-heal), Sec 15 (post-save
chain-map re-read; Save-to-Rack vs Save-to-Disk naming), Sec 18 + CMD 0x50
(Rig Tempo / Rig Volume / To Amp 1-2 are per-patch), Sec 21 (accepted rapid-nav
bounce; scroll-wheel status), and the Primer's CURRENT APP STATE.

Read this for the reasoning: how three symptoms (dead SW knobs after save,
knobs reviving on a physical turn, "instId does not match" broadcasts) traced
to one root cause — the firmware reassigning block handles on save — and how
the throttle, post-save re-read, and self-heal each address a different leg of
it.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2026-07-26 — ITEM A: KNOB COLOUR STATE (red-on-change)
STATUS: PROVISIONAL — built, not yet tested on hardware by Charlie.
NEXT: build EXE, confirm colours and baseline behaviour, then fold in.

WHAT: knobs mirror the rack's line-pointer glow. amber = amp/main-panel
base, green = fx (effect-panel) base, red = current value differs from the
patch's SAVED baseline. Deviation from any source (our drag, front panel,
Avid) shows red — confirmed with Charlie this is intended and matches the
hardware pointer.

CORRECTION TO PRIMER: the primer/plan said "TFX readback already stores the
original." It does NOT — knobs stored only their current value (dataset.value);
no baseline existed. Added one.

DESIGN:
  - Colours are named constants KNOB_COLORS {amber,green,red} at top of ui.js
    (item B will move these to settings.json for persistence + colourblind
    palette). amber #e0a020, green #30c050 (theme --green), red #e83828.
  - drawKnob no longer hardcodes amber; a new knobColor(canvas,value) reads the
    knob's wrap: data-base="fx" -> green base, else amber; if a baseline
    (dataset.orig) is stored and differs from current -> red. Value arc AND
    indicator dot both take the state colour; track stays dark.
  - MAIN-PANEL baseline: captureKnobBaselines() snapshots dataset.value ->
    dataset.orig for every .knob-wrap NOT marked fx. Called at the end of BOTH
    CMD 0x01 decode paths in sysex-handler.js (save path + nav/load path), so
    a nav or a save re-baselines.
  - EFFECT-PANEL baseline: those values arrive in a separate query ~150 ms
    after the patch dump, so they baseline themselves — updateDistKnob /
    updateReverbKnob set orig on the FIRST value after a (re)render, and the
    two refresh...AfterChainMap functions delete orig on nav so the re-query
    re-baselines instead of showing red against the previous patch.

FILES: ui.js (1649->1705, +56), sysex-handler.js (738->740, +2).

KNOWN MINOR (watch for in testing): changing amp MODEL inside a patch resets
tone knobs to 64 then repopulates; if the loaded baseline != those values the
knobs may flash/settle red. Red there is arguably correct (the patch is now
changed), but confirm it is not distracting.

OPEN (item C, flagged 7/26): scroll wheel already steps 1 hardware unit — the
finest the rack allows (integers 0-127). "0.2" seen on To Amp is one unit on
its dB scale (~0.19 dB); tone ~0.08, Amp Out ~0.6. No true 0.1 step is
possible. Real aim = more decimal places in the readout to test the rack's
~0.1 physical deadband. Handle under item C.

  ── 2026-07-26 FOLLOW-UP (test 1): main-panel knobs stayed amber ──
  CAUSE: normal navigation uses the four-phase TARGETED pull
  (requestPatchStateAfterNav in transport.js), not the CMD 0x01 bulk decode.
  captureKnobBaselines() was only wired into the 0x01 path, which now fires
  only on save/manual-capture — so on a normal nav the main knobs never got a
  baseline and could never read red. (Effect-panel knobs self-baseline, so
  they worked.) This is exactly the doc-vs-code drift the primer warns about:
  the plan assumed the bulk-decode load path.
  FIX: capture the baseline at the END of the nav pull, after NAV_BASELINE_SETTLE
  (new, 250 ms, tunable) so the async CMD 0x11 replies land first. The 0x01
  save/capture calls stay. transport.js 1133->1145 (+12).
  RETEST: main knobs should now be amber at rest and red when moved off the
  loaded value; nav to a new patch re-baselines. If a freshly navigated patch
  shows red knobs it should not, raise NAV_BASELINE_SETTLE.

  HW NOTE (Charlie, test 1): the rack's own knob does not change colour until
  the change reaches ~0.3 (on the 0-10 scale) — a hardware deadband. Our SW is
  exact (any step shows red). Worth confirming precisely under item C when we
  add finer readout precision.

  ── 2026-07-26 FOLLOW-UP (test 2): FX panels forgot red on reopen ──
  OBSERVED (Charlie): amp panel holds truth across panel switches (red stays
  where left); FX panels re-baselined to current values on reopen and went
  green again — losing truth. Decision (agreed): keep truth, fix the FX panels
  to persist, do NOT dumb the amp down to the hardware's per-screen reset.
  WHY the HW resets: its 3 physical amp knobs are shared across screens; on
  another screen the knob sits at the wrong position, so the rack suppresses
  colour until you turn PAST the truth point, then it goes red. It never forgot
  truth — it just can't reposition a physical knob. Our exact-value method is
  the truth; the rack's colour is a physical-limitation artefact.

  CAUSE: effect-panel knob DOM is rebuilt on every panel open, so orig (held on
  the wrap) was wiped and re-seeded from the re-queried current value.
  FIX: persistent per-effect baseline store fxBaseline[slotId][loHex] in ui.js.
  First value seen per patch = truth; survives open/close and visits to other
  panels. updateDistKnob / updateReverbKnob now read orig from the store, not
  the DOM. rebaselineOpenFxPanel() re-anchors an open panel after a save.
  Reset points:
    - full patch nav  -> clearFxBaselines() added to clearStaleReadoutsOnNav()
      (capture-scan.js) — the one choke point for SW, HW and first-connect nav
    - effect model change -> clearFxBaselineForSlot() in the two refresh funcs
      (kept OUT of the reorder case, which must not reset truth)
    - front-panel save -> sysex-handler save path clears + rebaselineOpenFxPanel
    - SW Save to Rack -> same, in saveToRackAndDisk (different path from HW save)
  KNOWN GAP (accepted): if an effect is changed on the front panel BEFORE its
  panel is ever opened this patch, first open baselines to the already-changed
  value (query budget forbids reading every effect on every nav). Normal flow
  nav->open->tweak is correct.

  FILES this session (start -> now):
    ui.js            1649 -> 1745  (+96)
    sysex-handler.js  738 ->  742  (+4)
    transport.js     1133 -> 1145  (+12)
    capture-scan.js   270 ->  278  (+8)
  All grew (additions only). STATUS still PROVISIONAL — retest, then fold in.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2026-07-26 — ITEM: SAVE-BUTTON DIRTY LATCH
STATUS: PROVISIONAL — built, not yet tested by Charlie.

WHAT: SAVE (#btn-save-menu) now starts grey like Load TFX and latches GREEN on
the first patch edit, staying green until the next patch nav or a save. Pure
latch — no polling, never re-checks whether a value was returned to original
(matches how the HW and Avid editor flag a dirty patch, which we did not have).

HOW: new sendPatchWrite(hex) wrapper in transport.js calls markPatchDirty()
then sendHex(); the 13 per-patch write functions now send through it
(sendGateParamWrite, sendParamWrite, sendCabParamWrite, sendToAmpVolume,
sendRawParamWrite [amp select], sendMonoStereo, sendRigTempo, sendChainOrder,
sendBypassWrite [covers amp/cab/block bypass], sendDist/ReverbModelChange,
sendDist/ReverbParamWrite). All queries/readbacks keep calling sendHex directly,
so navigation never false-triggers. markPatchDirty/clearPatchDirty live in ui.js
(add/remove the 'green' class). Load TFX marks dirty (uncommitted memory).
Reset: clearPatchDirty() on patch nav (clearStaleReadoutsOnNav) and on save
(front-panel save path + SW saveToRackAndDisk).

CONFIRMED WITH CHARLIE (per-patch, so they DO light SAVE): amp knobs, Gate,
Amp Out, 1/2 Vol (To Amp volumes), Rig Vol, rig tempo, Cab, Mic, Axis, Bright,
Speaker Breakup, amp-model swap, Stereo/Mono, chain reorder, block bypass, all
DIST/REVERB edits. GLOBAL, so does NOT light SAVE: To Amp source (CMD 0x37).

DOC CORRECTION (fold into Tech Ref/Primer at wrap): rig tempo IS per-patch —
Charlie verified by saving, navigating away and back, values stuck and differed
per patch, and the HW/Avid flag dirty on a tempo change. transport.js still
carries an old comment near the nav pull calling tempo "NOT per patch / strictly
a refresh" — that comment is WRONG and should be corrected. (To Amp volume was
already corrected 7/23 to per-patch; tempo is the remaining stale note.)

FILES this session (start -> now):
  ui.js            1649 -> ~1772

  ── 2026-07-26 FOLLOW-UP: Save-to-Disk should stay dirty ──
  Charlie question: if you Save to DISK only (no rack commit), keep dirty?
  ANSWER: KEEP dirty — disk save writes a TFX file but does not commit to the
  HW slot, so the slot still differs from the buffer. Only a rack commit cleans.
  BUG this surfaced: Save-to-Disk runs captureCurrentPatchNow() -> REQU_SEND_PATCH
  -> the CMD 0x01 generic readback branch, where I had put captureKnobBaselines().
  That branch fires only on manual Capture / disk save (normal nav uses the
  targeted pull), so it was wrongly re-baselining knobs to the current (dirty)
  buffer — knobs went amber while the dirty flag correctly stayed green.
  FIX: removed captureKnobBaselines() from the generic readback branch. Knob
  baselines are now set ONLY on a true patch load (end of nav pull) and on a
  rack commit (front-panel save branch already did; saveToRackAndDisk now calls
  captureKnobBaselines() explicitly). Net behaviour:
    - Save to Rack  -> knobs amber, SAVE grey  (clean)
    - Save to Disk  -> knobs stay red, SAVE stays green  (still unsaved to rack)
    - Capture Now   -> knobs unchanged, flag unchanged
  CAVEAT (logged): if REQUEST_BULK_ON_NAV is re-enabled (debug fallback), nav
  would use the generic branch and no longer auto-baseline knob colours. The
  default targeted path is unaffected.
  FILES: sysex-handler.js, capture-scan.js.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2026-07-26 — DEFECT: knob-drag MIDI flood ("lost control of amp knobs")
STATUS: PROVISIONAL — fix built, not yet tested by Charlie.
PRE-EXISTING: reproduced on the pre-7/26 build too, and nothing today added
MIDI traffic — the dirty-flag hook is a button-colour change only. NOT a
regression from today's work. Charlie confirmed by reverting and reproducing.

SYMPTOM: after a few saves + fast knob drags, the amp knobs went dead in BOTH
directions (SW->HW and HW->SW). Bridge/MIDI stayed connected — not a real
disconnect.
DIAGNOSIS (from session-2026-07-26-123511.log): a knob drag sent one CMD 0x11
write PER mouse-move — up to 48 writes/second. That flood swamps the rack; it
reassigns the amp block's runtime handle mid-drag and starts broadcasting from
the new handle. The app still holds the old currentParamHi, so every incoming
update is dropped ("instId 0x1d does not match currentParamHi=0x0f, ignored" —
138 such frames at the end of that log) and the UI freezes = looks disconnected.
Note: handles are per-patch runtime values from the chain map and legitimately
differ each patch; the bug is the app/rack disagreeing after a flood, not the
handle changing per se.

FIX 1 — throttle (ui.js): new queueKnobSend(key, fn, val) trailing throttle,
mirroring the existing tempo throttle. Coalesces per control (key = tone:/knob:/
dist:/reverb: + id) at KNOB_SEND_INTERVAL = 60 ms (~16 writes/s, tunable). The
on-screen knob still moves instantly (drawn locally); only the hardware write is
paced, and the final value always lands. Routed: tone drag + reset, initKnob
drag/wheel/dblclick (To Amp vols), DIST drag + reset, REVERB drag + reset.
One-shots left direct: reverb Type dropdown pick, once-per-drag Sync clear.

FIX 2 — self-heal (sysex-handler.js): if HANDLE_RESYNC_THRESHOLD (5) broadcasts
in a row arrive for an unknown handle, re-read the chain map once (debounced by
HANDLE_RESYNC_COOLDOWN = 2000 ms) to re-establish currentParamHi, instead of
ignoring forever. Safety net in case any flood ever slips past the throttle.

FILES: ui.js, sysex-handler.js.
RETEST: drag amp tone knobs hard/fast, back and forth, right after a save or two.
Knobs should keep tracking both directions; the log should NOT fill with
"does not match currentParamHi". If it ever does, the self-heal line
"Self-heal: ... re-reading chain map to resync" should appear and control return.
TUNING: if drags feel laggy to the ear, lower KNOB_SEND_INTERVAL (ui.js); if a
flood still occurs, raise it.

  ── 2026-07-26 FOLLOW-UP: SW knobs dead AFTER A SAVE (handle reassignment) ──
  From logs 132134 (throttle confirmed: writes down to ~15/s from 48/s, fast
  moves fine) and 132345 (SW knobs went dead; self-heal fired 2x).
  ROOT CAUSE (Charlie's hunch, confirmed): a SAVE makes the rack re-instantiate
  the patch and REASSIGN block handles. In 132345 the amp handle walked
  7D -> 0C -> 1A across saves; the app kept writing to the stale handle (dead SW
  knobs) while the rack broadcast from the new one (1A appeared only in
  broadcasts, never in our writes). A physical knob turn broadcast from the new
  handle and tripped the self-heal, which is why "HW brings them back". The
  rename is incidental — it is the commit/re-instantiation that moves the handle.
  (Not every save moves it: 132134 stayed on 0x61 the whole time, 0 mismatches.)
  FIX: on "Save confirmed" (isSave branch), re-read the chain map immediately
  (sendHex REQU_CHAIN_MAP) so currentParamHi and the effect handles refresh at
  once — no longer waiting for a HW broadcast + self-heal. Harmless when the
  handle did not change (returns the same map). The self-heal stays as backup.
  FILE: sysex-handler.js.
  RETEST: edit, Save to Rack (incl. rename), then immediately drag a SW amp knob
  — it should track the rack with no dead period and no "does not match" burst.

  ── 2026-07-26 KNOWN LIMITATION (accepted, not fixing): rapid SW nav bounce ──
  Machine-gunning the SW patch arrow (log 135642: ~6 recalls/sec, far faster than
  any normal use) makes the rack fall behind and return CMD 0x02 slot
  confirmations out of order, even jumping backward (reported H4=31 then F1=20).
  Feedback loop: the app advances position per click but also trusts a LATE/stale
  confirmation and resets to it, so the next clicks re-navigate from the old
  slot -> the rack is sent backward -> "recycling e-f-g" cycling. The nav-pull
  abandon guard worked (225 pulls abandoned, read side never piled up) and it
  SELF-RECOVERED: landed cleanly on G1 "Friedman HBE" with a normal nav pull.
  DECISION (Charlie): note but do NOT fix. Normal navigation has been solid over
  weeks of testing; no real user nav's this way; reworking position tracking
  isn't worth the risk. Accepted limitation, abuse-case only.
  IF ever revisited: (1) pace/coalesce recall sends on a trailing timer toward
  the latest target; (2) track nav INTENT and ignore CMD 0x02 confirmations for
  slots already navigated past, settling + pulling once when the confirmation
  matches intent (mirrors how the HW scroll wheel coalesces internally).
  NOTE: pre-existing behaviour, not related to today's changes.

AREA:        Master Volume knob (CMD 0x36 outSel 0x00).
FINDING:     CONFIRMED WORKING on real hardware — Charlie: "I like that you

             linear dial. Fixed with a new valToMasterVol(v127) = (v127/127)

             leftover of the borrowed dB function, not a real state).

DATE:        2026-07-29 (new feature — Master Volume + Mute)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference "MASTER (MAIN) OUTPUT VOLUME
             (CMD 0x36, outSel 0x00)" and "MASTER MUTE — MAIN / PHONES
             (CMD 0x3B)"; Primer testing-workflow bullet]
AREA:        New feature — Main output volume knob + Main/Phones mute
             buttons, chain-strip right-hand group (right of TEMPO).
FINDING:     Decoded Master_Volume_Mute_Phones_Capture.pcapng (USBPcap,
             tshark). Master volume is CMD 0x36 outSel=0x00, same wire
             family as To Amp 1/2 (confirmed from a live Main-knob drag,
             10->0->8, streamed as F0 13 0B 0F 02 36 00 [v0] 00 00 00 00 F7).
             Mute is CMD 0x3B, format F0 13 0B 0F [dir] 3B [channel] [state]
             F7 — channel 0x00=Main/0x01=Phones, state 0x00/0x01 — captured
             as clean SW-send (dir 0x00) + HW-echo (dir 0x02) pairs matching
             Charlie's described sequence exactly (Main mute/unmute x2,
             Phones mute/unmute x2). This RETRACTS the old CMD 0x3B entry
             ("Global Mute... Front-panel only. [DEAD-send]") — that note
             was from a front-panel-only observation and never saw a
             software send succeed.
EVIDENCE:    Master_Volume_Mute_Phones_Capture.pcapng, USB CIN framing
             stripped in groups of 4 (per existing "no 0x04 framing" rule)
             before reading — first pass without stripping misread the
             Main-volume stream as a slot-enumeration sweep; correcting the
             framing-strip fixed that.
CONFLICTS:   Retracts CMD 0x3B's old summary line (now updated in place)
             and the [DEAD-send] tag.
NEXT:        No read/query form was ever captured for CMD 0x3B — GUI
             defaults to "unmuted" until the first send/broadcast, same
             known limitation as the Input Selector. Master volume's WRITE
             direction and its per-patch-vs-global status are both
             unconfirmed (write mirrors To Amp's pattern by inference, not
             direct capture) — first hardware build/test will settle both.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-27 (review)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 14 (WorB annotation +
             operating-mode hypothesis), Sec 24]
AREA:        Input Selector is global — WorB body field is written but ignored;
             broader operating-mode model recorded
FINDING:     Section 14 lists WorB (0x98, Input Selector) in the TFX global
             section, implying per-patch storage; today's fix treats input as
             global. Both are true in a specific sense. Charlie's hardware test:
               - A patch saved under Mic vs Digital HASH-DIFFERS -> the value is
                 written into the patch body.
               - On recall the hardware IGNORES it -> save as Mic, nav away,
                 input stays on the current global; return to the Mic-saved
                 patch and it shows the global (e.g. Digital), not Mic.
             So WorB is present-but-not-authoritative in standalone use — the
             RMno/C13 trap again.
HYPOTHESIS (reasoned, not confirmed — Charlie): the TFX body carries a SUPERSET
             of state for two operating modes. Standalone (this app) = analog
             gear with global input/routing; Pro Tools-integrated (the rack's
             original bundled mode) = DAW drives DSP/IO per session, where input
             and similar fields plausibly ARE recalled. A body field can thus be
             authoritative in integrated mode and inert in standalone mode.
             Reframes every "per-patch or global?" question as "authoritative in
             WHICH mode?" and predicts more such fields. Arbiter = the hardware
             recall test, not presence in the body.
ACTION:      WorB line annotated (do not read back, do not dirty, do not wire
             per-patch). Operating-mode note added by the global-section
             heading. Both labeled fact vs hypothesis. Recorded because it took
             months of observation to reach and would otherwise be re-litigated.
NEXT:        If ever confirmable: Avid docs, or a capture of the rack in Pro
             Tools-integrated mode, would settle whether input/others recall in
             that mode. Not blocking anything.

WHEN A FOLDED ENTRY LEAVES THIS FILE: an entry marked [FOLDED IN] is archived

DATE:        2026-07-24
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 5, Sec 6 (correction),
             Sec 18 (new RIG TEMPO block), Sec 21 (closed), Sec 24;
             Primer Status / CURRENT APP STATE / WORKING / NOT YET
             IMPLEMENTED / CODE FILE MAP]. Shipped; awaiting hardware test.
AREA:        Rig tempo — CMD 0x50 decode; chain-strip right-hand group
FINDING:     CMD 0x50 carries MICROSECONDS PER BEAT as a 24-bit number in four
             SIX-bit digits, most significant first:
               us = (d1<<18)|(d2<<12)|(d3<<6)|d4,  BPM = 60000000/us
             The 7/23 reading of this as a mysterious non-reciprocal inverse
             with a ~98:1 span was an artifact of assuming 7-bit bytes, which
             is what every other multi-byte value in this protocol uses. Every
             payload byte in the capture is below 0x40.
EVIDENCE:    Tempo_Settings.pcapng, six values keyed directly on the Avid
             editor. Decode is exact on all six with no rounding slack, and
             the encoder reproduces all six captured messages byte for byte:
               500.0 00 1D 13 00   60.0 03 34 09 00   120.0 01 3A 04 20
               240.0 00 3D 02 10   32.1 07 08 15 27    10.0 16 38 36 00
CONFLICTS:   Three, all corrected IN PLACE rather than annotated:
             - Sec 21 "inversely related, not a simple reciprocal, ~98:1" —
               retracted; it is a simple reciprocal.
             - Sec 21 "startup reply 01 3A 04 20 is byte identical across
               sessions, so it is NOT the tempo value" — retracted; it decodes
               to exactly 500000 us = 120.0 BPM, the default. The probe
               evidently ran at 120 BPM every time.
             - Sec 6 "CMD 0x50 — Version/capabilities query, response
               04 03 14 29, likely firmware version" — wrong command, deleted.
NEXT:        Hardware test with the Avid editor CLOSED (this feature reads
             state from the rack, so it is not proven until it runs solo):
             - type 120.5, Enter — rack front panel should follow
             - click the tenths digit, hold the up arrow — should climb by 0.1
               a step, and the log should show far fewer sends than steps
             - change tempo on the RACK — our clock should track it
             - navigate patches — clock should stay put and re-read once
             UNVERIFIED: whether the rack accepts a written tempo outside
             10.0-500.0. Our field clamps to that range either way.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-24
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Technical Reference Sec 11 (two new blocks), Sec 21,
             Sec 24; Primer Status / CURRENT APP STATE / WORKING / CODE FILE
             MAP]. Shipped and hardware-tested same session.
AREA:        Amp model change — tone stack values / per-slot parameter storage
FINDING:     Changing amp model in an open patch blanked every tone knob to
             "--" while the Avid editor kept showing values. True even
             between two models with an identical layout (92 Treadplate
             Vintage -> Modern).
             CAUSE: amp params are stored PER CHAIN SLOT and shared across
             models; the model only selects which paramLos are exposed and
             what they are called. The rack never cleared anything. Our
             setCurrentAmp -> updateToneKnobs blanked the knobs to 64/"--"
             and no code path ever re-read the block. Avid was not
             remembering values; it was re-reading.
EVIDENCE:    Four user tests against the Avid editor, no capture needed:
             (a) same-layout Vintage -> Modern still blanked in our app,
                 not in Avid;
             (b) Vintage -> Tweed -> Vintage returned all eight original
                 values intact, so the rack retains hidden values;
             (c) editing Tweed's "Tone" to 2.2 then returning to Vintage
                 showed Treble = 2.2, not the original 7.4 — proving one
                 stored value under two model-specific names;
             (d) Sync survived 65 AC Boost -> DC family;
             (e) cab type changes already preserved mic/axis/breakup — the
                 tell that one path in the app re-read and the other did not.
FIX:         On the 0x0F echo, when the amp key actually changes, wait
             AMP_CHANGE_SETTLE ms and re-query the amp block's paramLo set
             against the EXISTING handle. No chain map re-read: 0x0F is a
             parameter inside the block, not a CMD 0x21 model change, so the
             handle does not move. No mapping table, no knob-count
             reconciliation — the hardware is authoritative.
             transport.js: ampBlockParamLos() (Phase 3's list builder,
             extracted so the nav path and this path cannot drift),
             ampParamQuery(), requestAmpBlockParamsAfterAmpChange() with a
             sequence guard that cancels on a further amp change or a patch
             change mid-refresh.
             sysex-handler.js: 0x0F handler calls it. Fires for our dropdown,
             the rack's front panel and the Avid editor alike.
BUILD 1
DEFECT:      "PROMISE: paramQuery is not defined" on load. paramQuery() is a
             PRIVATE helper declared inside requestPatchStateAfterNav and was
             called from module scope. Fixed with a standalone ampParamQuery()
             rather than hoisting the original, because the nav version also
             increments the Dev timings query counter — reusing it would have
             polluted the nav timing figures with queries the nav pull never
             made. LESSON: check the SCOPE of a helper before reusing it, and
             check whether it has side effects beyond returning its value.
             This one had both problems.
TESTED:      Build 2 passed on hardware. Settle time 150 ms, then lowered to
             100 ms, both good.
CONFLICTS:   none. Nothing in the Technical Reference contradicted any of it;
             this is new ground, not a correction.
NEXT:        (1) AMP_CHANGE_SETTLE is a fixed guess about other machines —
                 untested across hosts, VM vs bare metal, USB 2.0 vs 3.x.
                 Adaptive version deferred (Tech Ref Sec 21).
             (2) USB 3.x operation untested under this app (Tech Ref Sec 21).

AREA:        Chain drag — position-1 room problem
FINDING:     Charlie: dragging toward position 1 is hard because there's
             barely any room between the chain strip and the window's left
             edge before the drag runs out of space to cross into "before"
             territory — WAH (or whatever occupies position 1) sits right at
             x=0 with nothing to its left. Two fixes, both agreed:
FIX 1        (code only): position 1's ENTIRE width now counts as "land here"
             — not just its left half past the midpoint, which was the part
             requiring room that isn't there. No position becomes unreachable:
             landing right after this same block is still reachable via the
             SECOND block's left half, so this only removes a cramped,
             redundant path. ui.js wireChainDrag's mousemove handler: when
             the hovered target is chainDragStartOrder[0] (whatever started
             in position 1 for THIS drag), `after` is forced false instead of
             computed from cursor position.
FIX 2        (layout): a passive, READ-ONLY clone of the active INPUT
             selection (GUITAR/MIC/LINE/DIGITAL) now sits at the very start
             of the chain strip, before position 1, with its own single-line
             ("mono" styled — it's one signal entering the chain, not a
             stereo pair) connector leading in. This shifts the whole chain
             row right, creating the missing room, while giving that space a
             reason to exist instead of being bare margin (Charlie's framing:
             filling it with real state reads as deliberate, not broken).
             NOT a new control — mirrors setInputButtons(), which is the
             single source of truth already driving the real buttons above;
             the clone can't drift out of sync because it's set in the same
             function call, same place. Considered and explicitly REJECTED:
             a functional input dropdown here replacing the top-row buttons
             — input switching is its own documented weak spot (Primer:
             "reflect live input / stop forcing Guitar... future global-
             settings work") and conflating that unfinished work with a chain-
             drag usability fix was the wrong scope for this pass.
GOTCHA FOUND: applyChainOrder's arrow-recycling loop selects `.chain-arr` and
             excludes #mono-connector by id to avoid hijacking it as a
             between-block connector — the new #chain-input-connector is also
             .chain-arr and needed the same exclusion, or the loop would have
             stolen it as one of the real connectors and thrown the block<->
             arrow pairing off by one. Fixed before it ever reached a build.

             wireChainDrag's after-calculation, applyChainOrder's arrow
             selector).

FOLDER-DEPTH DECISION: the eventual loader should search the chosen folder
             RECURSIVELY for the plugin image structure rather than require
             one exact depth (Editor root vs. System Plug-Ins vs. something
             else) — this is what lets a copied-out, no-longer-installed
             subset of Avid's folder still work, which matters most for
             Windows 11 users who may never run a live Avid install at all
             (BSOD risk) and would only ever have a one-time copied folder.
MAPPING TABLE ARCHITECTURE: the MID->filename table is CODE DATA, not
             documentation — belongs in its own new JS file (pattern: like
             fx-transport.js/fx-panels.js), never pasted into a doc as a raw

             Primer GRAPHICS section same day).

DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED, verified live before shipping
AREA:        Chain row — STEREO badge orphaned from its wrapper on every reorder
FINDING:     Charlie's next test: height fix confirmed good (GUITAR now roughly
             matches REVERB's height), but the WHOLE ROW appeared shifted too
             far right, with a gap after GUITAR bigger than any other gap.
             Two wrong guesses in a row on this general area already — this
             time, rather than guess a third fix, actually rendered the real
             app: installed ffmpeg + used the pre-installed headless Chromium
             in this environment to load src/index.html directly and screenshot
             it via the DevTools protocol, including simulating a real chain
             reorder (setting currentChain and calling applyChainOrder
             directly, the same function the live app calls on every CMD 0x21).
             That reproduced it immediately and made the cause obvious: static
             page load looked fine, but ANY reorder broke it.
ROOT CAUSE:  applyChainOrder (ui.js) does
               const mono = document.getElementById('mono-indicator');
               ... strip.appendChild(mono);
             at the end of every call, to keep the STEREO badge pinned to the
             end of the row. That grabs the bare label — but the 7/28
             "bookend" fix wrapped #mono-indicator in a .chain-slot (with a
             hidden real .chain-open caret) so it would bottom-align like every
             other block. Re-appending the bare label ripped it straight back
             out of that wrapper. Since applyChainOrder runs on every single
             chain-map update — i.e. constantly, in the real app — the wrapper
             fix was being silently undone almost immediately after every nav,
             leaving an orphaned empty .chain-slot sitting in the flow (the
             extra gap Charlie saw) and the badge drifting back toward plain
             align-self:center behavior once orphaned. The #chain-input-wrap
             (GUITAR) clone was NOT affected — nothing in ui.js moves it
             directly, only .textContent via setInputButtons.
FIX:         mono is now resolved as the WRAPPER, not the label:
               const monoLbl = document.getElementById('mono-indicator');
               const mono = monoLbl ? monoLbl.closest('.chain-slot') : null;
             VERIFIED before shipping, not just fixed-and-hoped: re-ran the
             exact same live reorder test against the fixed code (reload +
             re-simulate a CMD-0x21-style reorder + screenshot) — GUITAR,
             REVERB and STEREO all landed on the same baseline with no
             orphaned gap.
LESSON:      This environment can actually render and screenshot the app
             (ffmpeg + headless Chromium + CDP for live JS/reorder simulation
             were all available/installable) — worth doing THIS, not more
             guessing, whenever a visual bug survives one attempted fix. The
             two earlier guesses on the bookend height were reasonable
             attempts at limited information; this one had the tool to check
             directly and should have been reached for sooner.
FILES:       ui.js only (applyChainOrder's mono resolution).
NEXT:        Charlie's real rebuild test — this was verified in a synthetic
             headless render, not the actual Electron app end to end.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED, verified live before shipping
AREA:        Chain row — AMP/CAB thumb sat 10px higher than every other
             block's thumb; GUITAR/STEREO bookends still felt "~5px too high"
FINDING:     Charlie flagged two things off the latest build, both precise:
             (1) AMP/CAB's dummy thumb looked smaller than the others', and
             (2) GUITAR/STEREO still read as sitting too high, worried a real
             fix would "snowball" into growing the whole panel. Measured both
             via the same live-CDP technique (getBoundingClientRect, not
             eyeballing) instead of guessing a third time:
               - AMP thumb and WAH thumb were IDENTICAL size (64x60) — not
                 smaller at all. But AMP's thumb top sat at y=62 vs WAH's
                 y=72: same box, 10px higher, read as "smaller" because of
                 the reduced headroom above it.
               - GUITAR/STEREO's label BOTTOMS already exactly matched
                 WAH's label bottom (166=166) — already correctly aligned.
                 The "too high" feeling was optical weight, not position:
                 11px font / 3px padding vs. the real .chain-name's 13px /
                 5px.
ROOT CAUSE:  #chainstrip used align-items:flex-end, which bottom-aligns each
             .chain-slot/.chain-slot-stack at ITS OWN natural content height.
             AMP/CAB's stack (2 stacked name-labels) is naturally 122px vs a
             normal slot's 112px (1 label + 1 caret) — 10px taller — so its
             thumb (the first child) started 10px higher to make the bottoms
             still line up. Same-size box, different top.
FIX:         #chainstrip switched align-items: flex-end -> stretch, so every
             slot is stretched to exactly this row's own content-box height
             (one number, tuned in #chainstrip alone — no duplicated magic
             height on the slot classes themselves). Inside each slot,
             margin-top:auto on the label element right after the thumb
             (.chain-name, .chain-name-stack:first-of-type, and — for the
             thumb-less GUITAR/STEREO bookends — #chain-input-wrap /
             #mono-indicator directly) pins the label group to the BOTTOM
             and lets the GAP above it (between thumb and label) absorb the
             slot's height instead. Every thumb now starts at the same Y
             regardless of how many labels follow it. #chainstrip's height
             bumped 130 -> 140px — the smallest bump that clears AMP/CAB's
             natural 122px content plus a few px of top breathing room; this
             does NOT touch or grow the effect-panel area below the chain
             row, so the "snowball" Charlie was worried about (thumb fix ->
             taller slots -> bigger panel -> more inconsistency elsewhere)
             does not happen — it's a single bounded number in one place.
             GUITAR/STEREO's font-size/padding bumped 11px/3px -> 13px/5px
             to match .chain-name's real weight (their position was already
             correct; this was purely visual weight parity).

DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED, verified live before shipping
AREA:        Chain drag — reorder trigger point depended on where you grabbed
             the block, not just how far you dragged
FINDING:     Charlie: "it seem to matter where I place the mouse on the source
             block with regards to how it triggers... movement of other chain
             items." Read wireChainDrag's mousemove handler (ui.js) directly
             rather than guess: the hit-test used the raw cursor position
             (document.elementFromPoint(ev.clientX, ev.clientY), and the
             before/after midpoint check ev.clientX > r.left + r.width/2)
             instead of the dragged block's own position.
ROOT CAUSE:  chainDragOffsetX (cursor position within the grabbed thumb,
             captured at mousedown) is already used correctly to keep the
             ghost visually anchored to the same grab point — but the hit-
             test math never applied that same offset, so it worked off the
             naked cursor. Grab the thumb near its left edge vs. its right
             edge (a ~50px spread on a 60px-wide thumb) and the SAME physical
             drag motion crosses a neighboring block's trigger boundary at
             different points, because the cursor's absolute position
             relative to the dragged block's own edges depends on where you
             grabbed it.
FIX:         Compute the dragged block's own effective center once per move —
               dragCenterX = ev.clientX - chainDragOffsetX + chainDragThumb.offsetWidth/2
               dragCenterY = ev.clientY - chainDragOffsetY + chainDragThumb.offsetHeight/2
             — and use THAT (not ev.clientX/clientY) for both the
             elementFromPoint hit-test and the before/after midpoint check.
             Same pattern the ghost's own positioning already used, just
             applied to the trigger logic too. No new state, no architecture
             change, one function.
VERIFIED:    Live CDP simulation before shipping (not guessed): seeded a
             chain, dispatched real mousemove events dragging WAH
             toward FX2 with two very different grab offsets (5px and 55px
             into the thumb) but back-solved so the DRAGGED BLOCK'S effective
             center landed at the identical point both times — raw cursor X
             was 588 in one case and 638 in the other (a 50px spread), yet
             both produced the exact same resulting order (WAH landing right
             before FX2). Confirms the trigger point is now a function of the
             block's own position, not the grab offset.
FILES:       ui.js only (wireChainDrag's mousemove handler).

DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      IMPLEMENTED, verified live via simulated drag — NOT YET tested
             by Charlie on real hardware (new session, real-hardware test
             comes later)
AREA:        Chain drag — dragged block now continuously follows the cursor
FINDING:     Charlie confirmed CHAIN_SLIDE_MS=1000 (his own edit, pulled in
             fine) reads well, but flagged a real remaining gap: "With Avid
             it is immediately as soon I click, hold move the block(s) are
             in motion... I get a hand cursor, the placeholder box dims,
             the hand moves practically to edge or the next block then they
             suddenly swap places." Diagnosed: this wasn't a tunable number,
             it was architectural. Nothing tracked the cursor at all during
             a drag — the dragged block sat still (dimmed) in its own slot
             until the cursor's effective position crossed fully into a
             neighbor's territory, at which point a reorder fired and ONLY
             THEN did the (now smooth, thanks to the prior entry) slide
             play. Removing the ghost had removed the one thing that WAS
             providing continuous tracking (the ghost always followed the
             mouse 1:1) without replacing it with anything on the real
             block.
FIX:         The dragged block's own container (chainDragCont) now follows
             the cursor continuously, every mousemove, via a directly-set
             transform (no transition — an instant 1:1 snap every frame, not
             an eased catch-up): clear the transform, measure the thumb's
             TRUE natural (untransformed) position, compute the delta needed
             to put the thumb's center exactly at dragCenterX/Y (the same
             grab-offset-corrected point already used for hit-testing), and
             apply it. This recomputation happens AFTER any reorder that
             fires in the same mousemove tick, so it's always working off
             the block's up-to-date slot position, never a stale one.
             Two supporting changes were required for this to work at all:
               - chainDragCont.style.pointerEvents = 'none' for the whole
                 drag — otherwise document.elementFromPoint would always hit
                 the dragged block itself (since it now visually sits right
                 where the cursor is), and could never find the real
                 neighbor underneath it to trigger a reorder. Same trick the
                 old floating ghost got for free by being a separate overlay
                 with pointer-events:none; now applied to the real element.
               - applyChainOrderAnimated gained an excludeEl parameter — the
                 OTHER blocks still get the FLIP slide-in from the previous
                 entry, but the dragged block itself is excluded from that
                 treatment, since its position is owned by the continuous-
                 follow code above and would otherwise fight over the same
                 element's transform on the same frame.
             chainDragCont.style.transition is forced to 'none' for the
             whole drag (only the OTHER blocks get an eased transition), so
             the follow is always an instant snap to the cursor.
             endChainDrag resets transform/transition/pointerEvents on the
             dragged block back to '' — no settle animation needed, since
             its natural DOM position (from the last live reorder) was
             already exactly where the cursor left it.
VERIFIED:    Live CDP drag simulation, stepping the cursor toward FX2 in 6
             increments and checking EVERY intermediate step, not just start/
             end: the dragged thumb's rendered center matched the cursor's X
             position within <1px at all 6 steps, including across the exact
             moments a live reorder fired (contLeft jumping, transform value
             adjusting to compensate so the VISUAL position stayed pinned to
             the cursor throughout) — this is the core claim of "continuous
             follow" and it held at every sampled point, not just before/
             after. Also confirmed: pointer-events/transition/transform all
             correctly applied when dragging starts and correctly cleared
             (empty string) after mouseup; zero ghost elements at any point;
             final reorder result correct (WAH landed before FX2 as
             expected); a mid-drag screenshot shows a clean, uncorrupted
             layout.
FILES:       ui.js only (wireChainDrag's mousemove handler restructured;
             applyChainOrderAnimated gained excludeEl; endChainDrag resets
             the three new inline styles).
NEXT:        Charlie's real rebuild/hardware test — this is the piece that
             should make the drag actually FEEL like Avid's, per his own
             description of what was missing. Same caveat as the previous
             entry: this is real interaction-model code, not a CSS number, so
             report specifics (which part, at what point in the drag) rather
             than a general "still not right" if anything's off.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      TWO REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED, verified live via simulated drag
             — NOT YET tested by Charlie on real hardware
AREA:        Chain drag — linked AMP-CAB+LOOP pair misbehaving under the new
             continuous-follow drag (previous entry)
FINDING:     Charlie, after confirming the plain continuous-follow drag was
             "pretty dang good": dragging the linked AMP-CAB+LOOP pair showed
             two distinct problems. (1) LOOP trailing "at least one block
             behind" AMP-CAB instead of staying close. (2) Dragging AMP
             toward the far end, LOOP would "shoot to slot 10 then back" —
             a real ricochet, repeated multiple times over a 4-position drag
             — though the pair always landed correctly by mouseup either way.
             Precise enough to diagnose directly from the description alone.
ROOT CAUSE 1 (the ricochet): computeLinkedAmpLoopReorder (ui.js) filters
             BOTH AMP and LOOP out of `rest` before searching for the hit-
             tested target's index. If elementFromPoint ever returned LOOP
             itself as the target — likely with continuous-follow, since
             LOOP (unlike the dragged AMP-CAB block) had no pointer-events:
             none and could genuinely be found under the cursor as AMP-CAB
             passed near/over it — `rest.findIndex` for SLOT_LOOP always
             returned -1 (LOOP isn't IN rest), and the old fallback
             (`idx = rest.length`) parked the whole pair at the very end.
             That IS the "shoot to slot 10" — a real logic bug, not a timing/
             animation artifact.
ROOT CAUSE 2 (the lag): LOOP had no special handling at all in the new
             continuous-follow code (previous entry) — it was just another
             "other block" getting applyChainOrderAnimated's FLIP slide-in,
             now running at Charlie's own CHAIN_SLIDE_MS=1000. Since a real
             drag re-triggers a reorder far more often than once per second,
             LOOP's slide could never fully catch up before the next one
             restarted it, so it visually lagged behind AMP-CAB instead of
             looking glued to it.
FIX 1:       computeLinkedAmpLoopReorder: idx < 0 (target not found in rest,
             meaning the hit-test landed on AMP or LOOP itself) now returns
             `src` unchanged — a no-op, matching how the plain single-block
             path already handles targeting yourself — instead of jumping to
             the end.
FIX 2:       New state var chainDragLinkedCont, set at drag-start alongside
             chainDragCont whenever linkedAmpLoopInfo confirms this is a
             linked drag. It gets the SAME pointer-events:none + transition:
             none treatment as the dragged block, is excluded from
             applyChainOrderAnimated's mover list (which now takes an array
             of exclusions, not just one), and — critically — receives the
             EXACT SAME translateX delta as chainDragCont every frame,
             rather than computing its own. Since LOOP's natural DOM position
             is always kept immediately adjacent to AMP-CAB's by
             computeLinkedAmpLoopReorder, applying an identical shift to both
             preserves their gap exactly, giving true lockstep movement.
VERIFIED:    Live CDP drag simulation across all four combinations (LOOP
             before/after AMP-CAB, dragging left/right), 8-10 steps each,
             measuring the gap between the two blocks' rendered rects at
             EVERY step: the gap was PERFECTLY CONSTANT (86px or 111px
             depending on config) across every single step in all four
             tests, with the reorder sequence progressing cleanly block-by-
             block and no ricochet observed at any point. Also caught and
             corrected a false alarm in my own testing: an early screenshot
             showed AMP-CAB visually overlapping FX1, which looked like a
             bug — turned out to be a pure test artifact (60 mousemoves fired
             with zero real elapsed time between them, so FX1's 1000ms
             slide-out animation never got a chance to render a frame).
             Redone with real ~50ms delays between steps confirmed clean,
             non-overlapping layout once actual time is allowed to pass —
             worth remembering for any future drag verification: a tight
             synchronous event-firing loop does not represent real drag
             timing for anything that depends on CSS transitions.
FILES:       ui.js only (computeLinkedAmpLoopReorder's idx<0 fix;
             chainDragLinkedCont added; wireChainDrag's pending-transition,
             hit-test's applyChainOrderAnimated call, continuous-follow
             block, and endChainDrag all extended to handle it).
NEXT:        Charlie's real rebuild/hardware test. If the linked pair still
             feels off, the specific thing to check is whether the reported
             symptom is the SAME as before (ricochet/lag) or something new —
             the two root causes here were narrow and specific, not a
             general "linked dragging is unreliable" issue.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED, verified live via simulated drag —
             NOT YET tested by Charlie on real hardware
AREA:        Chain drag — linked AMP-CAB+LOOP pair needed asymmetric travel
             to trigger a reorder depending on which side LOOP was on
FINDING:     Charlie, very precisely: 2 of the 4 direction/config
             combinations were good (whichever direction has LOOP trailing
             behind AMP-CAB), 2 were off (LOOP leading) — "I need to drag to
             slot [4 positions right] before a tile move[s]... more precise:
             move from slot 2 to slot 3, I have to drag amp to slot 4 before
             slot 4 moves to 2." Also confirmed individual AMP moves,
             individual LOOP moves, and ordinary multi-block slides were all
             already fine — this was specific to the linked pair.
ROOT CAUSE:  A direct consequence of the previous entry's lockstep fix, not
             a separate bug in that fix. Once LOOP moves in perfect lockstep
             with AMP-CAB (identical transform every frame), LOOP physically
             occupies the space directly ahead of AMP-CAB WHENEVER it's on
             the leading side of the drag direction — meaning the cursor has
             to travel across LOOP's own entire width before reaching any
             REAL neighbor to hit-test against (LOOP itself is pointer-
             events:none and can't be a target). The hit-test was built off
             AMP-CAB's own center only (dragCenterX), so that "wasted" width
             cost real travel distance whenever LOOP led, but cost nothing
             when LOOP trailed (nothing in the way) — hence the exact
             asymmetry Charlie described, and the two-slot jump once it
             finally triggered (the pair moving as an atomic 2-wide unit
             across a target it had to travel THROUGH rather than approach).
FIX:         Hit-test off the PAIR's center instead of AMP-CAB's own center,
             for the linked case only. New chainDragLinkedGapX, measured
             ONCE at drag start (before either block has any transform) as
             the signed distance between LOOP's and AMP-CAB's thumb centers
             — constant for the whole drag since they move in lockstep.
             hitTestX = dragCenterX + chainDragLinkedGapX/2 replaces
             dragCenterX in both the elementFromPoint call and the before/
             after midpoint check; chainDragLinkedGapX is 0 for a normal
             (non-linked) drag, so hitTestX reduces to plain dragCenterX
             there — no change to any of the already-confirmed-good
             individual-block or ordinary-multi-block-slide behavior. Only
             the TRIGGER point changed; the actual visual continuous-follow
             positions of both blocks (previous entry) are untouched.
VERIFIED:    Live CDP simulation, two angles: (1) measured the EXACT cursor
             travel distance (1px increments) needed to trigger the first
             reorder in all 4 combinations — before this fix the asymmetry
             would have been on the order of a full block's width or more
             (matching Charlie's "drag to slot 4" description); after the
             fix, all 4 combinations needed 136-148px, a 12px spread instead
             of that. (2) Re-ran the full step-by-step drag from the
             previous entry's "good" scenario in the previously-broken
             direction (AMP-CAB leading, LOOP trailing behind it, dragging
             toward FX2): the order now progresses ONE step at a time
             cleanly (no skipped intermediate state, no jump), with the
             86px lockstep gap held constant throughout — matching the
             already-good direction's behavior instead of differing from it.
FILES:       ui.js only (chainDragLinkedGapX added; measured at drag-start
             alongside chainDragLinkedCont; hitTestX introduced and used in
             place of dragCenterX for the hit-test and after-check only —
             the continuous-follow transform code is unchanged).
NEXT:        Charlie's real rebuild/hardware test. The residual 12px spread
             between combinations is expected (differing block/connector
             widths between configurations) and not something to chase
             further unless it's actually perceptible on real hardware.-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-28
STATUS:      REPLACED the pair-center fix with a correct leading-edge fix —
             CONFIRMED by Charlie on real hardware, "call that a success"
AREA:        Chain drag — linked AMP-CAB+LOOP, the pair-center fix from the
             immediately prior entry degraded the two directions that were
             already correct
FINDING:     Charlie, sharp catch: "could the changes you just made have
             impacted the two m[oves] previously noted as good? Those seem a
             bit off now but consistent with what the fix did for the
             problem in the other direction." Correct diagnosis on his part
             — averaging AMP's and LOOP's centers to symmetrize the trigger
             distance necessarily worked in both directions: it moved the
             trigger point CLOSER to the target for the previously-broken
             (LOOP-leading) directions, but by the same arithmetic moved it
             FARTHER for the previously-correct (LOOP-trailing) directions.
             Net result: the asymmetry shrank, but both directions were now
             uniformly a bit wrong instead of one being right and one wrong.
ROOT CAUSE:  The pair-center approach was the wrong model. The physically
             correct reference point for "has the pair reached the next
             block" is whichever of AMP/LOOP is actually OUT IN FRONT in the
             CURRENT direction of travel — not a fixed halfway compromise
             that ignores which way you're actually moving.
FIX:         Hit-test now tracks the drag's instantaneous direction (new
             chainDragLastX, compared each mousemove against the previous
             frame's clientX; new chainDragMovingRight, only updated on an
             actual nonzero horizontal delta so a zero-delta frame can't
             flip it) and picks whichever block is leading FOR THAT
             DIRECTION: hitTestX = dragCenterX + (movingRight ?
             Math.max(0, chainDragLinkedGapX) : Math.min(0, chainDragLinkedGapX)).
             When AMP leads, this reduces to plain dragCenterX (zero offset)
             — the exact original, already-correct behavior for that
             direction, untouched. When LOOP leads, this uses LOOP's actual
             center (the full gap), not a halfway point.
VERIFIED:    Live CDP simulation, same 4-combination exact-trigger-distance
             measurement as before, PLUS a plain single-block drag (WAH) as
             a baseline for comparison: the two previously-good (trailing)
             directions now measure 86px/87px — matching the single-block
             baseline (87px/86px) almost exactly, i.e. genuinely restored,
             not just "less wrong." The two previously-broken (leading)
             directions now measure 99px/99px — consistent with EACH OTHER
             and only ~12-13px more than the baseline (the real, expected
             cost of crossing the connector arrow between AMP-CAB and LOOP),
             a small fraction of the original near-block-width asymmetry.
             Also specifically tested a direction REVERSAL mid-drag (drag
             right 250px, then back left past the start point) to check for
             a hiccup at the exact moment chainDragMovingRight flips — the
             order sequence reversed cleanly step-by-step with no oscillation
             or double-jump at the transition. Confirmed clean layout via a
             real-time-paced screenshot too.
FILES:       ui.js only (chainDragLastX + chainDragMovingRight added;
             hitTestX's computation changed from pair-center to leading-
             edge; both new vars reset in endChainDrag).
