AREA:        FX1 — Stereo/Mono toggle blanks the panel ("Unknown model")
FINDING:     Charlie found this only happens on FX1, not DIST/REVERB/WAH/
             VOL: open the FX1 panel, toggle Stereo<->Mono, and the model
             dropdown goes blank / panel shows "Unknown model" instead of
             controls (or the generic placeholder for uncaptured models).
             His app log pinned it exactly:
               [13:51:57] refreshFx1PanelAfterChainMap: mid=0x03 handle=0x1D
                          (Stereo — C1 Chorus/Vibrato correctly resolved)
               [13:52:09] Stereo/Mono: MONO
               [13:52:09] refreshFx1PanelAfterChainMap: mid=0x01 handle=0x22
               [13:52:09] requestFx1Params: mid=0x01 unknown
             ROOT CAUSE: C1 Chorus/Vibrato has a MONO/STEREO MID PAIR — the
             same pattern REVERB (0x26 mono/0x27 stereo) and VOL (0x2B
             mono/0x2C stereo) already handle, just never accounted for on
             FX1: mono=0x01, stereo=0x03, confirmed directly by this log
             (toggling back to Stereo at 13:52:51 reported mid=0x03 again).
             FX1_MODELS only had mid 0x03 in it — mid 0x01 fell through to
             "unknown"/"Unknown model" every time. On top of that,
             openFx1Panel/refreshFx1PanelAfterChainMap compared the
             dropdown against the RAW wire mid instead of resolving through
             the model first (REVERB/VOL already do this correctly) — so
             even after adding 0x01, every mono/stereo toggle would still
             have looked like "the model changed" and needlessly rebuilt
             the panel + dropped the FX baseline each time.
             FIXED, three parts:
               1. protocol.js — C1 Chorus/Vibrato now `mid: 0x01,
                  mids: [0x01, 0x03]` (primary/send mid is the MONO variant,
                  matching the REVERB/VOL convention). FX1_MODEL_BY_MID
                  population now iterates `m.mids || [m.mid]` so both the
                  new variant-aware entries and the plain uncaptured stubs
                  populate correctly.
               2. fx-panels.js openFx1Panel / refreshFx1PanelAfterChainMap —
                  both now resolve FX1_MODEL_BY_MID[fx1Blk.modelId] FIRST
                  and sync the dropdown / compare against model.mid, not
                  the raw wire mid — exact mirror of how REVERB already
                  does this.
               3. index.html — C1's dropdown <option value> updated from 3
                  to 1 to match the new primary mid.
             The other 9 FX1 stub models almost certainly have their own
             mono/stereo pairs too — only whichever variant showed up in
             the original dropdown capture got recorded — but since they're
             captured:false anyway this doesn't block anything; note it
             when each one gets its own capture.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's app log, session20260730135144.log, lines 393-736 —
             the Stereo->Mono->Stereo round trip on a C1-loaded patch with
             the FX1 panel open.
CONFLICTS:   none — this refines, doesn't contradict, the earlier "mid
             table off-by-one" fix (that fix correctly identified 0x03 as
             C1's STEREO mid; this entry adds the previously-missing MONO
             mid 0x01 as a second variant of the same model, it doesn't

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer 7/30/2026 CURRENT APP STATE entry + WORKING/NOT YET IMPLEMENTED/CODE FILE MAP; Tech Ref Sec 23 FX1 SLOT subsection + Sec 24 Change Log + OUTPUT CHANNEL TABLE; Primer HARDWARE SAFETY for the drag-queue race. All confirmed TESTED LIVE by end of session.]
AREA:        DRAG-QUEUE RACE — malformed paramLo=-1 send wedged the hardware
FINDING:     Charlie sent the app's own session log after a hang working the
             Dyn3 Ratio knob (rapid dragging/wheeling). Rack stopped
             responding to everything, including the Avid editor (which
             could still probe/connect but got no data back either) — so
             this is hardware-side confusion, not an app-side hang. Found
             the exact trigger by grepping the log for anything with a
             negative or out-of-range paramLo byte:
               [12:11:51] RX: {"cmd":"send","hex":"F0 13 0B 0F 00 11 0F -1 63 00 00 00 00 F7"}
               MIDI OUT: F0 13 0B 0F 00 11 0F FF 63 00 00 00 00 F7
             paramLo arrived as the JS number -1, which (-1).toString(16) do
             encode as the literal text "-1" (not a hex pair) — the bridge
             sent it as raw byte 0xFF. 0xFF is not a legal 7-bit MIDI data
             byte (bit 7 set) — it is literally the MIDI System Reset
             status byte, landing mid-SysEx. The very next hardware reply
             came back one byte short (13b instead of the expected 14b,
             missing byte), so the rack's OWN parser visibly got confused by
             it too — strong corroboration this exact byte is the trigger,
             not a coincidence. Traffic continued for several more seconds
             after that one bad send, then went silent for good ~12 seconds
             before Charlie force-quit — consistent with a single bad byte
             putting the SysEx parser in a bad state that didn't fully
             surface until more traffic followed.
             ROOT CAUSE — a closure-timing race, present in EVERY effect
             panel's knob-drag handler (DIST, REVERB, WAH, VOL, and the FX1
             just built off the same template), NOT present in the ORIGINAL
             amp tone-knob drag (ui.js), which is why this took this long to
             surface: queueKnobSend throttles a knob's hardware write by up
             to 60ms (KNOB_SEND_INTERVAL) via setTimeout. The DIST-pattern
             drag handlers passed a closure like
               function(v) { sendDistParamWrite(activeParamLo, v); }
             where activeParamLo is the panel's SHARED mutable drag-state
             variable — read at SEND TIME (when the 60ms timer fires), not
             at the moment the closure was created. mouseup resets that same
             variable to -1 immediately on release. So: drag a knob, release
             the mouse within 60ms of the last drag tick, and the queued
             send fires AFTER mouseup already zeroed activeParamLo to -1 —
             sending garbage. The ORIGINAL amp tone-knob handler never had
             this bug because it captures a fresh `const lo = knobs[idx].lo`
             INSIDE the mousemove call before building the closure, so each
             queued send owns its own copy, immune to a later mouseup.
             FIX — same shape in all five: snapshot the shared var into a
             local (`var lo = activeParamLo;`) immediately before the
             queueKnobSend call, and close over that local, not the shared
             one. One line per handler, fx-panels.js: DIST (line ~172),
             REVERB (~397), WAH (~534), VOL (~712), FX1 (~943).
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's app log, session20260730120800.log, line ~5589 (the
             malformed send) and the short/garbled reply immediately after.
CONFLICTS:   none — this bug predates today's FX1 work (DIST/REVERB/WAH/VOL
             have had it since they were built) but was never triggered
             visibly before; it just happened to surface during fast Ratio
             knob dragging today.
NEXT:        Charlie power-cycle the rack (per Tech Ref: after a firmware
             assert/confusion, power-cycle recovers it — this is NOT the
             CMD 0x37 brick class, no factory reset needed) and rebuild with
             this fix before resuming any FX1 work. If a hang recurs after
             this fix, the closure race is NOT the whole story and needs
             more digging — but this is a clean, reproducible, well-
             corroborated root cause, not a guess.-------------------------------------------------------------------

               ratio = 100 ^ (v127/127)

AREA:        THE "9.9 BUG" — endpoint sentinels missing from fx-transport.js
FINDING:     Charlie's live FX1 test: Chorus and Rate knobs visibly reach 10
             on screen but immediately settle back to 9.9 on hardware
             readback; Depth stays at 10 on screen but hardware never passes
             9.9. Named "the 9.9 bug" per Charlie's request — this is a
             RECURRENCE, not a new bug: it's the exact defect already fixed
             once for the amp Tremolo Speed knob (Tech Ref, "ENDPOINT
             SENTINELS ON WRITES", 7/23/2026) — a CMD 0x11 value is five
             7-bit bytes, not one, and sending v0 with a 00 00 00 00 tail
             asks for the literal BOTTOM of that value's step. Byte-
             quantised controls can't tell the difference; anything with
             real sub-step precision reports back one tick low. The 7/23 fix
             (special-case true endpoints with sentinel tail bytes: max ->
             3F 7F 7F 7F 0F, min -> 40 00 00 00 00, else v0 00 00 00 00) only
             ever went into transport.js's amp-knob sendParamWrite. It was
             NEVER ported to the per-effect-panel send functions in
             fx-transport.js — so sendDistParamWrite, sendReverbParamWrite,
             sendWahParamWrite, and sendVolParamWrite (and, until this
             entry, sendFx1ParamWrite, copied from that same unfixed
             pattern) all still send a flat zero tail and are exposed to the
             same bug on any of their knobs that have real precision.
             FIXED for sendFx1ParamWrite only (fx-transport.js) — same
             endpoint-sentinel logic as transport.js, inline comment names
             it "THE 9.9 BUG" so it's grep-able next time. DIST/REVERB/WAH/
             VOL's send functions are UNCHANGED — same latent bug, left
             alone per this session's FX1-only scope; worth a dedicated pass
             later since it's the same three-line fix copied four more
             times.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's live hardware test, 2026-07-30 — Chorus/Rate/Depth
             knobs on the FX1 C1 Chorus/Vibrato panel.
CONFLICTS:   none.
NEXT:        Charlie rebuilds and retests FX1 — Chorus/Rate/Depth should now
             hold 10.0 on hardware, not settle at 9.9. If confirmed, this
             note plus the Tech Ref's existing 7/23 entry are the two places
             "9.9 bug" will turn up on a search. Separately: DIST/REVERB/
             WAH/VOL likely have the same bug on any knob a user pushes to
             its endpoint — flag to Charlie as a follow-up, not fixed here.-------------------------------------------------------------------

               - toggle cell (Chorus/Vibrato) — literally the same shape as
                 VOL's Taper toggle (val=0/val!=0 -> options[0]/[1]).
               - sync cell — a plain dropdown (not REVERB's knob+dropdown
                 Type composite) that reuses SYNC_DIVISIONS /
                 syncIndexFromV127 / syncV127FromIndex from protocol.js
                 (the same 14-zone table as the amp Tremolo Sync), wired to
                 this block's own paramLo (0x06) and its own runtime handle
                 instead of currentParamHi.
             GREEN/RED KNOB STATE — confirmed this is automatic and needed
             no new code: drawKnob() already keys off data-base="fx" (green
             baseline) vs amber, and dataset.orig vs dataset.value (red on
             divergence) — the same mechanism DIST/REVERB/WAH/VOL already
             use. Only the three real knobs (Chorus/Rate/Depth) get this;
             the toggle and sync dropdown do not change colour, matching
             VOL's Taper toggle (also uncoloured) — pre-existing pattern,

             Also extended rebaselineOpenFxPanel() (ui.js) to cover FX1 —

PART 8 — CHAIN DRAG REWORK, 2026-07-19 to 2026-07-28 (build detail)
===================================================================
Moved from the session log 2026-07-31 once the chain drag was confirmed done.
The full saga behind Avid-style live drag: reorder math, the native-HTML5-DnD
dead end, the mouse-tracking rewrite, and the cursor-following ghost. The
conclusion (working, QC-passed) and the one deferred sub-item (FLIP slide
animation) are in the live session log; this is the working detail and the
sequence of wrong turns.

-------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-19
STATUS:      provisional — deferred, not started
AREA:        Chain drag — live reorder preview (Avid's feel)
FINDING:     Our drag shows a thin insertion marker and only reorders on drop.
             Avid instead previews LIVE: as you drag, the blocks physically
             shift to make room, so the row continuously shows the result. That
             is the whole difference in feel Charlie noticed — it is deliberate
             and obvious rather than requiring attention to a small marker.
             THE FX LOOP HAS ITS OWN RULE. Observed: with the loop at position
             7, dragging it left made ALL SIX blocks before it move right in
             one step — i.e. the loop JUMPS to its next legal stop and the
             blocks in between shift as a group. It does not inch through
             illegal positions and get rejected. This matches the front-panel
             behaviour (scrolling steps the loop between its four stops,
             skipping everything between).
APPROACH:    Reorder the actual divs on dragover using a speculative order —
             the same move operation renderChainRow already performs, driven by
             a provisional order instead of the hardware's. Send that order on
             drop; redraw from currentChain on cancel. Remove the edge marker,
             since moving blocks are the indicator and both would be noise.
             Two rules needed: ordinary blocks shift one position at a time;
             FX Loop jumps between its four legal stops.
             BONUS: makes the loop's snap visible DURING the drag rather than
             surprising the user on release — it teaches the rule.
WATCH FOR:   During a drag we are showing a speculative order, so a broadcast
             arriving mid-drag (a move made in Avid, or a patch change) must
             not fight the preview.
OPEN:        Does AMP-CAB have a special rule too? Avid drags an adjacent loop
             along with the amp (Tech Ref Sec 4). And what triggers the loop's
             jump — cursor crossing a threshold, or distance dragged? The
             latter decides whether the preview feels responsive or twitchy.
NEXT:        Deferred by agreement 7/19. Current drag behaviour is correct and
             working; this is feel, not function. Revisit after the effect
             panel work.
UPDATE 7/27 (evening): effect panel work (DIST, REVERB) is done, so this is
             next in line. Charlie re-verified both open questions directly
             against the Avid editor rather than trust the original note.

  STATIC LEGALITY RULE — CONFIRMED, NO CODE CHANGE NEEDED. Charlie supplied a
  full 10x10 table of every legal AMP-CAB x LOOP position pair. Checked by
  hand against ui.js's existing legalLoopIndices()/enforceLoopPlacement()
  (computeReorder's insertion-index math: legal = {0, ampIndex, ampIndex+1,
  length}) for AMP-CAB at 1, 3, 5, 8, 9 and 10 — every row matched. Plain-
  English version of the invariant: LOOP is legal ONLY at position 1,
  position 10, or immediately adjacent to AMP-CAB (either side). That is
  already what the code enforces today. This was the part of the OPEN
  question below that turned out to already be correct.

  LIVE-DRAG "LINKED BLOCK" RULE — newly derived from Charlie's direct testing,
  NOT yet re-checked a second time, treat as strong-but-unconfirmed until
  implementation testing backs it up:
    1. Dragging AMP-CAB while LOOP sits immediately adjacent to it (either
       side, NOT already parked at position 1 or 10): LOOP moves WITH it,
       same direction, same distance, staying stuck on the same side — i.e.
       they behave as one linked "super block" while this holds.
    2. That linked pair stops when LOOP would be pushed past position 1 or
       10 — LOOP hits the physical end of the chain and the whole drag halts
       there (AMP-CAB ends up one-in from the end, LOOP AT the end).
    3. Once LOOP is parked at an end this way, grabbing AMP-CAB again lets it
       roam completely freely through every other slot — LOOP sitting at an
       end is legal regardless of AMP-CAB's position, so they un-link.
    4. Grabbing LOOP directly (AMP-CAB now fixed somewhere) only lets LOOP
       hop between whatever few slots are legal for that AMP-CAB position —
       this part is the EXISTING jump-between-legal-stops behaviour (see
       FINDING above), unchanged.
    5. Confirmed symmetric: works the same whichever side of AMP-CAB LOOP
       starts on (mirrors left/right), and the same whichever direction is
       dragged.
  So of the two things this entry originally flagged as open, only #1-3 above
  are actually new work — the jump-between-stops behaviour (#4) was already
  built. The net new piece is small: "when AMP-CAB moves and LOOP is touching
  it, move LOOP the same amount until LOOP hits an end."

  WHY THIS MATTERS ENOUGH TO FIX (Charlie's words, paraphrased): with the
  current drop-only reorder, you can't see what's about to move while
  dragging, and the missing linked-block behaviour makes some legal
  arrangements very hard or effectively impossible to land deliberately.
NEXT:        Implement per APPROACH above (live speculative-order preview)
             PLUS the linked-block rule above. Re-confirm the linked-block
             rule empirically during implementation before relying on it,
             per the NOT yet re-checked note.
STATUS:      IMPLEMENTED 7/28, PENDING CHARLIE'S BUILD TEST — not yet
             confirmed against the real app/hardware.
UPDATE 7/28: Built in ui.js (computeReorder rewritten, computeLinkedAmpLoopReorder
             added, applyChainOrder extracted, renderChainRow guarded against
             chainDragActive). Before touching the real app, verified the pure
             reorder math with a standalone Node simulation of every scenario
             Charlie described (both AMP-then-LOOP and LOOP-then-AMP starting
             arrangements, dragging AMP, dragging LOOP, hitting both ends).
             CAUGHT A REAL BUG this way: the first version treated LOOP as
             "linked" whenever merely adjacent to AMP-CAB, which wrongly kept
             dragging LOOP along even after it reached position 10 (an end,
             legal on its own). Fixed: linked now also requires LOOP is NOT
             already at either end. All scenarios matched after the fix.
             This does NOT replace a real build test — the simulation only
             proves the array math, not the actual on-screen drag feel, CSS,
             or anything DOM/browser-specific, none of which can run here.
UPDATE 7/28 (later, after Charlie's build test): confirmed exactly the gap
             flagged above — the reorder MATH was right, but native HTML5
             drag-and-drop itself was not usable. Symptom: cursor flickered
             between ok/no-drop, worst on the LOOP<->AMP-CAB swap (9,10) which
             showed the two block labels flashing back and forth at high
             speed; some moves that should work felt "impossible" over many
             attempts. ROOT CAUSE: applyChainOrder moves the actual dragged-
             over divs in the DOM on every preview update, and native drag-
             and-drop does its own hit-testing of whatever's under the cursor
             — moving those elements out from under a native drag confuses
             that tracking. This is a known limitation of native HTML5 DnD,
             not something the reorder math could have caught.
FIX:         Replaced native drag-and-drop with plain mousedown/mousemove/
             mouseup tracking in wireChainDrag (ui.js) — the same pattern
             already used for every knob in this app. A movement threshold
             (CHAIN_DRAG_THRESHOLD = 4px) distinguishes a real drag from a
             plain click, since native DnD used to do that distinction for
             free via its own drag-start heuristic. document.elementFromPoint
             does fresh hit-testing on every move, so it is not confused by
             applyChainOrder having just relocated things (unlike native DnD's
             event-target tracking). draggable="true" and its CSS removed —
             no longer meaningful once native DnD is gone.
STATUS:      RE-IMPLEMENTED 7/28 on mouse-tracking, PENDING CHARLIE'S REBUILD
             TEST. The reorder math itself (computeReorder, the linked rule)
             is unchanged from the version already confirmed correct by
             simulation — only the input mechanism changed.
UPDATE 7/28 (later, after 2nd rebuild): mouse-tracking fixed the flicker and
             "impossible" moves — Charlie confirmed all moves now seem
             possible, matrix not yet fully retested. New gap found: no
             visual follows the cursor while dragging (native DnD used to
             draw one automatically; mouse-tracking does not). FIXED:
             createChainDragGhost (ui.js) — a cloned, pointer-events:none,
             position:fixed element that tracks the cursor with a z-index
             above everything, offset by where within the block it was
             grabbed. Original block stays in the flex flow, dimmed, as the
             "where it will land" placeholder (unchanged from before); the
             ghost is the separate "what you're carrying" visual on top of
             it. document.body.cursor forced to 'grabbing' for the duration
             so the cursor icon stays consistent regardless of what's
             underneath. Charlie also mentioned "a timing var in play" during
             drags — NOT YET UNDERSTOOD, asked him for specifics (what
             exactly he's seeing) rather than guess at a fix.
UPDATE 7/28 (later still): two follow-ups from the ghost, requested
             together but handled as two separate items (Charlie: "chip away
             at a thing or two", both genuinely coupled to explain at once):
  1. SUPERBLOCK GHOST — DONE. The code already knows when a drag is the
     linked AMP-CAB+LOOP case (that was the whole point of computeReorder's
     "linked" check). Extracted it into linkedAmpLoopInfo(fromSlotId,
     baseOrder), shared by computeReorder AND createChainDragGhost, so there
     is exactly one answer to "is this linked" instead of two copies that
     could drift apart. createChainDragGhost now takes an optional partner
     container + which side it's on, and builds a two-block ghost (both
     clones side by side, correct relative order) when linked, a normal
     single-block ghost otherwise. Verified the refactor changed nothing by
     re-running the standalone simulation from the earlier pass — identical
     results before and after extracting the shared helper.
  2. SMOOTH "BLOCKS REACT AS THE GHOST PASSES" ANIMATION — NOT STARTED,
     explained but not built. Charlie: dragging shows no equal-and-opposite
     motion of the blocks the ghost passes over; the reorder just snaps to a
     new arrangement once the cursor crosses a midpoint, instead of those
     blocks visibly sliding out of the way as you approach. This is a
     genuinely separate, bigger animation feature — plain CSS transitions do
     not animate a flex reorder for free (changing DOM order is an instant
     reflow); doing it properly needs the FLIP technique (measure position
     before the reorder, reorder, measure after, animate a compensating CSS
     transform back to zero) applied inside applyChainOrder. Deferred as its
     own chip rather than folded into this pass.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DATE:        2026-07-31
STATUS:      CODE FIX APPLIED, NOT TESTED LIVE — needs Charlie's build/HW test
AREA:        Startup crash — "PROMISE: appLog is not defined" banner,
             regression from THIS SESSION's earlier graphics-scan logging
             fix; missing chain-row graphics came back as a side effect
FINDING:     Charlie: got a red "PROMISE: appLog is not defined" banner on
             launch, and the chain row was back to placeholder graphics —
             "prior to that I had not had missing startup graphics since
             you did the fix earlier." Traced it: the graphics-scan retry
             logic (added earlier this session, index.html) calls appLog()
             directly, unguarded, in four places. That inline <script>
             block sits at line ~1506 in index.html, BEFORE <script
             src="js/ui.js"> at line ~2113 — the file that actually defines
             appLog. The scan's IPC calls (getAvidDir/scanAvidGraphics)
             normally take just long enough to round-trip that ui.js has
             already finished loading by the time their .then() callbacks
             fire, which is why this worked on every earlier test — but on
             this launch the timing flipped, the promise resolved before
             ui.js was ready, and calling the plain global appLog threw a
             ReferenceError. The .catch() handler ALSO called appLog
             unguarded, so the error escaped even that and surfaced as an
             unhandled promise rejection (protocol.js's global handler,
             prefixed "PROMISE:") — which is why it wasn't silently
             swallowed. The crash aborted the scan entirely, which is why
             the graphics came back missing: not a repeat of the original
             bug, a new one this session's OWN fix introduced.
FIX APPLIED: every appLog() call in the graphics-scan block now goes
             through a new safeAppLog() wrapper (typeof-guarded), matching
             the SAME defensive pattern the neighbouring
             setAvidGraphicsManifest/renderChainRow calls in that exact
             function already used — those were guarded from the start,
             the newly-added appLog calls just weren't. No script-tag
             reordering; the guard is simpler and lower-risk.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's screenshot of the error banner + missing chain-row
             graphics. Traced via index.html line numbers (script tag
             order) and protocol.js's unhandledrejection handler (confirms
             the "PROMISE:" prefix format). node --check / inline-script
             syntax check passed. NOT hardware-tested.
NEXT:        Charlie builds and tests live: launch a few times and confirm
             no "PROMISE:" banner and the chain row graphics load

               0x02 LF Gain · 0x03 LF Freq · 0x04 LF Q · 0x05 LF Type ·
               0x06 LMF Gain · 0x07 LMF Freq · 0x08 LMF Q ·
               0x09 HMF Gain · 0x0A HMF Freq · 0x0B HMF Q ·
               0x0C HF Gain · 0x0D HF Freq · 0x0E HF Q · 0x0F HF Type ·
               0x10 Output Gain.
             DESIGN DISCUSSION (before the capture arrived) settled two
             things: (1) Avid's Type control on LF/HF is a knob linked to a
             dropdown (same shape as REVERB's Eleven SR Type) with a
             physical button that WRAPS at the ends — but Charlie decided
             against copying that shape verbatim ("could get confused with
             an EQ band setting"), going with a plain dropdown instead,
             confirmed by the capture showing Type as a genuinely
             continuous 0-127 sweep (same story as Roto Speaker's Speed
             originally) — evenly quantized into 6 named positions since no
             discrete-click capture pins the exact interior boundaries.

             If Charlie likes it live, a deliberate retrofit sweep across
             other panels' dropdowns is a separate future task, not bundled
             here.
             BAND COLOUR / CHANGE INDICATION — Avid colour-codes each band
             (LF red, LMF amber, HMF green, HF blue, OUT red — confirmed
             against Charlie's screenshot), which collides with the app's
             usual red-on-change knob arc (LF and OUT are ALREADY red).
             Design agreed with Charlie ahead of the capture: knob arcs
             keep their FIXED band colour always (cell.bandColor, never
             red), and "changed" moves to the value-text readout instead
             (bold+red) — new logic in updateFx1Knob and
             rebaselineOpenFxPanel (save-repaint must clear the text
             styling too, same lesson as the FX1 slider/save-corruption fix
             earlier this session).
             DISPLAY — Gain: LF/HF are ASYMMETRIC (-24..+12), reusing
             eqSliderDb's plain-linear shape (`linear:true`) per the
             Graphic EQ Output lesson (a false anchor-at-64 would be wrong
             for an asymmetric range with no confirming capture); LMF/HMF/
             Output are SYMMETRIC and reuse eqSliderDb's default two-slope-
             anchored-at-64 shape. Freq and Q are exponential by standard
             filter-control convention (same family as MultiChorus's Low
             Cut) — new eqFreqDisplay (ui.js) formats Hz/kHz to match
             Avid's own readout ("100.0 Hz", "2.0 kHz"). Freq/Q/Type's
             interior shape/boundaries are NOT multi-point-confirmed (only
             endpoints and, for Type, the option count/names) — same
             caveat class as Low Cut.
             LAYOUT — first FX1 model with FOUR grouped boxes (LF/LMF/HMF/
             HF) plus a trailing unlabeled Output column, built on the
             grouped-layout plumbing from MultiChorus. LMF/HMF's second row
             is Q alone (no Type) — confirmed box rows don't need equal
             cell counts.
             MONO/STEREO — 0x12 mono / 0x13 stereo, registered per the
             standing Graphic EQ/Dyn3 lesson, only one wire id observed.

CORRECTION (2026-07-31, same day, Charlie's live test): THREE issues.
  (1) FIXED — after a Save to Rack, the Speed lever rendered as a mangled
      bar with no tick labels (screenshot: before-save showed Slow/Break/
      Fast correctly, after-save showed a blank stretched shape). CAUSE:
      rebaselineOpenFxPanel() (ui.js, fires after every save "so save-then-
      green works") unconditionally called drawKnob() on every .knob-wrap
      in the open panel — fine for every panel until now, since FX1 is the
      first with non-round cell kinds. drawKnob's circular-arc math painted
      onto Speed's 160x34 canvas, corrupting it. LATENT SAME BUG on Graphic
      EQ's vertical sliders too (unreported so far, same root cause) — this
      fix covers both, not just Speed. FIX: rebaselineOpenFxPanel now looks
      up the FX1 cell for each wrap (via fx1AllCells) and dispatches to
      drawEqSlider/drawHSlider/drawKnob same as everywhere else, instead of
      a blanket drawKnob() call.
  (2) FIXED — Type's dropdown read as cramped against the panel's right
      border. FIX: wrapper padding widened on the trailing edge (12px 14px
      -> 12px 28px 12px 14px) — a model with few narrow columns has less to
      pad itself with than a wide multi-group layout like MultiChorus.
  (3) RESOLVED (not a bug) — Charlie clarified: the "fighting" wasn't a
      value jump at all, it was the thumb physically covering the tick
      label it was sitting near (worst right at "Break"), making it hard to
      read while dragging. Charlie's call: replace Speed with a 3-option
      dropdown (Slow/Break/Fast at v127 0/64/127, cell.select — same shape
      as Type) rather than fix the slider's label legibility — gives the
      panel dropdown|knob|dropdown symmetry and "we know that works." The
      underlying param is still genuinely continuous per the capture; the
      dropdown is an accepted UX simplification, not a hardware limit.
FIX APPLIED: Roto Speaker's Speed cell switched from cell.hslider to
      cell.select. Since nothing else uses the horizontal-slider control,
      REMOVED it entirely rather than leave it as dead code: drawHSlider
      (ui.js), the cell.hslider render/update branches (fx-panels.js), the
      activeIsH horizontal-drag-axis tracking in the FX1 drag handler
      (fx-panels.js), and the .h-slider CSS override (index.html). If a
      future model needs a real continuous horizontal control again, this
      commit's history has a working reference implementation.
CLARIFIED (2026-07-31, same day, Charlie's fuller report): the actual
      Speed-lever symptom was the value visibly OSCILLATING/bouncing
      between the tick position and wherever the mouse was, near a
      boundary — a real bug, not the label-occlusion read from the first,

BUILT (ahead of any capture, so nothing here is hardware-confirmed data —
             pure structural capability):
  - GROUPED ROWS — a model's `rows` array entries can now be a flat array
    of cells (unchanged, every existing model still works exactly as
    before) OR a group object {group:'LABEL', rows:[[cells...],...]},
    rendered as its own bordered/labeled sub-box inside the panel, matching
    Avid's CHORUS/MOD/DELAY/EQ/ENV MOD boxes. Ungrouped top-level rows
    (e.g. Rate/Depth sitting outside any box) render as a plain column so
    they line up visually with boxed groups beside them.
  - cell.select — a new generic named-position dropdown (cell.options =
    [{label, v127}, ...]) for controls with 3+ discrete named positions
    that aren't Sync/tempo-driven (e.g. Dynamic Delay's Feedback Mode
    Mono/Stereo/Cross/Pong, MultiChorus's Voices). Nearest-match on
    readback so an unexpected raw value still lands on the closest labeled
    option. Same spirit as the existing cell.sync dropdown, generalized.
  - Waveform Tri/Sine (MultiChorus) deliberately mapped to the EXISTING
    cell.toggle (2-state), not a new control — no reason to build a new
    widget for a binary choice. Avid's tiny rotary-switch graphics for both
    Waveform and Feedback Mode are NOT being replicated pixel-for-pixel;
    dropdowns/toggles are more legible at a glance (Primer's "old eyes"
    rule) and we already own the send/read/R1-R8 plumbing for both shapes.
  - fx1AllCells(model) — flattens a model's cells across flat rows AND
    group sub-rows into one array. updateFx1Knob and the R7 sync-interlock
    helpers (fx1SyncCellLo/fx1CellIsSyncDriven) now call this instead of
    walking model.rows directly, so grouped models get correct hardware-
    readback routing and Sync-interlock behaviour for free — no per-model
    special-casing needed.
VERIFIED: node --check on fx-panels.js; a standalone flatten test (not part
             of the app) confirmed cells nested inside groups are found
             correctly alongside flat-row cells.
NOT YET DONE: no model actually uses group/select yet — MultiChorus and
             Dynamic Delay both still need their Wireshark captures before
             any real paramLo/model data lands. This entry is pure
             plumbing, ready for whichever capture comes in first.
NEXT:        When Charlie's MultiChorus (or Dynamic Delay) capture arrives,
             build its FX1_MODELS entry using group/select as needed — no
             further fx-panels.js changes anticipated unless the real
             layout surfaces a shape not covered here.

             pcapng — every slider parked at 0.0 dB first, swept to the
             extreme, then Charlie tried to dial each back to exactly 0.0 dB
             ("some I overshot... eventually got to zero... that last one
             [Output] I never could land it back on zero"). Decoded the same
             way as the other captures. RESULT:
               SYMMETRIC BANDS — return-to-zero attempts settled tightly on
               raw 63/64 for all five, CONFIRMING the two-slope-anchored-at-64
               formula exactly. No change.
               OUTPUT — return-to-zero attempts clustered around raw 99-100,
               NOWHERE NEAR 64, and never truly settled — proving the
               two-slope-at-64 formula was WRONG for this band specifically
               (explains the "+20.0 dB" baseline visible in Charlie's first
               screenshot). Backed out the math: a plain proportional scale
               across the full 0-127 range puts 0.0 dB at a non-integer raw
               ~97.7, meaning EXACT 0.0 dB may not be reachable at 7-bit
               resolution on this specific asymmetric range — a genuine
               hardware granularity limit, which is exactly what Charlie
               experienced, not a bug to hide.

             two-slope-at-64 formula. Ticks reuse the same flag so they still
             line up with the live thumb position under either shape.

DATE:        2026-07-31
STATUS:      CODE FIX APPLIED, NOT TESTED LIVE — needs Charlie's build/HW test
AREA:        FX1 Chorus panel missing the Sync interlock (R7) the amp has
FINDING:     The amp Sync/Speed interlock (Tech Ref Sec 11 / now R7 in the
             Sec 20A Knob Contract) says: grabbing a Sync-driven knob sends
             Sync=OFF first, once per drag, then writes the knob — mirroring the
             rack's own knob, which clears Sync when grabbed. The FX1 Chorus
             panel (built 7/30) implements the Sync-SETS-the-knob half but NOT
             the grab-CLEARS-Sync half: grabbing Rate while Sync is engaged does
             not turn Sync off, unlike the amp panel and unlike the physical HW.
             Noticed by Charlie 7/31. This is the exact scatter failure the new
             contract exists to prevent — the rule lived only in the amp context
             and was not carried to the effect panel.
FIX APPLIED (2026-07-31, Claude Code): Rate (paramLo 0x04) marked
             syncDriven:true in FX1_MODELS (protocol.js). fx-panels.js FX1 drag
             handler now clears Sync (paramLo 0x06 -> 0) once per drag on grab
             of any syncDriven cell before writing the knob, matching the amp
             pattern exactly (mousedown latch, mousemove check, no write-guard/
             grey-out). Same interlock applied to the FX1 dblclick (R8) handler,
             since a baseline-restore is a knob move too. Built generically
             (fx1SyncCellLo / fx1CellIsSyncDriven / fx1ClearSyncIfDriving read
             the model's row data, not a hardcoded paramLo) so MOD/DELAY's
             future Sync-driven controls only need the syncDriven flag on their
             model row, not new interlock code.
EVIDENCE:    Code only — node --check passed on fx-panels.js/protocol.js/ui.js.
             NOT hardware-tested (no bridge in this session per usual).

             Sync correctly, but Chorus did NOT — Charlie confirmed grabbing
             Chorus while Sync was engaged left Sync on. Matches the earlier
             capture note ("engaging Sync overwrites the live Chorus AND Rate

                 enumeration-only). Closed the mid-0x01 gap the OUTPUT CHANNEL
                 TABLE's own 7/19 retraction note had left open -> coverage 50/53.
               - Dyn3 Compressor: Threshold/Attack/Release/Ratio/Knee/Gain, all
                 six display formulas confirmed (Ratio exponential, verified by a
                 19-point wheel sweep against Wireshark).
             Two bugs found and fixed across ALL FIVE panels (DIST/REVERB/WAH/
             VOL/FX1):
               - THE 9.9 BUG: endpoint-sentinel write tail (from the 7/23 amp
                 Speed-knob fix, Sec 3/4) was never ported to fx-transport.js, so
                 panels couldn't hold their true max (9.9 not 10.0; Pre-Delay
                 ~198ms not 200). Ported to all five send functions.
               - DRAG-QUEUE RACE: a queued knob-drag write could fire after
                 mouseup reset paramLo to -1, sending byte 0xFF (illegal 7-bit)
                 mid-SysEx and hanging the rack. Fixed in all five drag handlers.
                 See Primer HARDWARE SAFETY.
NOTE:        FX1 (0x08) is a generic host slot; FX2 (0x09) hosts the same model
             family, so a model captured here should apply to FX2 without its own
             capture. 8 remaining FX1 models are dropdown-known but uncaptured
             (protocol.js FX1_MODELS captured:false); all stereo-by-nature except
             Gray Compressor per Charlie. Full build-by-build detail: Archive
             Part 7.

  FX1 / FX2 / MOD OVERLAP (per Charlie, 2026-07-30 — see protocol.js
  FX1_MODELS header for the code-level note):
    - FX2 (SLOT_FX2) hosts the SAME model family as FX1 — once a model is
      captured for FX1, expect it to apply directly to FX2 too (same mids,
      same paramLos). An FX2 panel build should reuse FX1_MODELS rather
      than re-capturing every model from scratch.
    - Some FX1/FX2 models may also turn up in MOD's roster — check for
      overlap before capturing a MOD model fresh.
    - MONO/STEREO MID PAIRS (like C1 Chorus/Vibrato needed, Session Log
      2026-07-30): of the FX1 models still uncaptured, all are stereo by
      nature except Gray Compressor (mono-only) — so this is expected to be
      the exception, not the rule, going forward. Still worth a quick
      Stereo/Mono toggle test on each new model once captured, just don't
      expect to spend much time on it.

===================================================================
  DELAY/FX LOOP BUILD RECIPE (Primer, superseded 2026-08-02 — both slots
  are done; FX LOOP has Send/Return/Mix, DELAY has all three model
  families BBD Delay/Dyn Delay/EP Tape Echo, all TESTED LIVE). Kept here
  for provenance and as a reference for the NEXT genuinely new panel
  (should one ever be needed), not because DELAY/FX LOOP need revisiting:

  NEXT SESSION — EFFECT PANEL FOR ANOTHER BLOCK (as it read at the time)
  The remaining chunk is the two non-amp effect panels still on placeholders:
  DELAY and FX LOOP. (DIST, REVERB, WAH, VOL, FX1, FX2 and MOD are DONE.) The
  existing panels established the whole pattern; each new slot needs one
  Wireshark capture for its paramLo numbers, then the same six edits copied
  across. Read QUERY BUDGET first: fetch a block's parameters when its panel
  OPENS, not on every nav.

  QUERY BUDGET — READ THIS BEFORE ADDING BLOCK PANELS
  Measured from Avid (7/23/2026), per patch recall: 11 bypass flags, one per
  block, PLUS 25 parameters for the AMP BLOCK ONLY. Avid queries NO parameters
  for MOD, DELAY or any other block on recall — only their bypass flags. It
  pulls the amp in full because the amp is the panel it shows.
  So our ~20 queries should NOT grow toward Avid's 47 as blocks are added.
  Fetch a block's parameters WHEN ITS PANEL OPENS, not on every nav — the
  distPanelOpen / reverbPanelOpen flags already point this way. Querying every
  block on every patch change adds permanent cost for values nobody is looking
  at, and would make us slower than Avid rather than faster. STILL A LIVE
  RULE for any future panel, even though DELAY/FX LOOP themselves are done.

  WHAT EACH NEW SLOT NEEDED — copy the FX1 panel (built 2026-07-30), NOT
  DIST/REVERB/WAH/VOL, which were built before two bugs were found and
  fixed that day (a rack-hanging drag-queue race, and "the 9.9 bug"):
    1. A Wireshark capture: with our app running, open the effect panel in the
       Avid editor and sweep each knob in order. One capture per model family
       (3-knob, 4-knob etc.) is sufficient. Supply the .pcapng file.
    2. A model table entry (like FX1_MODELS) in protocol.js.
    3. A panel div and chain-open routing in index.html (copy the FX1 pattern).
    4. Open/close/refresh functions + scroll-wheel branch + rebaselineOpenFxPanel
       branch in ui.js/fx-panels.js (copy the FX1 pattern, not DIST's).
    5. CMD 0x11 routing in sysex-handler.js (copy the FX1 routing block).
    6. sendModelChange / requestParams / sendParamWrite in fx-transport.js
       (copy the FX1 functions — sendParamWrite MUST have the endpoint-
       sentinel tail logic, not a flat 00 00 00 00 tail).
  Both slots followed this recipe. DELAY additionally needed the grouped/
  boxed layout ported into its own render functions (Dyn Delay) and a
  NEW rule, R9 (Sec 20A), found via a live-test failure on BBD Delay's
  Sync/Rate interlock — see Tech Ref Sec 20A and the 2026-08-02 Session
  Log for the full story, including a retracted wrong theory about DELAY
  Sync needing a special 28-bit encoding (it didn't).

             clone) since .chain-open[data-panel] won't match their hidden

DATE:        2026-08-01
STATUS:      DECISION AGREED — folded into the same planned refactor (see
             the "REFACTOR FIRST" entry just above this one), not built now
AREA:        FX1/FX2/MOD — model-switch value memory + sane defaults
FINDING:     Charlie confirmed (from real hardware use, not yet a formal
             capture/test) something worth designing for: switching FX1's
             MODEL does NOT behave like the amp block's "shared storage
             under per-model labels" (Sec 11's confirmed amp behaviour).
             Concretely: load a patch with FX1 on Gray Compressor, its
             saved values read back correctly — but switch to any other
             model and back to Gray Compressor, and it shows different
             (apparently default/firmware-supplied) values, not what the
             patch had. Separately, some models' firmware-supplied defaults
             are nonsensical on their own terms — Parametric EQ's Gain
             knobs don't default to 0 dB (flat/no effect) the way a "just
             selected this, haven't touched anything yet" state should,
             they come in with some other value already cutting or
             boosting. Charlie: "a lot of FX Avid put in garbage defaults."
             This matches the Tech Ref's documented mechanism difference:
             amp model change (CMD 0x0F) reuses the SAME handle and shared
             storage (confirmed, tested); FX1/DIST-family model change
             (CMD 0x21) assigns a GENUINELY NEW HANDLE — the Tech Ref only
             confirms the handle reassignment itself, not what happens to
             parameter storage across it. Charlie's live-use report is the
             first evidence of what actually happens: it does NOT persist.
TWO FEATURE IDEAS, explicitly NOT to be built as one-off FX1 code:
  (1) Remember a slot's per-model values across a model switch within a
      session (at minimum: the model the patch loaded with, so switching
      away and back restores it — possibly generalized to EVERY model
      visited that session, open question, Charlie's call not yet made).
  (2) On ANY model switch, don't trust the firmware's freshly-broadcast
      values at all — push our own defined-sensible per-model defaults
      instead (Gain knobs at 0 dB/flat being the clearest example).
AGREED: both belong in the SAME centralized FX-model refactor already
      decided (see entry above) — Charlie's framing: "fix or modify it one
      time, one place, then minor adjust as needed from the callers picking
      from the FX central shopping cart" rather than three call sites each
      needing the same fix. Explicitly NOT to be squeezed into current FX1
      panel code that's about to be rewritten anyway.
OPEN QUESTIONS for the refactor-planning session (not yet answered):
  - Scope of (1): original-model-only, or every visited model this
    session?
  - Source of "sensible" defaults for (2): reuse each model's documented
    capture-time load state (Session Log, per model) as-is, or does that
    need its own review pass first (capture load states were TEST setup
    points, not necessarily curated "good creative starting point"
    defaults)?
  - Session-only, or does per-model memory/defaults need to survive across
    patch reloads / app relaunches too?
NEXT:        Design and build together with the FX1/FX2/MOD centralized
             engine refactor, next session (see the entry above this one).--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AREA:        FX1 panel architecture — copy-paste duplication risk ahead of
             FX2/MOD
FINDING:     Charlie asked whether FX1's panel CODE (not the captured model
             DATA) is shared or duplicated per slot before committing to
             build FX2. Answer, traced from the actual code: the model data
             (FX1_MODELS in protocol.js — paramLo maps, display formulas,
             layouts) is genuinely slot-independent and already reusable as-
             is. The PANEL CODE (open/render/update/drag functions,
             #fx1-knob-row DOM, fx1-w-/fx1-v- element IDs, the CMD 0x11
             sysex-handler routing block) is its own copy specific to FX1,
             same pattern DIST/REVERB/WAH/VOL used before it — SLOT_FX2
             exists as a constant (state.js) but no FX2 panel exists yet.
             Charlie then flagged (with a MOD dropdown screenshot showing 6
             of the 10 FX1 models: C1 Chorus/Vibrato, Flanger, MultiChorus,
             Orange Phaser, Roto Speaker, Vibe Phaser) that building FX2 AND
             MOD the copy-paste way means a bug found later in, say, C1
             Chorus would need fixing in THREE separate places (FX1, FX2,
             MOD), not one — worse than the five-panel porting problem
             already hit once this project (endpoint-sentinel/drag-race
             fixes, 2026-07-30). Also noted: all ten FX1 models "work the
             proper control on the HW" but have NOT been heavily used/
             vetted yet, so more edge cases are still likely to surface —
             an argument for fixing the duplication risk now, before it's
             tripled, rather than after.
AGREED: refactor into ONE shared engine (open/render/update/drag,
             parameterized by slot ID — FX1/FX2/MOD) BEFORE building either
             FX2 or MOD, so a future bug fix or new capture only needs to
             land once. MOD's dropdown would filter FX1_MODELS down to its
             6 supported models rather than getting its own copy of the
             table. Must re-verify FX1 behaves IDENTICALLY after the
             refactor (it's currently working, tested code) before treating
             it as done.

DATE:        2026-07-20 (late)
STATUS:      provisional — process, no protocol content
AREA:        Document regression during an out-of-date chat session
FINDING:     The fix above was made in a chat session opened BEFORE the 7/20
             doc updates, so that session was still working from the 7/19
             copies. The files it returned in good faith had silently reverted
             both the 7/20 GUI documentation (Tech Ref Sec 15 rename and
             save-to-rack, Primer working list, code file map, Phase 4a log
             entry) and the whole 7/20 sweep (Sec 4, 6, 20, 21, 23, C5, C9,
             C11). Nothing was wrong in what came back — it was simply older.
             Caught by diffing the returned files against the last known-good
             set; only the genuinely new finding was carried forward.
LESSON:      A chat holds whatever copy it was given, for its whole life.
             Returning to an older chat without re-uploading the current docs
             produces a rollback that looks exactly like an update, because
             the filenames match. Cheapest tell: LINE COUNT. A doc that comes
             back SHORTER after a session that only ADDED a finding has lost
             something. This one came back 81 lines shorter.
NEXT:        Two habits worth adopting: (1) when reopening an old chat, upload
             the current docs first and say they supersede anything in the
             thread; (2) prefer a fresh chat for a new day's work, since the
             three files carry all the state anyway.--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

             BUILT: every FX1-specific function/DOM id in fx-panels.js,

FINDING:     FX LOOP panel built following the VOL pattern (single fixed
             model, no dropdown — the block's 7 routing-variant mids
             0x2D-0x33 are firmware-picked from chain/stereo context, not
             user-selectable). Three knobs, paramLo 0x02/0x03/0x04:
               Send   -12..+12 dB (two-slope-anchored-at-64, same math as
                      valToAmpVol but without its To-Amp-specific MUTE
                      override at v127=0)
               Return -12..+12 dB, identical shape to Send
               Mix    0-100% linear (v127=0 -> 0%, v127=127 -> 100%)
             All three share the SAME wire encoding: standard
             (v127+64)%128, confirmed against Tech Ref Sec 23's existing
             "Encoding: standard (v127+64)%128" note for this exact model
             (TFX 0F/11/49, CMD 0x20 mid 0x2D-0x33, params send/rtrn/wetp).
             Endpoint sentinel (R1) confirmed needed and implemented:
             sendFxLoopParamWrite copies sendVolParamWrite's three-branch
             tail (v127>=127 -> "3F 7F 7F 7F 0F", v127<=0 -> "40 00 00 00
             00", else raw v0 + flat tail) verbatim.
EVIDENCE:    FX_Loop_Capture.pcapng (Charlie, 2026-08-02) — Windows USBPcap
             capture (linktype 249), endpoint 0x82 IN only (live broadcast
             readback, not the OUT writes — same evidence class as prior
             FX1 model captures). Hand-decoded in Claude Code (no tshark):
             custom Python pcapng block parser + USBPcap header parser +
             USB-MIDI CIN reassembly (CIN 0x4/0x5/0x6/0x7) -> 704 clean
             SysEx frames, F0 13 0B 0F 02 11 22 [paramLo] [v0 4 bytes] F7,
             split cleanly into 3 paramLo groups (0x02: 241 frames, 0x03:
             258, 0x04: 205) matching the capture's stated sweep order
             (Send -> Return -> Mix, each 0dB/0%-start -> max -> min).
             All three groups show the identical raw shape: v0 starts at
             0x40, climbs through 0x7F, wraps 0x00-0x3F, hits sentinel
             "3F 7F 7F 7F" three times, descends back to 0x40 — i.e. every
             knob's v127 (inverse of the wire transform) sweeps a full
             0->127 in one continuous pass, hitting the sentinel exactly at
             the true top.
             Mix's display anchor (0% at the sweep's start, not the 0dB
             centre reading Send/Return get) was NOT inferable from the
             wire bytes alone — asked Charlie directly what the Avid
             editor's Mix readout showed at the start of the capture;
             answer: 0%. This is the ONLY reason Mix isn't wrongly using
             the same two-slope dB-centred display as Send/Return despite
             sharing byte-for-byte identical wire behaviour — flagging this
             explicitly since it's exactly the class of scale mistake the
             Primer warns about (a guess here would have been silent and
             wrong, not caught by any build/compile step).
             paramHi (handle) in the capture is 0x22 — NOT hardcoded
             anywhere in the shipped code (Sec 4 rule); the panel resolves
             the real handle from currentChain via SLOT_LOOP every time,
             same as every other panel.
CONFLICTS:   none — Sec 23's existing FX LOOP row (0F/11/49, mid 0x2D-33,
             "send, rtrn, wetp") already anticipated this exact param set;
             this entry supplies the paramLo numbers (0x02/0x03/0x04) and
             display formulas that row didn't yet have.

AREA:        DELAY effect panel (new panel, Phase 16 continued) — DELAY
             slot 0x06, CMD 0x11 paramLo map, protocol.js/state.js/
             fx-transport.js/fx-panels.js/sysex-handler.js/ui.js/index.html
FINDING:     BBD Delay (mid 0x1E mono / 0x1F stereo, Tech Ref Sec 23 TFX
             1B/30) has 9 controls across paramLo 0x02-0x0A. Charlie
             confirmed the sweep order and control identities directly
             (the wire shape alone could not distinguish them):
               0x02 Mix        0-10 linear
               0x03 Feedback   0-10 linear
               0x04 Delay      32-400 ms linear
               0x05 Sync       14-zone (OFF + 13 divisions, reuses
                                SYNC_DIVISIONS), WIDE 28-bit encoding
               0x06 Input      0-10 linear
               0x07 Depth      0-10 linear
               0x08 Chorus/Vibrato toggle
               0x09 Expanded Delay toggle
               0x0A Noise toggle
             TWO GENUINELY NEW WIRE BEHAVIOURS not seen on any panel built
             so far (FX1/FX2/MOD/DIST/REVERB/WAH/VOL/FX LOOP all use the
             single-byte (v127+64)%128 shape throughout):
             (1) Delay (0x04) reports live sub-step precision in bytes
                 1-3 while dragging (values near 7F 7F 7X approaching an
                 endpoint) instead of sitting flat at 00 00 00 like every
                 other knob's byte0-only encoding. Treated as harmless
                 readback noise — only byte0 is read (same formula as
                 everywhere else), matching the confirmed endpoint
                 sentinel "3F 7F 7F 7F" appearing at the true top exactly
                 like every other knob.
             (2) Sync (0x05) is NOT a v127 parameter at all — it's a
                 28-bit big-endian value (byte0<<21|byte1<<14|byte2<<7|
                 byte3) with what looks like a real SysEx checksum as a
                 5th trailing byte on writes (varies per zone: 04, 07,
                 0B, 0E, 02, 06, 09, 0D, 01, 04, 08, 0B, 0F — not the
                 flat endpoint-sentinel convention). Rather than reverse
                 the checksum algorithm, the WRITE side (DELAY_SYNC_TAIL_HEX,
                 protocol.js) uses the EXACT captured 5-byte tail per zone
                 verbatim — the safest option since we have all 13 real
                 sends. The READ side (DELAY_SYNC_RAW) does nearest-match
                 against the 4 leading value bytes only. Interlock with
                 Delay (0x04) confirmed: every Sync broadcast in the
                 capture paired with a Delay re-broadcast, matching the
                 Tremolo/C1 Chorus Sync-overwrites-value family (R7).
             UNCONFIRMED: Sync zone 0 (OFF) — its raw/tail bytes were
             never captured (the sweep's first broadcast was already the
             move INTO 1/1, so OFF's own send/broadcast never appeared).
             Provisional all-zero tail in DELAY_SYNC_TAIL_HEX/DELAY_SYNC_RAW.
             This is a genuine gap, not a guess dressed up as confirmed —
             flagged in both the code comments and here.
EVIDENCE:    BBDDelay_Capture.pcapng (Charlie, 2026-08-02) — same decode
             pipeline as the FX LOOP capture (hand-written Python pcapng
             + USBPcap header parser + USB-MIDI CIN reassembly), this time
             capturing BOTH directions (endpoint 0x82 IN broadcasts AND
             endpoint 0x02 OUT sends — the OUT sends are what supplied the
             13 confirmed Sync write tails). 1364 IN + 21 OUT = 1385 clean
             SysEx frames. Knob sweep order confirmed by Charlie: "Delay,
             Feedback, Mix, Input, Depth" (matches the capture's own
             temporal paramLo run order 4,3,2,6,7 exactly). Toggle order
             confirmed: "Noise, Expanded, Chorus/vib" (matches temporal
             run order 10,9,8 exactly). Sync order confirmed: "starting
             from off and going down the list" (matches the 13 captured
             raw values mapping 1:1 in order onto SYNC_DIVISIONS indices
             1-13, with the final value landing exactly on the same
             "3F 7F 7F 7F" sentinel every other knob's true-max uses).
CONFLICTS:   none — no prior DELAY documentation existed beyond the TFX
             param-name row already in Tech Ref Sec 23.

AREA:        DELAY effect panel — Sync (paramLo 0x05) OFF value
FINDING:     Charlie's live test reported the Sync dropdown always
             reading OFF no matter what he picked, and that touching the
             Delay/Rate knob while Sync was engaged didn't visibly clear
             it. Root cause confirmed: OFF (zone 0) was entirely absent
             from DELAY_SYNC_RAW/DELAY_SYNC_TAIL_HEX (the original sweep
             never captured it — its first broadcast was already the move
             INTO 1/1), and the decode fallback for "no match found" was
             ALSO index 0 (OFF) — so every unmatched broadcast, including
             OFF's own real broadcast, silently produced the same visible
             symptom either way, masking whether real divisions were even
             being decoded correctly.
             Fixed with a targeted diagnostic capture, same 240 BPM tempo
             as the original sweep (ruling out tempo-dependence as an
             alternate theory floated earlier this session): OFF raw =
             0x40000000 (the exact midpoint of the 28-bit space), write
             tail = "40 00 00 00 00" (the same flat MIN-endpoint
             convention every other knob already uses, Sec 20A R1) — not
             a special case after all. 1/1's raw reproduced byte-for-byte
             identical to the original sweep's capture, confirming the
             table's other 13 values are solid and tempo was never the
             issue.
             delaySyncIndexFromRaw now includes index 0 as a real
             candidate (previously the loop started at i=1, structurally
             unable to ever return a genuine OFF match) and its
             now-unnecessary "too far, fall back to 0" branch was removed
             — every raw value nearest-matches to one of the 14 real
             confirmed zones now, no silent default.
             SEPARATE — also fixed same session, independent of the above:
             R7 interlock (dragging/scrolling the Delay knob now clears

DATE:        2026-08-02
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref DELAY SLOT section's RETRACTED note,
             Change Log 2026-08-02] — RETRACTION — corrects BOTH prior DELAY Sync entries below
AREA:        DELAY effect panel — Sync (paramLo 0x05), full mechanism
FINDING:     Charlie's third live test still failed (dropdown stuck on
              "1/4 triplet" for every selection, including patch load
              with Sync off) — the OFF-value fix wasn't the real bug, it
              was a smaller instance of a much bigger mistake in the same
              area. ROOT CAUSE, fully re-derived from scratch this pass:
              paramLo 0x05 is NOT a special 28-bit-encoded parameter.
              It uses the EXACT SAME plain single-byte v127 Sync encoding
              as every other Sync control in the app (amp Tremolo paramLo
              0x12, FX1/FX-host C1 Chorus) — SYNC_DIVISIONS +
              syncIndexFromV127 + syncV127FromIndex, unmodified, no new
              code needed at all. The two earlier entries this session
              (the original "wide 28-bit lookup table" build, and the
              "OFF value fix" that patched one entry in that same wrong
              table) were built on a misread: bytes 1-3 of the broadcast
              were treated as meaningful 28-bit precision, when they are
              actually incidental live-precision noise — the SAME noise
              pattern already correctly identified and ignored on the
              Delay knob itself (paramLo 0x04) in the very first DELAY
              entry below. Applying that already-known lesson to paramLo
              0x05 too, instead of building new machinery for it, would
              have avoided three live-test failures.
              PROOF (byte0 of the ORIGINAL sweep capture, through the
              pre-existing, already-proven syncIndexFromV127 — no new
              table, no fuzzy matching): v0=0x49(1/1)->val9->zone1,
              0x53->val19->zone2, 0x5D->val29->zone3, 0x67->val39->zone4,
              0x71->val49->zone5, 0x7B->val59->zone6, 0x04->val68->zone7,
              0x0E->val78->zone8, 0x18->val88->zone9, 0x22->val98->zone10,
              0x2C->val108->zone11, 0x36->val118->zone12,
              0x3F->val127->zone13, and separately 0x40->val0->zone0
              (OFF, confirmed by the diagnostic capture) — every one
              matches SYNC_DIVISIONS exactly.
              FIX: deleted DELAY_SYNC_RAW, DELAY_SYNC_TAIL_HEX,
              delaySyncIndexFromRaw, delaySyncRawFromIndex,
              sendDelaySyncWrite entirely (~100 lines, protocol.js +
              fx-transport.js). sysex-handler.js's DELAY routing now calls
              updateDelaySync(val) using the SAME `val` every other
              paramLo in the CMD 0x11 handler already decodes — no
              special-case byte extraction. updateDelaySync (fx-panels.js)
              now takes a v127 (matching ui.js's updateSyncReadout for
              Tremolo) and quantises locally via syncIndexFromV127. The
              dropdown's change handler now calls sendDelayParamWrite(0x05,
              syncV127FromIndex(idx)) — the SAME endpoint-sentinel tail
              every other DELAY paramLo already uses, no custom checksum
              table. R7 local-clear call sites (updateDelaySync(0) on
              Delay-knob touch) needed NO changes — v127=0 already meant
              zone 0/OFF under both the old and new signature, coincidence
              that masked how wrong the rest of the mechanism was.
              LESSON (Charlie's own framing, direct quote worth keeping):
              "What is different about this sync list that all the others
              aren't... Added probably 8 sync lists doing the FX1 block, no
              issues." Answer: nothing is actually different — FX1's Sync
              controls were built by reusing the standard mechanism
              directly, DELAY's Sync was NOT, for no real reason (the wire
              evidence never actually required it — the 28-bit theory was
              read into noisy bytes that a moment's comparison against the
              already-solved Delay-knob noise pattern would have caught).
              TWO FAILED FIXES = STOP AND RE-DERIVE (Primer, "How to work
              with Charlie") applied in hindsight, one live-test-round
              late — worth internalizing for any future "this control
              looks unusually complicated" moment before building new
              machinery instead of checking whether the standard one
              already fits.
EVIDENCE:    Re-analysis only, no new capture — every value cited above is
             from the ALREADY-CAPTURED original sweep + diagnostic
             capture, just decoded correctly this time.
CONFLICTS:   RETRACTS the "DELAY SYNC — 28-bit big-endian" design entirely
             (both the original entry's DELAY_SYNC_RAW/TAIL_HEX tables and
             the "fix DELAY Sync OFF" entry's addition to the same wrong
             table) — protocol.js's DELAY SYNC section now carries the
             retraction note in place of the deleted code, not a dated
             copy.
NEXT:        Charlie re-tests live: patch load with Sync off should show

AREA:        DELAY effect panel — Rate/Delay knob (paramLo 0x04) vs Sync
             (paramLo 0x05) interlock, the WRITE half
FINDING:     4th live-test round: Sync mechanism itself now decodes
             correctly (previous entry), but turning the Delay/Rate knob
             while Sync was engaged set the Sync dropdown to OFF locally
             yet did NOT change the hardware, and the knob itself felt
             "not free" (rack ignoring the write). ROOT CAUSE: the R7 fix
             two entries back only updated the UI locally
             (updateDelaySync(0)) and never actually SENT a Sync-off write
             to the hardware. The code comment at the time claimed
             "Charlie confirmed the rack itself already clears Sync on
             touch, no explicit clear write needed" — that claim was
             never actually re-confirmed for the SOFTWARE-write path
             specifically (only inferred from an earlier, more general
             statement), and it was wrong: this is the SAME rule as amp
             Tremolo Speed (ui.js, already correctly implemented there) —
             the rack refuses a knob write while Sync is on a division
             UNLESS the software explicitly sends the Sync-clear write
             first. Applying the ALREADY-SOLVED Tremolo Speed pattern
             (send sendDelayParamWrite(0x05, 0) once per drag before the
             first Delay value send) fixes it — same as its own dedicated
             comment already described but didn't implement.

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 20A R9] — superseded by the next

AREA:        DELAY effect panel — Rate/Delay knob (paramLo 0x04) drag vs
             an incoming CMD 0x11 broadcast racing it
FINDING:     Charlie asked, from memory of an old solved bug (a knob at
             the bottom of its range jumping to the midpoint then back on
             grab — the classic falsy-zero `parseInt(x) || 64` trap,
             fixed project-wide long ago), whether grabbing the Delay
             knob while Sync had it set to some value might show the same
             jump-then-settle symptom NOW, given today's Sync work.
             Checked the actual code rather than guess: the DELAY drag
             handler already uses the CORRECT guarded startVal read
             (`!== undefined && !== ''`, not `|| 64`), so the classic
             falsy-zero bug itself is NOT present here. But a DIFFERENT,
             real bug in the same failure family was found: the R7 fix
             two entries back (send sendDelayParamWrite(0x05, 0) to
             clear Sync the instant the Delay knob is grabbed) sends a
             real hardware write mid-gesture, and the rack's near-
             immediate broadcast reply for paramLo 0x04 (Delay itself)
             was being painted onto the knob UNCONDITIONALLY — with
             nothing to stop it landing while the user's own drag was
             still moving the knob. That collision (an incoming
             broadcast repainting the SAME control an active drag is
             updating) is EXACTLY the visible shape Charlie described
             (jump to some other value, then back to where the drag
             left it), just a new trigger for an old-sounding symptom,
             not a recurrence of the old bug itself.
             This app already has a proven, shipped fix for this exact
             class of problem: To Amp 1/2 (ui.js, toAmp1Dragging/
             toAmp2Dragging) — "readback handler checks these and skips
             display update while the user is actively dragging,
             preventing HW broadcasts from fighting the drag in
             progress." DELAY never got the equivalent guard. Added one
             (delayDragLo, state.js — which paramLo is the active drag
             target, -1 if none) and wired sysex-handler.js's DELAY
             paramLo 0x02-0x0A branch to skip repainting whichever
             paramLo is currently delayDragLo, same shape as the To Amp
             precedent. Scroll-wheel and double-click paths were left
             unguarded — they're single-shot, not a sustained drag loop,
             so the race window is much smaller and the described
             symptom is specifically about dragging.
EVIDENCE:    Code inspection only — Charlie's question was diagnostic
             (had not yet reproduced this specific symptom live), and the
             fix follows an EXISTING, already-live-tested pattern in this
             exact codebase (To Amp 1/2 drag guard) rather than a new
             invention.
CONFLICTS:   none.
NEXT:        Charlie re-tests when he's back: grab the Delay knob while a
             Sync division is active and confirm there's no visible
             jump/flicker during the drag (on top of the two already-
             fixed issues this session — Sync dropdown accuracy and the
             hardware actually receiving the write). If a jump still
             happens, this specific theory is wrong and needs fresh
             evidence (ideally a screen recording or a described exact
             sequence), not another guess.

DATE:        2026-07-31
STATUS:      RESOLVED 2026-08-03 — root cause found (graphics auto-scan racing
             its own script-tag load order at HTML parse time); fix TESTED LIVE,
             clean across dozens of real-PC and VM cold starts. See the
             2026-08-03 startup-graphics entry above and Primer / Tech Ref Sec 24.
             Original provisional text kept below for history.
AREA:        Chain row — intermittent missing graphics on program load
FINDING:     Charlie: on some launches the chain row shows only dashed
             placeholder boxes, no real Avid pedal thumbnails — intermittent,
             no error visible in the logs, fixed by either relaunching or
             manually running Settings -> Scan Folder. Asked whether a
             startup timing value needed bumping.
             TRACED (not guessed): the graphics scan (main.js scan-avid-
             graphics, walks the whole Avid folder tree for PNGs) runs once
             automatically at startup, racing the MIDI nav pull's own chain-
             row render. Read applyChainOrder (ui.js) closely — it DOES
             re-fetch every thumbnail fresh from the graphics manifest on
             every renderChainRow() call, so a slow-but-eventually-
             successful scan self-heals once its own post-scan
             renderChainRow() call fires (the currentChain.length guard just
             defers that call until the chain is loaded, it doesn't skip it
             forever). The scan TIMING isn't provably the bug on its own.
             What IS a confirmed, evidenced bug: an outright FAILED scan
             attempt (folder walk error, or the "not set" case) was nearly
             invisible — main.js's "folder not set" early-return never
             called logWrite at all, and the renderer's failure handler only
             updated the (usually closed) Settings panel's text field, never
             appLog. So a transient failure (AV lock, disk not settled right
             after boot, etc.) left NOTHING in the log — exactly matching
             "I don't see anything in the logs" — and nothing ever retried
             it automatically, matching "quit and try again... probably
             works" (a relaunch is just a fresh attempt; a manual Scan click
             is just a manual retry).
FIX APPLIED: (1) every failure path (main.js's "folder not set", its catch
             block already logged; the renderer's failure branch and its
             .catch) now calls appLog/logWrite, so a future occurrence
             leaves a trace regardless of ordering. (2) the startup auto-
             scan now retries automatically up to 2 more times, 3s apart, on
             failure — automates the exact "quit and try again" workaround
             Charlie was doing by hand, rather than a blind timing bump.
             Does NOT touch the self-healing slow-scan-vs-fast-nav-pull
             ordering, since that traced out as already correct.
EVIDENCE:    Code trace only (main.js/index.html), no live repro available
             this session — Charlie described the symptom from memory, no
             fresh log/capture. node --check passed on main.js; inline
             <script> blocks in index.html parsed clean via a standalone
             syntax check. NOT hardware/build tested.
NEXT:        Charlie builds and watches for a recurrence. If it still
             happens, this session's fix should at minimum leave a log line
             ("Avid graphics scan failed: ...") pinpointing which failure
             path fired — bring that log back rather than guessing further.
