DATE:        2026-08-03
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer WORKING + Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log,
  2026-08-03] — Charlie confirmed TESTED LIVE, working perfectly after the
  colour/stale-value fix pass.
AREA:        Amp Controls — tone-knob drag/drop reorder + remember (UI)
FINDING:     New feature, agreed with Charlie before coding: the tone-stack
  knobs (up to 8 per amp, AMP_TONE_PARAMS) can be dragged into a user-
  preferred order, per amp, persisted across restarts. Design:
    - Drag handle is the knob's LABEL only (not the knob itself), reusing
      the chain row's mousedown/mousemove/mouseup + FLIP-slide technique
      (wireChainDrag) as the agreed reference implementation — adapted, not
      copied verbatim (see MECHANISM below for why).
    - Locked by default every launch (toneRowLocked, state.js — NOT
      persisted, a deliberate extra guard Charlie asked for on top of the
      label-only handle) — a small lock icon must be clicked to arm
      dragging. A reset icon clears just the current amp's saved order.
      Both docked to the right of the tone-knob row behind a divider
      (Charlie's call, after seeing a mock), no grip glyph on the labels
      (Charlie's call — undocumented detail, shifted label text off-center).
    - Order is loaded from settings.json (toneKnobOrder, keyed by ampKey ->
      array of paramLo) at app-init, BEFORE the first tone knob ever paints
      this session (same timing slot as the existing bank-cache load) — so
      a saved custom order is what's drawn on the very first render, never
      a default-order paint that then jumps to the preferred one. This was
      Charlie's explicit ask ("don't want to see them jump around").
  MECHANISM (why this isn't a literal chain-row copy): the chain row
  physically relocates real DOM nodes on reorder. The tone row's 8 slots
  (tone-k0..tone-k7) are fixed DOM elements that every OTHER tone-knob code
  path (drag/dblclick/wheel handlers, the live CMD 0x11 readback router in
  sysex-handler.js, updateToneReadouts) already addresses by fixed slot id,
  derived from a single new ordering function, getOrderedToneKnobs(ampKey)
  (protocol.js) — physically moving those divs would break every one of
  those id-based lookups. So a reorder here swaps CONTENT between fixed
  slots (paintToneSlots / repaintToneRowPreview, ui.js) instead of moving
  nodes, and the smooth "slide into place" feel is recreated on top of that
  with a FLIP animation keyed by paramLo (content identity) rather than by
  which DOM node moved (repaintToneRowAnimated) — same First/Last/Invert/
  Play idea as the chain row's applyChainOrderAnimated, different unit of
  identity. All existing tone-knob consumers were updated to route through
  getOrderedToneKnobs/toneSlotIndexForLo instead of the raw table filter,
  so drag/dblclick/wheel/live-readback/TFX-initial-readback can never
  disagree with each other or with what's painted on screen about which
  slot is which control.
  Files touched: state.js (toneKnobOrderPrefs, toneRowLocked), protocol.js
  (getOrderedToneKnobs, toneSlotIndexForLo, setToneKnobOrder,
  resetToneKnobOrder), ui.js (updateToneKnobs rewritten + ~340 new lines:
  paintToneSlots/repaintToneRowPreview/repaintToneRowAnimated/
  wireToneKnobDrag/lock+reset button wiring; updateToneReadouts and the
  tone knob drag/dblclick/wheel handlers updated to route by slot), sysex-
  handler.js (live CMD 0x11 tone router updated to route by slot, and
  skips repainting while a reorder drag preview is on screen — same
  "don't fight the live preview" guard the chain row uses), app-init.js
  (loads toneKnobOrder before initMIDI), main.js + preload.js (get/save-
  tone-knob-order IPC, same storeGet/storeSet pattern as bank cache/zoom).
EVIDENCE:    code written and read back this session; node --check clean on
  every touched .js file. NOT run against real hardware — per project rule,
  nothing here is "working" until Charlie builds the EXE and drags real

  [UPDATE 2026-08-03, same day, after first live test] Charlie found a real
  bug: during a reorder drag, knobs that slid into a new slot (not the
  dragged one) turned red and their value/readout dropped toward 64/"--",
  even though no send happened and the watchdog never tripped (correctly
  read by Charlie as "display-only, not a HW issue"). ROOT CAUSE, two
  compounding bugs in repaintToneRowPreview (ui.js):
    (1) knobColor/drawKnob decide red-vs-amber by comparing live value
    against wrap.dataset.orig (baseline) — a property of the FIXED slot
    DOM node, not of the knob's content. The preview repaint moved VALUES
    between slots on reorder but left each slot's dataset.orig behind, so
    a knob landing in a new slot got compared against the PREVIOUS
    occupant's baseline (false red almost every time).
    (2) The dragged knob's own slot content was deliberately skipped each
    preview step (excludeLo) so its position could be driven by a separate
    continuous cursor-follow transform — but that left stale leftover
    value/text in that slot, and since the next step's snapshot trusted
    lo-bookkeeping over what was actually painted, that staleness could get
    attributed to the wrong knob and compound over a multi-slot drag,
    eventually surfacing as a knob showing 64/"--" though nothing was ever
    reset. FIX: repaintToneRowPreview now snapshots+carries dataset.orig
    alongside value/text on every slot swap, and no longer skips the
    dragged knob's own content (safe — its value isn't changing while its
    LABEL is what's being dragged; only repaintToneRowAnimated's FLIP
    transform still skips it, for position only, not content).
  STATUS: fix written; CONFIRMED TESTED LIVE 2026-08-03 (see entry STATUS above).

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer Status line + Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log,
  2026-08-03] — Charlie confirmed resolved.
AREA:        Startup — second launch kills the running instance's bridge
FINDING:     Charlie noticed (may have existed before, only surfaced/noticed
  since the startup gate splash landed 2026-08-02): launching the EXE while
  it's already running shows a brief, barely-visible splash flash, then
  the ALREADY-RUNNING instance's Java bridge connection dies (Restart JB
  in the running instance fixes it, but shouldn't be needed — the second
  launch should just no-op / focus the existing window).
  ROOT CAUSE (main.js): the single-instance lock
  (app.requestSingleInstanceLock()) already existed, but the `if (!gotLock)`
  branch only called app.quit() — which merely SCHEDULES a quit, it does not
  stop the rest of the script executing synchronously. So a second launch
  still reached app.whenReady() -> launchBridge() ->
  killOrphanedBridgeProcesses() (a PowerShell sweep that force-kills any
  ElevenRackBridge.jar process — by design, to clean up a stale one left
  over from a crashed prior session) before the second instance's own
  app.quit() actually took effect — killing the FIRST, legitimately running
  instance's bridge in the process.
  FIX: added `return;` right after app.quit() in that branch, so a second
  launch bails out of the rest of main.js entirely — never reaches
  whenReady/launchBridge/window creation at all.
EVIDENCE:    code read and root-caused directly (killOrphanedBridgeProcesses'
  blanket kill-by-jar-name behavior is unambiguous); one-line fix, node
  --check clean. CONFIRMED TESTED LIVE by Charlie 2026-08-03 (double-launch scenario).
CONFLICTS:   none — killOrphanedBridgeProcesses itself is intentional/correct
  behavior for its actual purpose (cleaning up a truly orphaned bridge from
  a crashed session); the bug was only that a second instance could reach it
  at all.
NEXT:        Charlie's live test: launch the EXE, then launch it again while
  the first is running and connected — confirm the second launch does
  nothing observable (no splash flash) and the first instance's bridge
  connection is undisturbed.

DATE:        2026-08-03
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer WORKING/intermittent-bug note + Tech Ref
  Sec 24 Change Log, 2026-08-03] — Charlie confirmed clean across repeated
  cold starts.
AREA:        Startup — chain row graphics sometimes blank on load
FINDING:     Long-standing intermittent bug (predates the 2026-08-02 startup
  gate; a 2026-07-31 pass added retry+logging but Charlie confirmed the
  symptom still recurs). ROOT CAUSE FOUND: the Avid-graphics auto-scan (and
  the retry loop around it) lived inside index.html's big INLINE <script>
  block (starts ~line 1574), which executes at HTML PARSE TIME — i.e.
  BEFORE the <script src="js/chain-graphics.js"> / js/ui.js tags further
  down the same file have even loaded. The scan is an async IPC round-trip
  (main.js walks the folder, replies); its .then() callback is a genuine
  RACE against those later <script> tags finishing load. If the scan
  resolved first, setAvidGraphicsManifest/renderChainRow didn't exist yet,
  the existing typeof guards silently no-op'd, and the manifest was simply
  never applied that session — timing-dependent, so it took many runs to
  reproduce, and the 2026-07-31 retry logic couldn't help because every
  retry re-runs the exact same race, not a different failure mode.
  FIX: the scan trigger is now exposed as window.startAvidGraphicsAutoScan
  (defined in the same inline-script closure, so runAvidGraphicsScan/
  safeAppLog are still reachable) but no longer auto-invoked there. Instead
  app-init.js's init() calls it — init() only ever runs after EVERY script
  has fully loaded (it's wired via a setTimeout(init, 150) at the bottom of
  app-init.js, the last file loaded), so the race is structurally gone, not
  just less likely. This lines up with Charlie's own suggestion ("scan
  right at the transition point of the loading screen going away") — init()
  IS that checkpoint; the manual Settings-panel Scan button is untouched
  (its handler already only runs after a user click, long after load, so it
  was never exposed to this race).
EVIDENCE:    root-caused directly from script tag order in index.html + the
  promise-based scan flow (unambiguous once traced to which script block
  the auto-scan actually lived in). node --check clean. CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-08-03: dozens of cold starts
  since, across both real PC and VMs, with no recurrence — well past the
  several-cold-starts bar an intermittent bug needs.
CONFLICTS:   none — additive/relocation only, no protocol or manifest-format
  change.
NEXT:        Done — confirmed fixed across dozens of real-PC and VM cold
  starts with no recurrence. Closed.

DATE:        2026-08-03 (UPDATE — addressing bug found + fixed same day)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer DO NOT RETRY + WORKING (Jump List) + Tech
  Ref Sec 24, 2026-08-03] — Charlie confirmed TESTED LIVE, A-G banks all
  correct.
AREA:        Jump List by-name scan — wrong CMD 0x04 addressing (B1-B4
  showed factory a1-a4's names, C1 onward blank)
FINDING:     Charlie's first live test (session log attached,
  session20260803101536.log) surfaced a real bug immediately: A1-A4 named
  correctly, but B1-B4 showed factory a1-a4's names instead, and C1 onward
  came back blank. Root cause, CONFIRMED directly from the attached
  capture: CMD 0x04's GET-form addressing is NOT the SAVE protocol's
  bank=floor(slot/4)/num=slot%4 split this feature originally (wrongly)
  reused. The real addressing is data[6]=SPACE (0x00=user patches,
  0x01=factory patches — only two valid values, confirmed: bank 2+ got no
  reply at all in the capture, matching the blank C1-onward Charlie saw),
  data[7]=the FULL raw slot 0-103 within that space (not slot%4). Under the
  old wrong scheme, slot 4 (B1) sent bytes (1,0), which the real hardware
  reads as [space=1 FACTORY, slot=0] = factory a1 — exactly the bug.
  Slots 0-3 (A1-A4) happened to look correct by coincidence only
  (floor(slot/4)=0 for those four regardless of which scheme is used).
  The NAME DECODE ITSELF (offset 8 to null terminator) was correct from the
  start — the capture's raw ASCII bytes matched the displayed names exactly
  every time; only the space/slot math was wrong.
  FIX: sendPatchNameQuery (transport.js) now takes (slot, spaceIdx) with
  spaceIdx defaulting to 0 (user) — this app only tracks user A1-Z4 by
  design (Charlie's call, factory a1-z4 explicitly out of scope for now,
  though he flagged it as maybe worth reconsidering some day). scanPatchNames
  (capture-scan.js) sends the raw slot directly, no more /4 math.
  handlePatchNameEnumReply (sysex-handler.js) reads data[6] as space (ignores
  the reply entirely if it's factory, 0x01) and data[7] as the raw slot.
  ALSO NOTED, not acted on: Charlie mentioned the MIDI Monitor panel has
  been hidden/disabled for a while (since full file logging was added) —
  he used the attached session log instead, which turned out to have every
  byte needed anyway (appLog captures the same hex the Monitor would have
  shown). No change made here; flagging in case Charlie wants the Monitor
  panel itself revisited separately.
  ALSO RAISED, explicitly deferred: Charlie noticed the hidden "Scan Bank"
  button/feature (capture-scan.js/index.html) is real prior art toward a
  future full bank export/import (EHB format, Tech Ref Sec 17) — agreed
  as a separate future topic, not part of this session.
EVIDENCE:    session20260803101536.log (Charlie's attachment) — direct
  request/reply hex pairs for all 8 replies that came back, cross-checked
  byte-for-byte against the fix. node --check clean on every touched file.
CONFLICTS:   corrects the previous entry's (now-superseded) description of
  this addressing as reused-with-medium-confidence from the SAVE protocol —
  that assumption is now known WRONG, replaced with the confirmed scheme
  above.
NEXT:        Charlie's re-test: confirm A1-Z4 all populate with correct
  names (not just A1-A4), confirm no factory names leak into the user list.

       Charlie: "the exact reason I avoid Chromium browsers." mainWindow
       already had backgroundColor set (rules out the other common cause,
       an unpainted white frame), so this is most likely a Windows DWM
       compositor artifact from destroying a topmost frameless window
       (splash) in the SAME tick a hidden window is first shown. FIX
       (main.js, 'app-ready' handler): show() the main window, then wait
       120ms before closing the splash, giving the compositor one paint
       cycle to actually get the main window on screen before the splash
       frame disappears, instead of both changes landing simultaneously.
       Also added html{background} (index.html) as a belt-and-suspenders
       safety net alongside the existing body background.

AREA:        Splash->main white flash — STILL PRESENT after the window-

FINDING:     Charlie confirmed the 120ms splash-close delay (main.js) did
  NOT remove the flash, and restated clearly: it did not exist before the
  Jump List work specifically. Per the Primer's own "two failed fixes = stop
  and re-derive" rule, did not just retry a variant of the same window-
  manager-timing guess. Re-derived by asking: what does the Jump List
  feature add that runs UNCONDITIONALLY on every single launch, regardless
  of whether the list is ever opened? Everything else it added (click
  handler, grid rebuild, colour/size CSS) only executes if the user actually
  opens the overlay — it can't explain a flash on a launch where they never
  touch it. The one exception: the by-name scan (scanPatchNames), which
  fires unconditionally 2s after every 'connected' event — 104 rapid MIDI
  sends + log lines, real main-thread work. Theory: if that burst happens to
  overlap the renderer being busy right as Chromium tries to composite the
  just-shown main window, the paint could be delayed and show a blank/stale
  frame — a renderer-side stall, not the window-manager-handoff race the
  first fix targeted, which is exactly why that fix had no effect regardless
  of whether the theory is right.
  ALSO RECONSIDERED, same pass: the EARLIER same-session chain-graphics-race
  fix (moving the Avid graphics auto-scan from index.html's inline <script>
  into app-init.js's init()) has a side effect not accounted for at the
  time — its OLD trigger point (raw HTML parse time) fired well BEFORE
  initMIDI()/bridge-connect even starts (several script loads + init()'s own
  earlier steps stood between them). Its NEW trigger point (early in init(),
  a handful of quick awaited IPC calls before initMIDI()) is actually CLOSER
  in time to the connect/reveal sequence than before, not further away —
  the opposite of what "move it later, after scripts load" sounds like it
  should mean. That scan involves a disk walk + IPC round-trip + a real
  renderChainRow() repaint — also genuine main-thread work landing near the
  reveal window on a real launch's timing.
  FIX: both background scans — the Jump List name scan (transport.js) and
  the graphics auto-scan (app-init.js) — pushed further out (2s->5s and
  immediate->3s respectively) so neither can realistically land during the
  reveal transition regardless of how fast or slow a given launch's
  connect/nav-pull sequence is. Name scan's own pacing also gentled
  (NAME_SCAN_GAP_MS 20->30ms, capture-scan.js). The earlier 120ms
  splash-close delay (main.js) is left in place — harmless, and a
  legitimate general practice, just not what's actually causing this.
EVIDENCE:    node --check clean on every touched file. Reasoning only —
  Claude Code cannot reproduce a Chromium/Electron paint-timing artifact
  directly; this is the best re-derived hypothesis from what's structurally
  different about Jump-List-era launches vs. before, not a confirmed root
  cause. Flagged honestly as still provisional.
CONFLICTS:   none — timing-only changes, no behaviour change to what either
  scan actually does once it fires.
NEXT:        Charlie's live test. If the flash is STILL present after this,

  timing — e.g. whether the flash reproduces with the Jump List's new CSS

DATE:        2026-08-03 (RESOLVED — confirmed VM-only, not this app)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: see below] — non-issue, timing reverted
AREA:        Splash->main white flash — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: VM-only, not
  caused by Eleven Edit at all
FINDING:     Charlie tested on his primary (real) PC: no flash. Only
  reproduces on VMs — a virtualized/software GPU rendering artifact,
  matching a long-standing, unfixed Chromium/Electron compositor bug on
  virtualized graphics that has nothing to do with this app's code. The
  flash was very likely always latent on VMs; the Jump List work's timing
  just happened to shift the reveal moment enough to make it visible for
  the first time, not introduce a new bug.
  REVERTED both timing changes from the two chase attempts above, since
  neither fix addressed a real cause and both made real features
  noticeably slower for no benefit:
    - Jump List name scan (transport.js 'connected' handler): 5s -> back
      to 2s; NAME_SCAN_GAP_MS (capture-scan.js) 30 -> back to 20.
    - Avid graphics auto-scan (app-init.js): 3s delay removed, fires
      immediately again (as it did right after the original chain-graphics
      race fix, earlier this same session).
  The 120ms splash-close delay (main.js 'app-ready' handler, the FIRST
  flash-chase attempt) was left in place — harmless/imperceptible, doesn't
  affect either scan's speed, and is a reasonable defensive practice on its
  own merits even though it wasn't the actual cause here.

AREA:        /NOGPU startup flag — opt-in software rendering for VM
  shortcuts (white-flash mitigation)
FINDING:     Follow-up to the VM-only white-flash finding above. Charlie
  asked for a command-line flag (his suggestion: something like /NoGpu) he
  can add to VM-only shortcuts, rather than a global always-on change —
  same pattern the app already uses for /LOGS (process.argv check in
  main.js). /NOGPU calls app.disableHardwareAcceleration() before
  app.whenReady(), which takes Chromium off the GPU-compositor code path
  that has the flash bug, forcing software rendering instead. App-wide when
  present — only meant for shortcuts aimed at VMs, not the real-hardware
  install, since forcing software rendering carries some performance cost
  (likely small for this app's simple 2D UI, but untested).
EVIDENCE:    code written and read back; node --check clean on main.js.
  NOT yet tested live — needs Charlie to add /NOGPU to a VM shortcut's
  target and confirm both (a) the flash is gone/reduced, and (b) the app
  still feels responsive running in software-rendering mode.
CONFLICTS:   none — opt-in, no effect at all unless the flag is present.
NEXT:        Charlie: add /NOGPU to the Target field of a VM shortcut
  (after the .exe path, e.g. "...\Eleven Edit.exe" /NOGPU) and test.

FINDING:     Charlie reported a different flash from the VM white-flash

  start (waited a while since last launch); an immediate relaunch right
  after closing never shows it.
  BEST-GUESS CAUSE (unverified — Claude Code cannot reproduce or observe
  this directly): Windows' own "ghost window" launch feedback — Explorer/
  DWM shows a placeholder sized like the app's LAST window when a launch
  takes a moment, dismissed once the real window appears. This would
  explain both why it's sized like the main window specifically (that's
  the window Windows remembers, not the splash) and the cold/warm timing
  (a cold start's slower process spin-up gives Windows enough of a gap to
  decide to show the placeholder; a fast warm relaunch doesn't). This is a
  genuinely different mechanism from the VM white-flash (a Chromium GPU-
  compositor bug) — different color, different size, different trigger
  condition, likely a Windows-shell-level artifact rather than anything in
  Eleven Edit's or Chromium's render pipeline.
  FIX TRIED: app.setAppUserModelId('com.charleswardick.eleveneedit')
  (main.js, called early, before app.whenReady()) — can help Windows
  correctly associate the launching process instead of guessing/ghosting.
  One line, cheap, easy to revert if it makes no difference.
  ALSO NOTED: Charlie separately confirmed his OTHER two Electron-based
  Eleven Rack utilities show the classic white flash too — independent
  confirmation this class of bug is a real, widespread Electron/Chromium
  issue, not anything specific to how Eleven Edit is built.
EVIDENCE:    none yet — reasoning only, explicitly flagged as unverified.
  node --check clean on main.js.

DATE:        2026-08-10
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 17, replaces the old Sec 17
  placeholder] — CONFIRMED as the mechanism actually shipped, though
  still ONE capture, ONE machine, not cross-checked
  against a second run or against real hardware traffic from Eleven Edit
  itself sending these commands.
AREA:        Bank export/import — Avid Editor's OWN mechanism (distinct
  from ElevenHack's EHB method, Tech Ref Sec 17). Not yet in Tech Ref;
  this is genuinely new protocol, no prior research existed.
FINDING:     Captured via Wireshark/USBPcap (Charlie): Avid Editor sitting
  at a random patch, "Save All Rigs to Computer" -> pause with a couple of
  live patch changes -> "Load All Rigs from Computer" (same files just
  written). Decoded raw USB bulk transfers by hand (USBPcap header parse +
  USB-MIDI CIN reassembly into SysEx, same method as the 7/31 FX1 capture
  work) — no tshark available in-session, so a from-scratch Python decoder
  was written and cross-checked against protocol.js's own decode7bit.
  EXPORT ("Save All Rigs to Computer") — for slot 0x00-0x67 (all 104), ONE
  query/reply pair each, NO recall, nothing touches the active patch (this
  is why it never visibly walks slots on the front panel, unlike our own
  Scan Bank / the ElevenHack method):
    OUT: F0 13 0B 0F 01 00 [slot] F7
    IN:  F0 13 0B 0F 12 00 [slot] [encode7bit(968-byte body), 1107 bytes] F7
  No separate name query — the name comes for free inside the body (same
  body layout as Tech Ref Sec 13: name at body[8:35], reversed per
  quadlet). Decoded slot 0's reply body with protocol.js's own decode7bit
  algorithm (reimplemented in Python) and got a clean, fully-readable name
  ("800 EchoScream") straight out of the documented offset — the body
  format is NOT new, it's the same one already fully trusted.
  IMPORT ("Load All Rigs from Computer") — for each slot, TWO writes, again
  no recall, no separate commit step, no rename-then-commit dance:
    OUT: F0 13 0B 0F 00 00 [slot] [encode7bit(968-byte body)] F7  -> HW
      echoes it back (dir 0x02, same cmd)
    OUT: F0 13 0B 0F 00 04 00 [slot] [ASCII name] 00 F7            -> HW
      echoes it back (dir 0x02, same cmd)
  Decoded the slot-0 bulk write the same way -> same name, "800
  EchoScream" -> confirms export and import share one wire body format,
  consistent with Sec 13's "all sources SHA-256 identical" claim.
  The CMD 0x04 write here uses a THIRD addressing scheme for that command
  (constant 0x00, then a single raw slot byte 0-103) — distinct from BOTH
  the Save protocol's bank/num split (Sec 15) AND the enumerate-GET's
  user/factory-space + raw-slot format (the 2026-08-03 DO NOT RETRY entry).
  Do not conflate any of these three.
  EVERYTHING ELSE IN THE CAPTURE (a ~78-query CMD 0x11 param dump appearing
  twice, plus one instance each of CMD 0x21/0x50/0x07/0x08/0x0A/0x03) is
  Avid's own live UI re-syncing to whichever patch got selected during
  Charlie's "patch change or two" pause — normal already-understood
  traffic, not part of the bank protocol, not restated here.
EVIDENCE:    Djent_and_Newer_Modded_20260615_1649.xml (bank map: <bank>/
  <file> pairs, populated slots only, no -unused- entries) +
  Bank_Save_Bank_Load_Capture.pcapng (843 reconstructed SysEx messages;
  USBPcap linktype 249, device 4, endpoint 0x02/0x82, bulk transfers).
  Both files are Charlie's uploads this session, not yet copied into the
  repo/captures folder.
CONFLICTS:   none with existing Tech Ref content — this is a new mechanism
  documented nowhere yet, not a correction to anything confirmed. Tech Ref
  Sec 17 (EHB) stays accurate for the ElevenHack method; this would become
  a NEW subsection, not a replacement, once confirmed further.
NEXT:        Charlie separately captured ElevenHack doing the same
  save/load of the same bank files, not yet reviewed — worth a look to (a)
  confirm it actually matches the already-documented Sec 15-17 protocol
  (recall-per-slot via CMD 0x03 + 4-step Save sequence) rather than assume
  it, and (b) get real round-trip/timing numbers for a fair side-by-side
  with the Avid method above. Decide implementation approach with Charlie
  before writing any code. If/when confirmed by a second capture or a live
  build test, fold into Tech Ref as a new Sec (post-17) and update Primer
  NOT YET IMPLEMENTED accordingly.

DATE:        2026-08-10
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 17 "ABANDONED APPROACH"] —
  SUPERSEDED — see the follow-up entry immediately below,
  same date, for what actually shipped. Left in place rather than
  deleted: the byte-level evidence and the ruled-out name-length theory
  are still the reasoning trail that led to the fix, and Charlie asked
  this failure be documented clearly for anyone who wants to pick the
  no-recall approach back up post open-source.
AREA:        Export All Rigs — cold-slot read produces a stale
  signature/cab-table snapshot Avid's loader rejects
FINDING:     Live test (Charlie): a bank exported via the new Export All
  Rigs button mostly round-trips fine, but Avid Editor errors loading
  specific patches, both individually and as part of the full bank.
  RULED OUT: patch name / character length. First suspected an internal
  16-char-name truncation quirk in Avid's OWN exporter (real, separately
  confirmed — see below) but that turned out to be a red herring for
  THIS bug: Charlie's existing, already-shipped "Capture Current Patch"
  manual-save (unrelated code, REQU_SEND_PATCH, no slot argument) captured
  the SAME "Glaswerks SODII1" patch with the SAME full correct name and
  imports into Avid with zero problems — so a correct 16-char name is not
  what Avid is rejecting.
  ACTUAL DIFFERENCE (byte diff, manual-capture file vs bank-export file of
  the identical patch, both 1088 bytes, name field identical): 8 bytes
  differ — file offset 60-63 (= body[4:8], the documented "signature/
  headerCode" field, Sec 13: "changes when content changes") and four
  single bytes at file offsets 324/340/356/372 (body[268]/[284]/[300]/
  [316], evenly spaced 16 bytes apart, inside the per-model cab-data
  table region).
  THEORY (unconfirmed): the manual capture only ever runs against a patch
  Charlie has just navigated to / recalled — a "hot", fully-settled patch.
  Export All Rigs deliberately never recalls anything (Sec 17-adjacent,
  the whole point of the Avid-style by-slot method vs ElevenHack's
  recall-per-slot) and fired all 104 queries back-to-back with ZERO gap
  between them. If the signature/cab-table fields only get freshly
  recomputed at recall time, a cold slot read with no settle time could
  hand back a stale signature sitting next to otherwise-current data —
  and Avid's loader appears to validate that signature and reject the
  mismatch (would explain "works individually as a single capture" vs
  "fails via the no-recall bulk walk" perfectly). Does NOT explain why
  Avid's OWN by-slot-style export (same wire mechanism, Sec 24 2026-08-10
  entry) never hits this — possibly a timing/pacing difference between
  Avid's own walk and ours, not something visible in the wire capture.
  FIX APPLIED (bank-transfer.js/state.js): added EXPORT_SETTLE_MS (60ms)
  before EVERY by-slot query, including the first, instead of firing all
  104 with no gap. Pure bet on timing, not proven — NEXT below is the
  actual test.
  SEPARATE, CONFIRMED, UNRELATED FINDING (worth keeping even though it
  wasn't this bug): Avid Editor's OWN exporter silently drops the LAST
  character of a patch name whenever that name is exactly one character
  longer than the immediately-preceding slot's name and shares its full
  prefix (e.g. slot content "Dumble Drive" then next slot "Dumble
  Drive1" -> Avid's own file for the second slot loses the "1"; same
  pattern confirmed independently on "Glaswerks SODII" -> "Glaswerks
  SODII1", a 16-char name at the true documented limit). Eleven Edit's
  exports (both old single-patch capture AND new bank export) capture
  the correct full name every time — cross-confirmed by two independent,
  unrelated code paths agreeing with each other and disagreeing with
  Avid. Reads like a stale-buffer/didn't-fully-clear bug in Avid's own
  legacy capture code, specific to back-to-back same-prefix names — not
  an Eleven Edit defect, but worth remembering since it means Avid's own
  reference exports cannot always be trusted as ground truth for a
  name field specifically.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's live files this session: Dumble_Drive.tfx /
  Dumble_Drive1.tfx / Dumble_Drive2.tfx (ours) vs AE_Dumble_Drive.tfx /
  Dumble_AE_Drive1.tfx / Dumble_AE_Drive2.tfx (Avid's own, same slots);
  Glaswerks_SODII.tfx / Glaswerks_SODII1.tfx (ours, bank export) vs
  AE_Glaswerks_SODII.tfx / Glaswerks_AE_SODII1.tfx (Avid's own);
  Glaswerks_SODII1_manual.tfx (ours, single-patch capture, the working
  reference this entry is built on). All decoded and byte-diffed in
  session (Python, decode logic mirrors protocol.js's trusted
  decode7bit/extractNameFromBody).
CONFLICTS:   none with existing Tech Ref content — Sec 13's "968-byte
  body" figure is ALSO now known to be a typical/simple-patch figure, not
  a fixed constant (1032/1088/1096/1192-byte bodies all seen this session
  on real, valid, non-corrupt patches with richer effect chains) — worth
  a Tech Ref wording fix at wrap, not urgent.

DATE:        2026-08-10
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 17, Primer CODE FILE MAP
  (sysex-handler.js)] — CONFIRMED, Charlie: "2 for 2 good" (two full
  clean 104-file exports at the lowered 200ms settle time, back to back)
AREA:        scanSlot() (capture-scan.js) / handleBulkTfxData
  (sysex-handler.js) — a genuine race condition in code that predates
  this session entirely, just never exercised at real scale before
FINDING:     Charlie's live test: full Export All Rigs runs coming back
  with roughly HALF the expected 104 files (54, then 55, on separate
  runs) — one BEFORE the SCAN_SETTLE_MS 400->200 change, one AFTER, so
  NOT a timing-margin regression from that change specifically.
  ROOT CAUSE: scanSlot() recalls a slot, then sends a bare
  REQU_SEND_PATCH (no slot argument — queries whatever's CURRENTLY
  active). That reply (CMD 0x01) carries no slot number on the wire
  either, so handleBulkTfxData has always had to GUESS which slot a
  reply belongs to by reading the app's own `currentSlot` variable —
  which itself only updates when a SEPARATE broadcast (CMD 0x02, "slot
  confirmed") happens to have already arrived. Two independent messages,
  no ordering guarantee between them — if the confirm broadcast is even
  slightly late, the guess is wrong, the early-exit match
  (`slotNum === pendingScanSlot`) fails, the reply falls through
  unclaimed, and that slot's scanSlot() promise just times out and gets
  skipped. This is not new code from this session — scanSlot() and this
  matching logic are exactly what Scan Bank has always used, but Scan
  Bank was a hidden, essentially unused feature (Primer: display:none,
  no UI entry point), so a race this session is the first time anything
  has walked all 104 slots through it for real, repeatedly, and actually
  checked the output count.
  FIX (sysex-handler.js, handleBulkTfxData): for isReqResp (CMD 0x01)
  replies specifically, trust pendingScanResolve unconditionally instead
  of gating on the currentSlot guess — nothing else sends a bare
  REQU_SEND_PATCH while a scan/export has one in flight, so if
  pendingScanResolve is set, this IS that reply regardless of whether
  the confirm broadcast has landed yet. Resolves with pendingScanSlot
  (the slot actually requested) rather than the possibly-stale guess.
  CMD 0x00 broadcasts (which DO carry a real slot byte) are unaffected —
  still require the exact match, no change to that path.
  NOTE: this is a MATCHING-LOGIC fix, not a delay/timing tweak — the
  distinction matters per the Primer's "two failed fixes" lesson from
  earlier this same session (export's cold-read corruption). A wrong
  guess resolved with more patience is still a wrong guess; this fixes
  what the guess was based on.
EVIDENCE:    code read start to finish, race explained mechanically;
  node --check clean. NOT yet confirmed against a live full walk.
CONFLICTS:   none — same bug would also explain any historical Scan Bank
  flakiness nobody investigated, since it was never used enough to
  notice a pattern.
NEXT:        Charlie runs a full Export All Rigs and confirms 104 files
  land every time, across a couple of repeats (per the Primer's own rule
  that an intermittent/race-class bug needs more than one clean run to
  trust, not just one). If it's still short, get the exact count and
  which slots are missing — that would mean there's a second, different
  cause, not just this race.

FINDING:     Import Rigs previously only accepted a loose .xml with

DATE:        2026-08-10
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 17, Primer WORKING] — CONFIRMED,
  Charlie's screenshot showed exactly this behaviour live (87 valid / 17
  flagged with reasons, one clean decision point)
AREA:        Import Rigs — validate-up-front, decide-once (replaces the
  original all-or-nothing behaviour, same day)
FINDING:     Charlie's own clarification of Avid's real behaviour: Avid
  does NOT validate up front — it writes progressively until it hits a
  bad/missing file, throws an error on that one patch, and just STOPS,
  leaving a partially-imported bank with no clear record of what did or
  didn't land. Eleven Edit's first cut at Import Rigs (same day, earlier
  entry) went the other direction — all-or-nothing, abort before any
  write if ANYTHING was missing — which Charlie found too strict (one
  bad file in a 104-entry bank blocked the other 103).
  DECIDED (Charlie: "we will make you plan option B and go with it...
  better to check up front than mid stream"): neither of the above.
  Validate every entry up front like before, but instead of aborting,
  return BOTH lists — valid (ready to write) and problems (missing or
  malformed, with why) — and show ONE prompt with the complete picture:
  "Will import N patch(es)... Skipping M missing/invalid patch(es):
  [bank — filename (reason)]..." Charlie chooses skip-and-continue
  (Import button, disabled/relabeled "Nothing to Import" if valid is
  empty) or Cancel. No hardware write happens before that one decision,
  and no per-failure interruptions during the walk itself.
  main.js's read-import-bank (XML and ZIP paths both) now returns
  { ok, valid, problems } instead of aborting with ok:false on the first
  problem. bank-transfer.js's confirm-modal wiring shows both lists
  (problems in --red) and only ever imports the valid list.
EVIDENCE:    code read back; node --check clean on both touched files.
  NOT yet tested live.
CONFLICTS:   supersedes this same session's earlier "all-or-nothing,
  matching Avid" note — that was an incomplete read of Avid's actual
  behaviour (Avid doesn't validate up front either; it fails mid-walk).
NEXT:        Charlie tests against a bank with at least one deliberately
  missing or renamed TFX file, confirms the prompt lists it correctly,
  and confirms Cancel truly writes nothing while Skip-and-continue writes
  everything else.

DATE:        2026-08-11
STATUS:      confirmed (retraction) — [FOLDED IN: Primer SLOT PICKER
             entry + Tech Ref Sec 15, both marked WITHDRAWN/RETRACTED
             in place, not deleted]
AREA:        Save to Different Slot / CMD 0x02-0x04 commit sequence
FINDING:     Tech Ref Sec 15's "protocol is identical to current save,
             only the target slot number changes" is WRONG. Confirmed by
             Charlie's live test of the newly-built slot picker: saving
             to a picked slot different from currentSlot committed to
             the ORIGINAL slot instead. This line had carried an
             unverified assumption since June-era research — every real
             save before today was always to the already-loaded slot
             (slot === currentSlot in every prior test), so the
             assumption had literally never been exercised, let alone
             confirmed, until this session's first live test of the
             genuinely-different-slot case.
             UNCONFIRMED WORKING THEORY: the commit sequence's Steps 2-4
             (CMD 0x03 dirty write, CMD 0x04 bank-index write, CMD 0x02
             final commit), all addressed to the target slot, may only
             update that slot's NAME-INDEX ENTRY and then navigate there
             — while the actual "commit the live edit buffer to a slot"
             operation is tied to whatever slot the rack's own firmware
             considers currently active internally, which our sequence
             never changes (deliberately, so as not to discard the live
             buffer by recalling the target first). Not yet confirmed by
             any capture — a theory to test, not a fix to ship.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's direct live-hardware test + follow-up multiple-
             choice confirmation ("Saved to the ORIGINAL slot instead").
             No packet capture yet — that's the NEXT step.
CONFLICTS:   Directly contradicts Tech Ref Sec 15's prior text (now
             marked WITHDRAWN in place, original wording preserved in a
             surrounding note per the Archive rule — never delete a
             retraction's context, only append the correction).
NEXT:        Charlie: Avid's own editor software does NOT have a
             save-to-a-different-slot feature, but the RACK'S OWN FRONT
             PANEL does. Plan: run Eleven Edit connected (so its passive
             SysEx monitoring/log captures whatever the rack broadcasts)
             while doing a genuine front-panel save-to-a-different-slot,
             then bring the resulting session log back for decoding —
             same method already used for Bug Reports #1/#2, no
             USBPcap/Wireshark needed since the app already sees this
             traffic passively.
             Added ahead of that test (sysex-handler.js, parseSysEx): a
             catch-all "UNHANDLED CMD 0x.. ..." logger for any incoming
             command byte that doesn't match a known case — previously
             silently dropped with zero log trace unless it happened to
             also be in PROBE_DUMP_CMDS. A front-panel save-as might use
             a command this app has never decoded, and silently dropping
             it would defeat the whole point of this capture.
             DO NOT attempt another blind byte-sequence fix here without
             that capture — this is the project's own "two failed fixes
             = stop and re-derive" rule, and blind-guessing a hardware
             WRITE command carries real risk (the existing Sec 15 note
             about a wrong Step 4 byte order once mis-sending saves to
             the factory-preset area is exactly the class of mistake to
             avoid repeating here).

             UPDATE 2026-08-11 — RESOLVED, capture obtained sooner than
             expected: Charlie found Avid's OWN software actually does
             have a "Save Rig To…" different-slot feature after all (he'd
             said it didn't, then caught his own correction — "Avid
             doesn't have this feature on software but the rack does" ->
             then sent a capture of exactly that Avid feature). Captured
             with Wireshark/USBPcap: AE_Save_to_Another_Slot_Capture.pcapng
             (Editor loaded to A1, nav'd to A4, then Save Rig To → Z4 with
             a name change). No tshark/scapy available in-session, so
             parsed by hand in Python — pcapng Enhanced Packet Blocks,
             then USB-MIDI CIN de-framing (CIN 0x4 continues, 0x5/0x6/0x7
             end with 1/2/3 valid bytes) to reconstruct the same clean
             "F0 13 0B 0F [dir] [cmd] ... F7" shape the app's own logs
             already show — matches the C1 0x04-framing note (Sec 22)
             exactly, same technique as the rest of this protocol's
             research.
             ROOT CAUSE FOUND: Step 3 (CMD 0x04, bank-index name write)
             uses [0x00 constant][raw_slot], NOT [bank][num] — Avid's
             capture for Z4 (raw slot 0x67) sent "00 04 00 67 <name> 00",
             our code was sending "00 04 19 03 <name> 00" (bank=0x19,
             num=0x03 for slot 103). Steps 2 and 4 were ALREADY correct
             (both use raw_slot, confirmed byte-identical to Avid's
             capture). The "byte-identical by coincidence for Bank A"
             theory from the retraction above is confirmed exactly right:
             for any A1-A4 target, bank=0=the real constant AND
             num=rawSlot, so the wrong formula silently produced correct
             bytes anyway — which is why this had never been caught
             despite the code existing since Phase 4a.
             BONUS FINDING: this exact mistake (bank/num split where the
             real format is [space-or-0x00][raw_slot]) had already been
             found and fixed ONCE before — on CMD 0x04's sibling REQU
             (read) form, sendPatchNameQuery (transport.js), 2026-08-03's
             "B1-B4 show a1-a4" bug. That fix was never generalized to
             this WRITE form, which is exactly how the same mistake
             survived here for months afterward. Worth remembering: a
             fix to one form of a shared command doesn't imply its
             sibling form got the same fix — check both explicitly.
             ALSO CONFIRMED FROM THE SAME CAPTURE: the hardware's full
             broadcast response to a different-slot save includes a
             spontaneous CMD 0x00 bulk TFX broadcast for the TARGET slot
             — exactly the "hardware save" signal handleBulkTfxData's
             isSave branch already watches for (Sec 15), so that
             mechanism needs no changes to generalize to this case.
             FIXED: saveCurrentPatchToSlot (capture-scan.js) Step 3 now
             sends "00 [slotHex]" instead of "[bankHex] [numHex]"; the
             now-unused bank/num locals removed. Tech Ref Sec 15's step
             table corrected in place (marked, not silently rewritten).
             STATUS -> [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 15 corrected table +
             re-confirmation note, Primer SLOT PICKER entry rewritten].
             TESTED LIVE, CONFIRMED WORKING (Charlie, 2026-08-11) — verified
             with real saves to non-Bank-A slots landing on the correct
             slot: "Nailed it works good now."

(All other folded / resolved / closed entries have been drained to the
Archive — see the index below.)

DATE:        2026-08-11
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Primer WORKING/Tech Ref Sec 15 cross-check]
AREA:        RigRollerPlus auto-advance / CMD 0x03
FINDING:     Roller self-paused 4x in an 11:26-12:01 session with no real
             hardware save. Root cause: handleSaveRigResponse (sysex-
             handler.js) treated CMD 0x03 data[7]===0x01 as "a save just
             happened" and called togglePause(). But per Tech Ref Sec 4/15,
             data[7] is a per-SLOT DIRTY flag (unsaved edits present on
             that slot), not a save event, and CMD 0x03 also fires as a
             side effect of ordinary CMD 0x02 slot navigation (Sec 6) —
             including the roller's own auto-advance. Landing on an
             already-dirty slot (leftover from earlier manual editing)
             falsely read as a hardware save. Confirmed against the
             uploaded log: all 4 pause events line up exactly with "PROBE
             resp CMD 0x03 ... 01" broadcasts following ordinary nav, not
             any front-panel save action.
EVIDENCE:    RigRollerPlus session-2026-08-11-112642.log lines 8327,
             15255, 16975, 21485 — each pause immediately follows an
             unsolicited CMD 0x03 broadcast (data[6]=slot, data[7]=0x01)
             with no preceding save action; cross-checked against the full
             set of CMD 0x03 variants in the same log (data[7] is 0x01 for
             specific slots throughout an ordinary bank walk at
             11:28-11:31, long before any save).
CONFLICTS:   none — clarifies Tech Ref Sec 4's existing "CMD 0x03 dirty
             flag" note, does not contradict it.
NEXT:        FIXED, 1st pass (WRONG SCOPE): deleted the CMD 0x03 dirty-
             flag pause trigger outright and routed roller-pause only
             through handleBulkTfxData's isSave (real-save) branch. Broke
             an existing, working feature — Charlie confirmed the dirty-
             flag trigger is what correctly pauses the roll when a user
             touches ANY control (bass, vol, etc.) mid-roll; that behavior
             is intentional and was not part of the reported bug.
             FIXED, 2nd pass (CORRECT SCOPE): restored the dirty-flag
             pause-on-edit trigger, but gated it with a settle-window
             guard — ROLLER_NAV_SETTLE_GUARD (2000ms, transport.js) —
             using a new lastNavTime timestamp set in goToSlot
             (patch-nav.js). A CMD 0x03 dirty broadcast arriving within
             2000ms of the last recall is treated as settle/query noise
             from the recall itself (matches worst-case NAV_RECALL_SETTLE
             + NAV_CHAIN_TIMEOUT + NAV_AMP_TIMEOUT + query gaps); one
             arriving after that window pauses the roll same as before.
             Real hardware-save pause (isSave branch) is untouched and
             coexists with this. NOT YET TESTED LIVE — needs (a) a full
             roll session with no false pauses on dirty slots, (b) a knob
             touch mid-roll (after settle) still pausing it, and (c) a
             real front-panel save during a roll still pausing it.

             UPDATE 2026-08-11 (later) — Charlie's live test confirmed the
             first-edit pause works and confirmed (via testing on main)
             that this gap predates today's changes. Diagnosed by Charlie:
             once dirty flag is 1, a second edit after Resume produces no
             new CMD 0x03 broadcast (nothing to announce — flag already
             set, and only a save clears it), so the roller never
             re-paused on the second+ edit within a dirty session.
             FIXED: added maybePauseRollerOnEdit(), shared by CMD 0x03
             (first edit, dirty-flag transition) and CMD 0x11 (every
             discrete param-change broadcast, unconditional — catches
             every subsequent edit the dirty flag alone would swallow).
             Both share the same ROLLER_NAV_SETTLE_GUARD gate. NOT YET
             TESTED LIVE — needs confirmation that a second/third control
             touch after Resume now pauses the roll again.

             UPDATE 2026-08-11 (later still) — Charlie's report of a missed
             touch confirmed against session-2026-08-11-124328.log: a real
             drag on tone Middle (paramLo 0x0b, 60->69->71) at ~2s after a
             nav did NOT pause, because it landed inside the CMD 0x11
             trigger's own 2000ms settle guard — the same guard meant to
             stop false pauses ended up eating a real one. Confirmed from
             the same log that the hardware's settle process re-broadcasts
             every tone/amp paramLo TWICE with the identical value right
             after "Nav pull complete" — so a time window can't cleanly
             separate settle noise from a fast genuine touch, since both
             can land in the same few seconds.
             FIXED: CMD 0x11's trigger no longer uses a time guard at all.
             paramSettleBaseline (state.js), keyed by instId:paramLo,
             cleared in goToSlot, records the last value seen for each
             param since the current nav. First sighting of a paramLo this
             nav = settle baseline, not a pause. Any LATER value that
             differs from that baseline = the knob actually moved = pause,
             regardless of elapsed time. CMD 0x03's dirty-flag trigger is
             unchanged (still time-guarded) — its settle-noise broadcasts
             were confirmed to arrive within milliseconds of the recall
             every time, so a time guard is sufficient there specifically.

             UPDATE 2026-08-11 (final) — Charlie confirmed the 1.3.12 miss
             reproduced every time once he knew the trick (touch right
             after nav, before settle). On 1.3.13 still reproducible but
             "a lot harder." CHARLIE'S CALL: good enough — STOP TUNING
             THIS for now, no further rounds without a new concrete
             repro/log. STATUS -> [FOLDED IN: Primer WORKING, 2026-08-11
             entries]. Bug Report #1 (RigRollerPlus false pauses) closed
             at this state.

DATE:        2026-08-27
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 7 / Sec 24 Change Log, Primer
             2026-08-27 SESSION SUMMARY] — confirmed — TESTED LIVE
             (Charlie, 2026-08-27): min/max/midpoint
             (0.0 at centre) all correct on both To Amp 1 and To Amp 2.
AREA:        To Amp 1/2 Volume / CMD 0x36 / sendToAmpVolume (transport.js)
FINDING:     Charlie's report: both To Amp knobs rotate correctly from mute
             to +12 but hardware never actually reads past +11.8 at the top
             end (min/mute end is fine). Root cause: sendToAmpVolume never
             got the endpoint-sentinel fix that sendParamWrite (CMD 0x11)
             got on 7/23/2026 for the same "9.9 bug" class of symptom. Tech
             Ref Sec 7 already documents CMD 0x36 as part of the same
             single-byte-offset-binary family as 0x11/0x07, sentinels
             included (min 0x40 00 00 00, max 0x3F 7F 7F 7F) - the fix was
             simply never applied to this specific send function. The old
             code always sent v0 + a flat "00 00 00 00" tail, which for the
             true top value (v0=0x3F) asks for the BOTTOM of that step, not
             the top - landing about one 24dB/127-step (~0.19 dB) short of
             +12.0, consistent with the reported 11.8. Min was unaffected by
             coincidence: 0x40 00 00 00 already IS the true minimum's exact
             encoding, so there's no "bottom of the step" gap to fall into
             at that end. FIXED: sendToAmpVolume (transport.js) now special-
             cases v127>=127 with tail "7F 7F 7F 7F" (true max) and
             v127<=0 with "00 00 00 00" (unchanged, already correct),
             matching sendParamWrite's approach but WITHOUT copying CMD
             0x11's extra final-byte 0x10-broadcast-offset quirk (0x0F in
             its own max sentinel) - that quirk is specific to CMD 0x11's
             message framing per Sec 7's TRAILING BYTES note and has no
             documented equivalent for CMD 0x36.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's live-hardware report (11.8 max, never 12.0); Tech Ref
             Sec 7 CONTINUOUS KNOBS entry naming 0x36 in the same family as
             0x11/0x07 with the same sentinel scheme; sendParamWrite's
             existing 7/23/2026 fix as the confirmed working pattern for
             this exact symptom shape.
CONFLICTS:   none — pure bug fix, no documented value changes.
NEXT:        None — closed. CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-08-27. Master (Main) Volume
             was flagged as a same-family risk but Charlie clarifies it is
             NOT a knob (it's the chain-strip stepper/value-box control,
             valToMasterVol) and is already spot-on at every value he's
             checked — the endpoint-sentinel gap is specific to the two
             CANVAS KNOB controls (To Amp 1/2) using sendToAmpVolume; no
             action needed on Master Volume.

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log, Primer 2026-08-27
             SESSION SUMMARY] — CLOSED — CHARLIE'S CALL (2026-08-27).
             Manual-knob display
             confirmed live: "solved like 99% of the issue." Sync-engaged +
             Expanded Delay showed a several-ms residual gap between HW
             truth and the display; root cause identified (not a new bug —
             see below) and Charlie explicitly decided NOT to chase it
             further: "who cares if our display is off by 6ms — nobody can
             hear that difference in the context of a delay." Do not
             reopen without a new concrete complaint from Charlie.
AREA:        DELAY / EP Tape Echo / Expanded Delay toggle / Delay knob display
FINDING:     EP Tape Echo's Delay knob display (protocol.js DELAY_MODELS,
             lo 0x04) was captured with Expanded Delay OFF only (70-600 ms,
             plain linear) — the original capture never accounted for the
             Expanded Delay toggle (lo 0x09) changing that range. Charlie's
             own eyeballed numbers (not yet Wireshark-verified): 20-2400 ms
             with Expanded Delay ON. Avid's own editor does NOT rescale its
             display when the toggle changes (a known Avid bug per
             Charlie) — ours now does. DISPLAY-ONLY CHANGE: the wire
             encoding/raw v127 sweep for the Delay knob is untouched, only
             the ms text shown is now toggle-aware. Added `affectedByToggle`
             on the Delay cell so updateDelayKnob (fx-panels.js) refreshes
             the Delay knob's displayed ms the moment Expanded Delay is
             flipped, even without moving the Delay knob itself.
             STILL OPEN, DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED THIS PASS: Charlie flagged
             that Sync-driven delay times may ALSO shift under Expanded
             Delay — two different possible shapes (A: same raw v0 per Sync
             division in both toggle states, just this same ms-display
             rescale applies once Sync hands the knob a raw value =
             "autopilot", nothing more to fix; B: hardware actually
             broadcasts a DIFFERENT raw v0 for the same named division
             depending on the toggle, meaning SYNC_DIVISIONS/
             syncIndexFromV127/syncV127FromIndex need a toggle-aware
             variant too). Charlie is testing this rescale live first,
             specifically to see whether Sync's readout now falls out
             correct on its own (case A) before we chase case B.
             ADDRESSED 2026-08-27 (3rd entry): BBD Delay's own Expanded
             Delay toggle (also lo 0x09) and Delay knob got the identical
             fix — see that entry. Dyn Delay has no Expanded Delay control
             at all (not applicable, confirmed by Charlie).
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's live-hardware eyeballing (20-2400 ms with Expanded
             Delay ON), not yet cross-checked against a Wireshark capture
             the way the original 70-600 ms range was.
CONFLICTS:   none.
RESOLUTION:  ROOT CAUSE OF THE RESIDUAL GAP (no code change, explanation
             only): the app never reads an ms value from hardware — the
             display is ALWAYS our own math (ms = lo + raw/127*(hi-lo)) on
             top of the raw 0-127 value HW actually sends. The ORIGINAL
             70-600 ms range was also hardware-eyeballed, same as the new
             20-2400 range — the difference is the original got cross-
             checked against 13 separate Sync-division points (Charlie's
             chart) and landed within 1 ms of each; the 20-2400 range is
             just the two endpoints, never point-by-point verified. Because
             the extended range is 4x wider, the same size of underlying
             endpoint/linearity error shows up ~4x bigger in ms on screen —
             expected amplification, not a distinct new bug. HW's own
             Sync behaviour is correct regardless (confirmed by Charlie);
             only our display math is approximate. Would need the same kind
             of multi-point Wireshark capture (raw v0 at several Sync
             divisions, Expanded Delay ON) to close the gap further, but
             per Charlie's call above, not worth doing.
NEXT:        None. Closed.

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log, Primer 2026-08-27
             SESSION SUMMARY] — CLOSED — TESTED LIVE, CONFIRMED (Charlie,
             2026-08-27): "Look
             good first try. Probably because you didn't remove the caret

AREA:        Chain row / ▼ caret removal (visual only, no protocol change)
FINDING:     Follow-up to the selection-ring entry above. Charlie: the
             carets have "no real purpose having moved what they did [to]
             the graphic handle" (thumb-click has opened/closed panels
             since 8/2/2026) — asked to remove them and let every effect
             block's label drop to the same visual baseline as GUITAR/
             STEREO/TEMPO. AMP-CAB (never had a caret) untouched.
FIXED:       .chain-open — visibility:hidden (NOT display:none, NOT
             removed from the DOM). This was a deliberate choice, not the
             obvious one: display:none would have let .chain-name's own
             margin-top:auto pull it down to fill the freed space, but
             landing it flush with the OLD caret's own bottom edge — 12px
             HIGHER than GUITAR/STEREO/TEMPO's already-established
             baseline (see below), not on the same plane at all.
             visibility:hidden instead preserves the caret's real
             reserved height (nothing reflows) while making it invisible
             and non-hit-testable — same trick #chain-input-wrap/
             #tempo-wrap ALREADY use for their own hidden carets (body
             markup, 7/28) — and crucially leaves .click() (used by the
             thumb-click-forwarding handler) and the .panel-open class
             (watched by the selection ring's MutationObserver) both
             completely unaffected, since neither depends on visibility.
             .chain-name — added transform:translateY(12px), the EXACT
             same nudge #mono-indicator/#chain-input-wrap/#tempo-wrap
             already use and that Charlie hand-picked off four rendered
             options on 7/28 (their hidden-caret position alone read "too
             high" without it) — reused rather than re-derived, since that
             number was only ever established by eye, never by formula,
             per this file's own 7/28 history. Also changed .chain-name's
             border-radius from 4px 4px 0 0 (flat bottom, assumed a caret
             glued below) to a plain 4px (standalone pill) now that
             nothing visible sits below it.
             Fully reversible: dropping the one visibility:hidden line
             brings the carets straight back, nothing else in this change
             depends on them being invisible (Charlie's own explicit ask
             before agreeing to this, given the chain row's history of
             half-day debugging sessions over small changes here).
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's request; the ALREADY-PROVEN GUITAR/STEREO/TEMPO
             hidden-caret + translateY(12px) pattern (7/28) reused
             directly rather than inventing new positioning math.
CONFLICTS:   none.

DATE:        2026-08-27 (6th entry)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log, Primer 2026-08-27
             SESSION SUMMARY] — CLOSED — TESTED LIVE, CONFIRMED (Charlie,
             2026-08-27): "I
             think it looks good now." Both screenshots (low and high
             value) show the split tick sitting cleanly above/below the
             track. No further action.
             FULL ROUND HISTORY (kept — a real multi-bug saga, not just a
             one-line fix): 1st report: correct build confirmed via
             About box, tick computed correctly (value/color logic all
             fine — the red thumb/fill proves dataset.orig existed and
             differed) but genuinely invisible on screen, not just subtle.
             ROOT CAUSE: a native <input type=range> paints its own
             track/thumb as a special replaced-element rendering layer
             that sits above ordinary sibling content regardless of DOM
             order — the tick <div> came AFTER the input in markup but was
             still being painted UNDER it. FIXED: gave the input
             position:relative + z-index:1 and the tick z-index:2 (z-index
             only compares POSITIONED elements, which is why this needed
             both sides, not just the tick).
             2ND ROUND: NO CHANGE ("build confirmed .35, no change" —
             Charlie offered to drop it after one more try). Re-derived
             instead of guessing a 3rd stacking fix (Primer's TWO-FAILED-
             FIXES rule) — found the REAL bug: updateBreakupColor
             (ui.js) set `tick.style.display = ''` to show the tick. That
             clears an INLINE override and falls back to the stylesheet,
             whose .sctrl-baseline rule IS display:none (the hidden-by-
             default state) — so that line never actually showed anything,
             on any of the previous rounds. The z-index changes were
             correct but irrelevant: there was nothing rendered to stack in
             the first place. FIXED: `tick.style.display = 'block'`
             instead of ''.
             3RD ROUND (consistency, not a bug): Charlie confirmed the
             tick now shows, but asked it match the knob's version, which
             sits OUTSIDE the ring rather than crossing through it. Split
             the tick into two short segments (top/bottom) via a linear-
             gradient with a transparent 6px gap through the middle,
             matching the track's own height so the gap lines up exactly
             with the groove/thumb instead of a solid line running through
             them.
AREA:        Speaker Breakup slider / baseline mark (visual only)
FINDING:     Charlie asked to extend the knob baseline-tick treatment
             (this session's earlier work: a short static mark showing the
             value a control loaded with) to Speaker Breakup — the one
             control in the app that's a native <input type=range>, not a
             .knob-wrap canvas (2026-08-03 rework, ui.js/index.html), so
             it already needed its own separate wiring into the amber/red
             "changed" contract and gets the same separate treatment here.
FIXED:       A native range input has no canvas to draw a tick on and no
             way to inject a positioned child INSIDE it, so the tick is a
             separate DOM element (#breakup-baseline, index.html) layered
             on top via a new positioning wrapper (.sctrl-track-wrap,
             sized to the input by default — no layout change to the
             slider itself). Positioned in JS (updateBreakupColor, ui.js)
             rather than drawn, corrected for the thumb's own 14px width
             (the thumb's CENTRE travels thumbW/2 to trackW-thumbW/2, not
             edge to edge — a plain 0-100% placement would drift from the
             thumb's real position near either end). Same light-grey
             (#c8c8c8), same "only shown once a baseline exists" rule as
             the knob version.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's request, direct continuation of this session's

DATE:        2026-08-27
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Primer.txt CURRENT APP STATE, 2026-08-28 wrap entry]
AREA:        Knob rendering — value-arc round-cap blob at tiny values
             (drawKnob, src/js/ui.js) — applies to EVERY knob, reported on
             FX LOOP Mix
FINDING:     Charlie reported a round dot sitting outside the knob's ring
             at very low values (e.g. FX Loop Mix at 1%), and that the dot
             disappears once the value hits exactly 0 (knob turns red at
             that point too, but the two are coincidental, not related).
             ROOT CAUSE: the value arc is stroked with lineWidth 5 and
             lineCap 'round'. At a small enough value, the swept angle is
             shorter than the cap's own width, so the two end-caps overlap
             almost entirely into a solid round blob instead of reading as
             a short line segment — and because a round cap bulges
             tangentially past its stroke's own endpoints, that blob can
             sit partly outside the ring's normal silhouette. At value127
             === 0 no arc is drawn at all (existing `if (value127 > 0)`
             guard), which is why the dot vanishes there — not because red
             fixes anything, just because nothing is drawn.
FIX:         drawKnob (ui.js ~line 67) now computes minSweepForRoundCap =
             5/(r-4) (the arc's own lineWidth as an angular width at this
             radius) and uses lineCap 'butt' instead of 'round' only when
             the swept angle is below that threshold; normal-sized arcs
             are unaffected and still get the polished rounded-cap look.
             Threshold is geometric (radius-relative), not a magic value
             count, so it should hold for any knob canvas size, not just
             FX Loop's.
EVIDENCE:    JS-only diff, src/js/ui.js drawKnob(). Not yet visually
             confirmed against a real drag to a low value on hardware.
             Version bumped 1.3.43 -> 1.3.44 (package.json).
CONFLICTS:   none
NEXT:        TESTED LIVE (Charlie, 2026-08-27) — confirmed fixed on FX
             Loop Mix. Not yet folded into Primer/Tech Ref masters — waits
             for a wrap signal per HOW UPDATES WORK.

AREA:        Factory patch navigation, Stage 1 of 4 (nav / jump list /
              auto-advance / save) — protocol.js, ui.js, patch-nav.js,
              sysex-handler.js, index.html
FINDING:     Charlie wants the app able to navigate/audition the Eleven
              Rack's 104 read-only factory patches (a1-z4), not just the
              104 user patches (A1-Z4) it has handled until now. Confirmed
              via a Wireshark capture Charlie made of the Avid Editor
              stepping Z3->Z4->a1->a2->a1->Z4->Z3 (uploaded pcapng,
              manually parsed here — pcapng/USBPcap framing + USB-MIDI CIN
              reassembly done by hand in Python, no tshark in-session, same
              method as the 7/31 FX1 captures) that the CMD 0x02 slot-
              confirm broadcast's data[6]/data[7] are a space flag
              (0=user, 1=factory) + raw slot 0-103 — the SAME convention
              already confirmed for the CMD 0x04 name-query command, not a
              bank/num pair as the code assumed.
DESIGN:       Rather than thread a separate "space" flag through every nav/
              roller/jump-list call site, the app now represents the full
              range as ONE continuous slot index 0-207 (0-103=A1-Z4,
              104-207=a1-z4, protocol.js MAX_NAV_SLOT). Two new helpers,
              slotToSpaceRaw()/spaceRawToSlot() (protocol.js), are the ONLY
              places that convert to/from the wire's space+rawSlot pair —
              everywhere else (range pickers, wrap/step math, display)
              just treats it as a bigger slot range, unchanged in shape.
              MAX_SLOT (103, user-only) is UNTOUCHED and still used exactly
              as before by bank-transfer.js (Export/Import All Rigs),
              slotNumFromLabel, and the save-to-rack picker — none of
              those are in scope for factory patches and none were
              touched.
FIX:          (1) sendPC (patch-nav.js) builds the recall command's space
              byte from the unified slot instead of a hardcoded 0x00. (2)
              handleSlotConfirm (sysex-handler.js) decodes data[6]/data[7]
              via the confirmed space+rawSlot formula — this RETRACTS the
              prior "commit echo: data[6]=bank,data[7]=num" theory, which
              turns out to have been indistinguishable from the space+slot
              reading for bank A (space=0 either way) and was apparently
              never actually confirmed for a non-A bank — same shape of
              bug as the CMD 0x04 addressing bug from 2026-08-03 (Sec 24).
              Flagged in-code as the first place to check if a save-commit
              echo on a non-A user bank ever mis-navigates. (3) slotLabel
              (ui.js) and updateNavDisplay (index.html, which had its own
              SEPARATE bank/letter computation duplicating slotLabel's —
              same bug independently) both now emit lowercase a1-z4 past
              Z4 instead of an out-of-range "?1". (4) populateRangeSelects
              (patch-nav.js) offers the full 0-207 range in the roller's
              from/to pickers; default selection is unchanged (still
              A1-Z4) so nothing changes unless a user deliberately expands
              the range into factory territory.
NOT DONE YET: Jump List left/right click zones (Stage 2), auto-advance
              range-picker LIVE testing (Stage 3 — the plumbing exists
              from this stage, and is already reachable/testable today via
              the roller's existing "Roll from/to" pickers, which now list
              the full 0-207 range — just not wired to any factory-aware
              UI beyond that), Save-to-Rack factory guard (Stage 4).
              CORRECTION (same day): the ◀BNK/BNK▶ whole-bank-step buttons
              were initially left user-only (clamped to MAX_SLOT) on the
              assumption they were a separate feature from "patch nav" —
              Charlie's own framing ("nav is nav") corrected this; there
              was never a real reason to split them. Fixed same session:
              btn-next-bank's clamp changed from hardcoded 103 to
              MAX_NAV_SLOT (index.html). Bank size (4) divides evenly into
              both the 104-slot user and factory ranges, so bank
              boundaries stay aligned straight across the Z4/a1 seam on
              the unified index with no special-casing needed — same
              free-of-charge payoff as step nav's wrap logic.

              Version bumped 1.3.44 -> 1.3.46 (package.json, two bumps this

DATE:        2026-08-28
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Primer.txt CURRENT APP STATE, 2026-08-28 wrap entry]
AREA:        Manual step nav (PCH>/PCH<, arrow keys) wrongly clamped to the
              Roll from/to range — patch-nav.js stepNav()
FINDING:     Live-tested follow-up to Stage 1 (factory patch nav): Charlie
              reported PCH> from Z4 landed on A1 (not a1), and PCH> from a4
              (reached via BNK>, confirmed correct — bank-step deliberately
              preserves slot-within-bank position across a bank change,
              not a bug) also landed on A1 instead of b1. ROOT CAUSE:
              stepNav (used by both the manual Prev/Next buttons/arrow
              keys AND shared, pre-dating today, by autoStep's timed
              auto-advance loop) wraps within getRangeFrom()/getRangeTo()
              — the Roll from/to pickers, which default to 0-103 (user
              only). That range exists to bound the TIMED auto-advance
              loop; manual single-stepping had no business sharing it, but
              did. So PCH> would silently snap back to A1 the instant you
              were anywhere the roll range didn't cover — including a4,
              reached validly via BNK>, which is outside the default
              range entirely.
FIX:          stepNav decoupled from the Roll from/to pickers — now wraps
              across the full 0-MAX_NAV_SLOT (207) range unconditionally,
              independent of whatever the roller's range is set to.
              autoStep/startAuto (the actual timed loop) are UNCHANGED and
              still respect the Roll from/to pickers exactly as before —
              that was always their correct job, just wrongly shared with
              manual stepping.
EVIDENCE:     JS-only diff, patch-nav.js stepNav(). Passes `node --check`.
              Version bumped 1.3.46 -> 1.3.47 (package.json). Diagnosis
              derived from Charlie's two reported cases (Z4->A1, a4->A1)
              both matching the shared-range-clamp math exactly; not yet
              re-tested live after the fix.
CONFLICTS:    none
NEXT:         NOT YET TESTED LIVE. Charlie to confirm PCH>/PCH< now cross
              Z4<->a1 and wrap z4<->A1 at the true ends, with auto-advance
              still respecting the Roll from/to range unchanged.

DATE:        2026-08-28
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Primer.txt CURRENT APP STATE, 2026-08-28 wrap entry]
AREA:        Firmware identity check / startup gate — state.js, transport.js,
              sysex-handler.js, ui.js, main.js, preload.js, src/splash.html
FINDING:     Charlie asked for a hard startup gate so nobody runs Eleven
              Edit against untested firmware — HARDWARE SAFETY, not
              cosmetic: CMD 0x37 (To Amp Source) is confirmed to brick the
              rack on assert (three separate incidents, full factory reset
              each time), and this app has zero visibility into whether an
              older firmware's memory layout is even compatible with any
              command it sends.
              Traced to a genuine, standard mechanism: a Wireshark capture
              of the Avid Editor's cold-start (Charlie's capture,
              "Avid_Startup_With_Rack_On.pcapng") showed a MIDI Universal
              SysEx Identity Request/Reply (F0 7E 7F 06 01 F7 / F0 7E
              <id> 06 02 <mfr> <family> <member> <4-byte revision> F7) —
              a different prefix from Avid's own 13 0B 0F command set
              entirely, which is why three separate "what does this
              unknown command do" investigations never found it. The
              4-byte revision field is plain ASCII: "0157" — Charlie's
              firmware, Version 2.0.1 Build 0.1.5.7. Same capture also
              turned up (but did not decode the meaning of) two of the
              four long-documented startup-only unknowns, CMD 0x35 (reply
              "02") and CMD 0x39 (reply "00") — Sec 6's "purpose unknown"
              note — plus reconfirmed 0x08/0x0A are per-patch state, not
              device info (their values tracked the loaded patch exactly
              across the earlier Z3-a2 nav capture).
DESIGN:       Sends the Identity Request once, only on the FIRST connect
              of a session (same scope as the existing no-hardware gate —
              a mid-session reconnect doesn't re-block, matching that
              gate's own "mid-session drop is the status bar's job" rule).
              FAILS CLOSED: checkInitialPopulateReady (transport.js) now
              also requires firmwareCheckDone before revealing the main
              window, and refuses to reveal at all if firmwareOk is false
              — whether that's an exact-mismatch reply OR no reply within
              FIRMWARE_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS (4000ms, armFirmwareCheckTimeout).
              No reply is treated exactly the same as a wrong reply, per
              Charlie's own call ("assume it won't [start] if it doesn't
              see the 0157") — a device that doesn't answer the check at
              all is not a device we've confirmed is safe.
              Reuses the EXISTING splash/gate machinery (the "can't find
              hardware" Try Again/Quit modal) rather than building a
              second one — extended splash-show-gate's IPC payload to
              carry an optional {reason, detail}, so splash.html shows a
              different explanatory message for a firmware mismatch and
              hides the Try Again button entirely (rescanning can't fix a
              firmware mismatch, so offering it would be actively
              misleading — Quit is the only real option).
EVIDENCE:     JS/HTML diff across 7 files, `node --check` clean on all
              (main.js, preload.js, state.js, sysex-handler.js,
              transport.js, ui.js, and splash.html's extracted inline
              script). Version bumped 1.3.49 -> 1.3.50 (package.json).
              Charlie's own literal words captured the intended fail
              mode: "Best I can do for test the gate is to confirm the
              program still starts and assume it won't if it doesn't see
              the 0157."
CONFLICTS:    none
NEXT:         NOT YET TESTED LIVE. Charlie to confirm: (a) normal startup
              on his known-good firmware still reaches the main window
              (the gate must NOT fire on the confirmed-working case,
              CRITICAL — a false positive here would make the app
              unusable), (b) appLog shows "Firmware identity reply: build
              "0157" (matches expected 0157)" during that normal startup.
              Testing the actual BLOCK path (b) requires either different
              firmware (which Charlie has said he doesn't have access to)
              or a deliberate temporary change to EXPECTED_FIRMWARE_BUILD
              to force a mismatch — worth doing once, briefly, just to
