AREA:        Stage 3 auto-advance — directional roll range + colour-coded
              picker options — patch-nav.js
FINDING:     Charlie confirmed Stage 3 (auto-advance across the factory
              boundary) already worked from Stage 1's plumbing alone — no
              code needed for the Z2->a2 direction. But Z2->A2 the OTHER
              way started immediately at A2 instead of walking
              z2,z3,z4,A1,A2 as intended. ROOT CAUSE: autoStep/startAuto/
              updateRangeLabel all used Math.min(from,to)/Math.max(from,to)
              to turn FROM/TO into a lo/hi numeric range — invisible with
              an all-user range (people naturally pick the lower letter as
              FROM anyway) but wrong now that factory space (104-207) sits
              numerically ABOVE all of user space (0-103): FROM=z2(205),
              TO=A2(1) silently normalized to lo=A2,hi=z2, discarding
              which one was actually typed as the start. Charlie correctly
              recalled this is the same "always runs low to high" shape as
              a pre-existing quirk in the original user-only code, just
              never visible before because the numeric range never
              crossed a a "reversal" the letters didn't already imply.
FIX:          FROM/TO reinterpreted as a DIRECTIONAL CIRCULAR walk instead
              of a numeric min/max range. New slotInRollRange(slot,from,to)
              helper replaces the old lo<=slot<=hi check with one that
              wraps through the top when from>to. autoStep now steps
              current+1 (wrapping 207->0) until it hits `to`, then jumps
              back to `from` — same result as before for the normal
              from<=to case, correctly walks the intended direction for
              from>to. updateRangeLabel/startAuto's status text now show
              FROM — TO in the order actually selected, not normalized.
              ALSO (Charlie's request, same conversation): the FROM/TO
              picker options are now colour-coded — factory (a1-z4)
              options render in KNOB_COLORS.blue, matching the accent
              already used for Parametric EQ's HF band, so the two ranges
              read as visually distinct in what would otherwise be one
              flat alphabetical list with no visual seam at Z4/a1.

DATE:        2026-08-28
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Primer.txt CURRENT APP STATE, 2026-08-28 wrap entry]
AREA:        Stage 4 (final stage) — Save-to-Rack factory guard —
              index.html, capture-scan.js
FINDING:     Completes the 4-stage factory-patch plan agreed 2026-08-27:
              Save to Disk needed no change (slot-agnostic, as agreed).
              Save to Rack, when the loaded patch is factory, must never
              default to writing at the current (illegal) factory address
              — found the real risk point in doRackSave's confirm handler:
              `var targetSlot = (saveTargetSlot !== null) ? saveTargetSlot
              : currentSlot;` — if a user opened Save to Rack on a factory
              patch and just clicked Save without redirecting via the
              picker, this would have sent a write to a factory slot with
              no defined hardware behaviour.
DESIGN:       New defaultRackSaveTarget() (index.html) is the ONE place
              that decides the default target: translates a factory
              currentSlot to the SAME alphanumeric user address (c4 -> C4,
              Charlie's own call from 2026-08-27) via slotToSpaceRaw's
              rawSlot, else just currentSlot as always. Used in BOTH
              openSaveModal (what the slot label shows before you touch
              anything) and doRackSave (what actually gets sent) — one
              function, not two copies that could drift apart. The
              picker's own user-only lock (openSlotPicker forcing
              matrixSpace=0, Stage 2) still covers the "redirected to a
              different slot" path, unchanged. updateSlotLabel's existing
              "currently: X" heads-up naturally fires for this new default
              too (translated target always differs numerically from a
              factory currentSlot), which is exactly the right warning to
              show.
              HARDWARE SAFETY BACKSTOP: saveCurrentPatchToSlot
              (capture-scan.js) now refuses outright (logs + returns, no
              write sent) if ever called with slot > MAX_SLOT, regardless
              of how it got called — a last line of defense if the UI-side
              logic above is ever wrong or bypassed, same "refuse rather
              than guess" pattern already used for Amp Out (no real value
              received yet) elsewhere in this file.
EVIDENCE:     JS/HTML diff, index.html + capture-scan.js. Both pass
              node --check (index.html's inline script extracted and
              checked separately). Version bumped 1.3.54 -> 1.3.55
              (package.json).
CONFLICTS:    none
NEXT:         NOT YET TESTED LIVE — completes the whole 4-stage factory-
              patch plan pending this final live confirmation. Charlie to
              confirm: (a) opening Save to Rack while on a factory patch
              shows the translated user slot pre-filled (with the
              "currently: X" warning), not the factory slot itself, (b)
              clicking Save without redirecting actually writes to that
              translated user slot and NOT the factory address, (c) the
              slot-label picker still redirects to any other user slot

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Technical_Reference.txt Sec 15 + CHAIN/

AREA:        HARDWARE SAFETY — SAVE sequence with an out-of-range slot
              (CMD 0x04 write form) confirmed to corrupt hardware state
FINDING:     Charlie live-confirmed a real, serious hardware danger:
              sometime between Stage 1 (currentSlot decode fix) and Stage
              4 (before the save guard existed), he did Save to Rack while
              on a factory patch. currentSlot correctly read as factory
              (>103) at that point, but Save to Rack still defaulted
              straight to currentSlot and sent that raw value directly as
              the save sequence's slot byte — no translation, no
              boundary check. RESULT: the name update partially/visibly
              took on hardware (briefly shown in the factory slot), but a
              second test (name-only save, then nav-away-and-back) showed
              the write had NOT actually persisted — the original factory
              name was still there. Further interaction with the unit
              after the first attempt caused a BSOD. Per Charlie: "I
              guess what I was doing was somehow writing back the name to
              a normally unreachable or unwriteable section of fw or
              memory and it seems to work but further fiddling make it
              crash." This is now closed by Stage 4 (already shipped,
              1.3.55) two independent ways — see that stage's own log
              entry — but the finding itself is permanent, hardware-level
              knowledge that belongs in the masters immediately, per the
              Primer's own "fold immediately, hardware-safety item"
              exception, not held for a wrap.
FOLDED TO:    ElevenEdit_Technical_Reference.txt Sec 15 (SAVE PROTOCOL) —
              new *** HARDWARE SAFETY *** block right after the
              raw_slot=0-103 statement, spelling out the confirmed
              partial-write/BSOD behaviour and cross-referencing both
              guards. Also added a matching one-line pointer in the
              existing CHAIN/DANGER "do not do X" catalog (Sec 22-ish,
              same neighbourhood as the CMD 0x37 bricking entry it's
              modeled on) so a future session scanning that list for
              known dangers finds this one too.
EVIDENCE:     Charlie's own live test, described in conversation (no
              capture — the interesting bytes were the SAME save sequence
              already fully documented in Sec 15, just with an
              out-of-range raw_slot; the new information is the hardware
              BEHAVIOUR on receiving it, not new protocol bytes).
CONFLICTS:    none — this is new information, not a correction to
              anything previously documented.

              are what prevent recurrence going forward.

              bounds) — a real rendering bug, not a missing feature. (2)

              rename — same as any other realtime patch edit, marking the

              is actually chosen.
FIX:          (1) Ellipsis truncation moved from #patch-name-box onto
              #patch-name itself (display:inline-block, its own overflow/
              ellipsis) — frees the box from needing overflow:hidden, so
              .hint now actually renders below it. Also added a dotted-
              underline hover cue on the name text itself, distinct from
              the box's existing border-brighten hover, so the two click
              targets read as different affordances.
              (2) startPatchNameEdit() (index.html) swaps the name span
              for a text input (maxlength 16, matching the hardware name
              limit) on click; Enter/blur commits (updates currentPatchName,
              calls markPatchDirty(), logs — no SysEx sent), Escape
              cancels. The box's click handler is now ONE delegated
              handler checking e.target.id, not two separate bindings —
              rename's commit/cancel REPLACES the span with a fresh
              element each time, which would silently break a listener
              bound directly to the old node; delegation on the box
              (which never gets replaced) survives that.
              THREE REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED ALONG THE WAY, all from the
              same root cause (existing code assuming #patch-name always
              exists, which the new edit-mode breaks): updateDisplay
              (ui.js) would have thrown on ANY nav that happened mid-edit
              (front-panel wheel, auto-advance) — now null-guards AND
              cancels any in-progress edit first, since a nav means the
              edit no longer applies to what's loaded. handlePatchName
              (sysex-handler.js, CMD 0x05 broadcast) would have both
              crashed AND silently overwritten a pending local rename with
              hardware's own value — now skips entirely while mid-edit,
              same "broadcast must not fight an in-progress user action"
              rule as R9 elsewhere in this codebase. The two existing
              patch-name writers in capture-scan.js were already null-
              guarded, no change needed there.

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: ElevenEdit_Primer.txt CURRENT APP STATE, 2026-08-28 wrap entry]
AREA:        Click-to-rename crash + hint still invisible — index.html,
              ui.js (follow-up to the 1.3.56 entry)
FINDING:     Charlie hit an uncaught error typing a 16-char name and
              pressing Enter: "Failed to execute 'replaceWith' on
              'Element': The node to be removed is no longer a child of
              this node." ROOT CAUSE: removing a FOCUSED element from the
              DOM fires 'blur' on it synchronously — so Enter's commit()
              called input.replaceWith(span), which itself triggered the
              same input's still-attached blur listener a moment later,
              calling commit() a SECOND time on an already-detached node.
              Also: the hint text was STILL invisible after the 1.3.56 CSS
              fix — found a SECOND layer of the same problem: #topbar
              itself carries height:64px + overflow:hidden (a fixed-height
              toolbar), which clips the hint just as the box's own
              overflow:hidden did before, one layer up.
FIX:          (1) Rename's finish() is now idempotent via input._settled,
              checked at the top and set BEFORE any DOM replacement — every
              path (Enter, Escape, blur, and updateDisplay's forced-cancel
              on a mid-edit nav) is now first-caller-wins, every later
              call a no-op. updateDisplay (ui.js) also sets editEl._settled
              = true before its OWN replaceWith, closing the same race from
              the external nav-mid-edit path, not just the internal
              Enter/blur one. (2) Rather than chase a THIRD ancestor that
              might someday also clip an absolutely/negatively-positioned
              child, .hint moved into NORMAL DOCUMENT FLOW, stacked under
              the name via #patch-name-box's own new flex-column layout —
              sidesteps ancestor overflow:hidden entirely instead of
              fighting it one layer at a time. Confirmed it still fits:
              topbar is 64px, the box's own content (padding+border+name
              line+hint line) comes to well under that.
EVIDENCE:     HTML/JS diff, index.html + ui.js. Both pass node --check.
              Version bumped 1.3.56 -> 1.3.57 (package.json).

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE) — this

AREA:        Amp-select never re-verified after a chain-map refresh —
             requestAllBypass (transport.js)
FINDING:     Charlie captured a clean Wireshark trace of the Avid Editor
             doing the IDENTICAL sequence (Save to Rack to the same slot,
             then FX1 model change Parametric EQ <-> Graphic EQ) with NO
             corruption. Decoded it (wrote a pure-Python pcapng + USB-MIDI
             CIN reassembler, no tshark/scapy available in-session) and
             diffed it against this app's equivalent traffic:
               - Avid's own CMD 0x21 model-change WRITE is byte-for-byte
                 structurally identical to sendFxHostModelChange's — not a
                 malformed-write bug.
               - Avid ALSO saves to the same slot first — that alone isn't
                 what protects it.
               - The amp select value (CMD 0x11 paramLo 0x0F) never once
                 changed across Avid's whole capture — confirmed by
                 checking every amp-select query/reply in the trace
                 (before save, after save, before model change, after
                 model change): always 0x0B. So this is NOT unavoidable
                 hardware behavior that happens regardless of which
                 software drives it.
               - Avid explicitly RE-QUERIES amp select (paramLo 0x0F) every
                 single time the chain map changes — initial load, after
                 the save, and again after the model change. This app's own
                 log (67cc3df5) was grepped for every "Amp Select" line —
                 found exactly ONE, at initial connect, and never again for
                 the rest of the session. Confirmed real, standing gap:
                 this app has literally no code path that would ever
                 notice or update its display if the amp changed after the
                 initial connect — matching Charlie's own observation that
                 the amp visibly changes on the RACK'S OWN FRONT PANEL in
                 this bug scenario but nothing arrives over the wire to
                 update the app (whereas a manual front-panel amp change
                 normally does broadcast and update the app fine).
EVIDENCE:    f94123e7-AE_Save_to_Rack_then_FX1_changes..pcapng (Avid,
             clean) vs. 67cc3df5-session20260829101823.log (this app,
             corrupts — only one "Amp Select" line in the whole log).
CONFLICTS:   none.

             (amp select) for the amp block, alongside its existing 0x06/
             0x14 bypass queries — matching Avid's own proven behavior.

DATE:        2026-08-29 (ninth round, same day)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE) — strongest
             lead of the whole investigation: a concrete, byte-level
             difference between this app and the Avid Editor, visible in
             both captures, not another guess.
AREA:        Post-save query flood injected mid-save-transaction
             (sysex-handler.js isSave branch -> moved to capture-scan.js
             saveCurrentPatchToSlot, after the commit)
FINDING:     1.3.66's amp-select re-query CONFIRMED the corruption in this
             app's OWN log for the first time: model change at 10:28:01,
             amp readback flips 0x0B Treadplate -> 0x23 DC Modern OD at
             10:28:02, queried on the correct new handle. Real, not
             inferred. Then answered Charlie's standing question ("if
             same-slot and different-slot save do the same thing, why does
             only same-slot break?") by actually byte-diffing the two:
               - DIFFERENT-slot save (Z1, be90309a log): the commit
                 navigates hardware to the new slot -> "Slot confirmed: 100
                 (Z1)" -> full Nav pull -> hardware RELOADS the patch fresh
                 into its live buffer. handleSlotConfirm's slot-changed
                 guard fires.
               - SAME-slot save (C4): commit says "go to C4" but already on
                 C4 -> no slot change -> handleSlotConfirm does NOTHING ->
                 no reload. Buffer keeps its post-save state.
             BUT: Avid ALSO saves same-slot (confirmed in its pcapng) and
             does NOT corrupt, so "same-slot leaves a dirty buffer" can't be
             the whole story. Byte-diffed the two save SEQUENCES and found
             the one thing this app does that Avid doesn't:
               - Avid order: name (05) -> dirty (03) -> bank-index (04) ->
                 COMMIT (02) -> THEN chain-map read (01 21) + param queries.
                 All four save commands first, queries only after commit.
               - This app: name (05) -> dirty (03) -> [the dirty write's
                 bulk broadcast triggers the isSave branch, which
                 IMMEDIATELY fires a chain-map read + bypass/amp/param query
                 flood] -> bank-index (04) -> COMMIT (02). The re-read and
                 flood are injected BETWEEN the dirty flag and the commit —
                 into the middle of the hardware's own save transaction.
                 Plainly visible in the 10:27:48 timestamps: "Post-save:
                 re-reading chain map" and the CMD 0x11 queries all land
                 before "Save commit sent".
             This mid-transaction poking is the prime suspect for leaving
             the hardware in the state where the NEXT chain rewrite (an
             FX-host model change) corrupts the amp. Different-slot save
             hides it because the post-commit navigation reloads the buffer
             fresh afterward.
EVIDENCE:    f94123e7 Avid pcapng (msgs 0-6,11: all 4 save cmds then commit
             then queries) vs. b0b9794b log (10:27:48: re-read + queries
             before "Save commit sent" at 10:27:49). Amp flip:
             b0b9794b 10:28:01->10:28:02.
CONFLICTS:   retracts 1.3.65's "missing settle delay" theory (settle didn't
             help — Charlie waited 36s and it still broke) and 1.3.66's
             amp-select re-query as a FIX (it was diagnostic, not a fix —
             still correct to keep, it's what caught the flip in-log).
FIX APPLIED: (1) sysex-handler.js isSave branch — the immediate post-save
             chain-map re-read now runs ONLY for a hardware front-panel save
             (saveSequenceIsHardware; the hardware already committed before
             broadcasting, so it's not mid-transaction there). For a
             software save it no longer fires here. (2) capture-scan.js
             saveCurrentPatchToSlot — added a step 5 AFTER the commit
             (CMD 0x02): settle, then REQU_CHAIN_MAP. This matches Avid's
             ordering exactly (all four save commands, THEN the queries) and
             still refreshes the reassigned handles a same-slot save needs.
             Version bumped 1.3.66 -> 1.3.67.
NEXT:        CHARLIE TO TEST LIVE: the same-slot Save to Rack + FX1
             model-change sequence. If the amp no longer flips (watch the
             new "Amp Select readback" line — it should stay Treadplate),
             this ordering difference was the cause. If it STILL flips even
             with queries moved after the commit, the next definitive step
             is a Wireshark capture of THIS APP failing, to byte-diff
             against the Avid clean capture already in hand and find any
             remaining divergence.

             requestPatchStateAfterNav, which always waits NAV_RECALL_SETTLE
             before querying anything) when the confirmed slot DIFFERS from
             currentSlot. A different-slot save trips that guard (real slot
             change) and gets the full, settled resync for free. A
             same-slot save never trips it — confirmed===currentSlot,
             guard skipped — leaving ONLY the isSave branch's own narrower
             "Post-save: re-reading chain map" REQU_CHAIN_MAP resend, which
             had NO settle delay before it at all, unlike every other
             post-commit/post-nav re-read in the app. If hardware hasn't
             finished reassigning handles yet when that resend goes out,
             the chain-map reply (and therefore currentChain) can be
             stale — and a subsequent FX-host model change builds its
             10-block chain-array write from that stale currentChain,
             embedding wrong handles for the other 9 blocks. Matches every
             observation: same-slot only, right after save only, breaks
             specifically at the model-change write.
             SEPARATELY: while re-reading this code, found the 1.3.64
             pacing fix has its own real bug — requestAllBypass and
             requestFxHostParams are both now async/paced, but nothing
             stops two from running AT THE SAME TIME if triggered around
             the same event; their individual awaited sends round-robin on
             the event loop and interleave on the wire (confirmed in
             Charlie's f5c82674 log, lines ~2447-2472 — a bypass query and
             an FX-host param query shuffled together mid-burst). Before
             1.3.64 each burst was atomic/synchronous, so this never
             happened; the pacing fix traded one hazard for a smaller,
             different one.
EVIDENCE:    be90309a-session20260829092822.log (different-slot save, Z1,
             clean) vs. the prior several same-slot-save logs (all showed
             the corruption or its precursor). f5c82674 log for the
             interleaving.
CONFLICTS:   none.
FIX APPLIED: (1) sysex-handler.js isSave branch — added
             `await sleep(NAV_RECALL_SETTLE)` before the post-save
             REQU_CHAIN_MAP resend, matching what requestPatchStateAfterNav
             already does for every other post-nav/post-recall re-read.
             (2) transport.js — added a small shared serialization queue
             (runPacedBurst/pacedBurstQueue) so paced bursts run one at a
             time instead of interleaving; requestAllBypass and
             requestFxHostParams (fx-transport.js) both now route through
             it (renamed to *Impl internally, public name unchanged, no
             call-site changes needed). Version bumped 1.3.64 -> 1.3.65.
NEXT:        CHARLIE TO TEST LIVE: repeat the exact same-slot Save to Rack
             + FX1 model-change sequence that broke before. If clean, this
             is very likely the real fix. Keep the different-slot-save
             comparison in mind as the control case either way — it's the
             cleanest reproducible signal found so far in this whole
             investigation.

DATE:        2026-08-29 (sixth round, same day)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE) — Charlie's
             own instinct ("could this be the timing/flood gotcha again"),
             not a fresh capture — going in alongside the narrower-capture

AREA:        Unpaced query bursts — requestAllBypass (transport.js),
             requestFxHostParams (fx-transport.js)
FINDING:     Both fire their entire query list (11+ and up to 15
             respectively) in one synchronous burst with ZERO delay between
             sends — no pacing at all, unlike every other query burst in
             the app (requestPatchStateAfterNav and friends, transport.js,
             all paced with NAV_QUERY_GAP=15ms). Same shape as the
             pre-existing, already-fixed "knob-write flood" bug (Primer:
             unthrottled knob/slider writes could desync the amp handle and
             freeze knobs — fixed by queueKnobSend's 60ms throttle).
             requestAllBypass runs right after EVERY chain-map refresh,
             including the one immediately after every Save — i.e. right on
             top of whatever traffic a block/model edit just put in flight.
             requestFxHostParams fires every time an FX-host panel opens,

             above). NOT proven causal — the log has a blind ~66s gap
             around the actual corruption moment (no full bulk readback in
             that window) so this can't be confirmed from log evidence
             alone, only judged plausible from pattern-matching this
             project's own history of exactly this failure mode.
CONFLICTS:   none.
FIX APPLIED: both functions converted to async and paced with the existing
             NAV_QUERY_GAP interval (15ms) between each sendHex call — the
             same interval already used everywhere else, not a new one
             invented for this. No call site needed changes (both were
             already fire-and-forget). Version bumped 1.3.63 -> 1.3.64.
NEXT:        CHARLIE TO TEST LIVE: repeat the FX1 bypass-toggle +

DATE:        2026-08-29 (fourth round, same day)
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE) — needs a
             Wireshark capture to nail the exact wire format with certainty,
             this fix is a best-effort heuristic from ONE live sample
CONFIRMED:   (a) and (b) from the prior entry's NEXT — Save to Rack now
             produces exactly one file (plain name); Save to Disk produces
             one file (plain name, incremented on a genuine name conflict —
             confirmed correct by Charlie). (d) not yet retested — Charlie
             moved on to testing (c) and found it broken before circling
             back to the FX1 model-change repro.
AREA:        Hardware front-panel save detection — handleCmd04 /
             handlePatchNameEnumReply (sysex-handler.js)
FINDING:     (c) FAILED — Charlie pressed the real front-panel SAVE button
             twice; NEITHER produced a TFX file. Root cause: the original
             CMD 0x04 discrimination (Primer/code comment, long-standing,
             predates this session) assumed a real hardware save's arm
             broadcast is SHORT (<=10 bytes, no name) and only a name-query
             reply is long (>10 bytes, has a name) — so handleCmd04 routed
             anything long straight to handlePatchNameEnumReply, never to
             handleSaveArm. Charlie's live capture shows this assumption is
             WRONG: a genuine front-panel save's CMD 0x04 broadcast DOES
             carry the embedded name (same [00][slot][name] shape as a name
             reply) — so it always landed in the "long" bucket and NEVER
             reached the save-arm logic. This may mean hardware-save
             auto-capture has never actually worked via this code path in
             the app's history (Charlie's original "the bug has been there
             a very long time" framing, though this is a different specific
             mechanism than the chain/bypass bug fixed earlier today).
             The two formats ARE distinguishable, just not by overall
             length>10: a plain name-echo/scan-reply always ends exactly 2
             bytes after its name's null terminator (terminator + F7,
             constant regardless of name length: total length = 10 +
             nameLength, algebraically, confirmed against 2 independent
             26-byte and 24-byte real examples in earlier logs). A real
             hardware save's CMD 0x04 pads much further past the
             terminator — ~41 bytes total for the same 16-char name
             "Driftwood Purple" vs. 26 for a plain echo of the identical
             name — presumably a fuller "slot committed" structure with
             reserved/status bytes beyond just the name.
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-08-29-081455.log, 08:15:53 (Charlie's first real
             front-panel save press): "IN BRIDGE [41b] F0 13 0B 0F 02 04 00
             0B 44 72 69 66 74 77 6F 6F 64 20 50 75 72 70 6C 65 00 00 00 00
             00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00" — routed to
             handlePatchNameEnumReply (just logged "Patch name scan: C4 =
             ..."), never armed anything, so the following "Bulk data: ...
             isSave=false" (line 1833) skipped the whole capture-to-disk
             path. Same shape repeated at 08:16:31 (Charlie's second press),
             same result — no file either time.
CONFLICTS:   Retracts the "existing, confirmed behaviour" framing on
             handleSaveArm/the short-form arm broadcast in the comment
             added earlier today (armSaveSequence) — it was never actually
             confirmed against live hardware-save traffic, only assumed
             from older documentation. Tech Ref's CMD 0x04 section needs a
             pass at wrap to reflect this.
FIX APPLIED: handlePatchNameEnumReply (sysex-handler.js) now checks the
             padding past the name's null terminator: if space is user
             (0x00) and more than ~4 bytes follow the terminator, treat it
             as a real hardware save too and call armSaveSequence(rawSlot,
             ..., true) in addition to the normal name-cache update (which
             still runs regardless — the name is genuinely correct either
             way). handleSaveArm/the short-form path is left in place
             untouched in case it's real for something else (e.g. a
             bank-load-only signal, per the existing chain/save-vs-nav
             comment) — this is additive, not a replacement.
NEXT:        CHARLIE TO TEST LIVE: press the real front-panel SAVE button
             and confirm a "_manual"-suffixed TFX now appears, then continue
             to (d) — the Save-to-Rack-then-model-change repro, still not
             retested. If a real save STILL produces no file, the 4-byte
             padding threshold or the space/slot parse itself needs a fresh
             Wireshark capture rather than another guess — flag that
             explicitly rather than iterating blind a third time on this
             one signal.

             block manipulations... never cause other things to happen."

             Charlie's own mental model of how this was originally built
             (matching Avid's simpler per-action save behaviour): he expected
             a hardware front-panel save to be the one marked "_manual" (an
             unprompted, out-of-band capture), and both software actions
             (Save to Rack, Save to Disk) to write the plain name — the code
             had it exactly reversed.
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-08-29-072932.log — bypass state matched exactly

             sleep(500)+captureCurrentPatchNow() pull entirely. Save to Rack

STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE)
AREA:        AMP chain-row bypass state — updateCabMicReadouts (ui.js)
FINDING:     The isSave-arming fix above (armSaveSequence) was confirmed
             correctly detecting the software save this time (log shows
             "Software save — arming...", "isSave=true", "Post-save:
             re-reading chain map...", "requestAllBypass...", and "Bypass:

             updateCabMicReadouts (ui.js ~line 876, pre-existing code, not
             something either save fix touched) unconditionally set
             blockBypass[SLOT_AMP] = undefined whenever the TFX body decode's
             ampActive comes back null — which it ALWAYS does, because amp
             active/bypass has no TFX key and is never present in a body
             decode (the comment directly above it already said as much —
             the comment was right, the code below it didn't follow its own
             logic). saveToRackAndDisk's own trailing step (a manual-capture
             SEND_PATCH pull, done purely to write the .tfx to disk) reuses
             this same body-decode/updateCabMicReadouts path with isSave
             correctly false (it's not a hardware-state-changing event) —
             but that path still clobbered the AMP state that
             requestAllBypass had JUST correctly resolved moments earlier,
             with nothing left afterward to fix it again. cabBypassActive
             right next to it already had the correct guard (only overwrite
             when the decode has a real value) — blockBypass[SLOT_AMP] did
             not.
EVIDENCE:    session-2026-08-29-072226.log: 07:23:39 "Bypass: AMP -> ACTIVE"
             (correct, from post-save requestAllBypass) -> 07:23:40 "Bulk
             data: ... isSave=false" (the saveToRackAndDisk disk-capture
             pull, decodeCabMicValues ampActive=null as always) -> 07:24:05
             "Amp bypass state unknown yet, ignoring click".
CONFLICTS:   none.
FIX APPLIED: ui.js updateCabMicReadouts — blockBypass[SLOT_AMP] (and the
             display driven by it) now only gets overwritten when
             cabMic.ampActive is a real (non-null/undefined) value, mirroring
             the guard cabBypassActive already had; otherwise the existing
             known value (and its display) is left alone instead of being

DATE:        2026-08-29
STATUS:      [FOLDED IN: Tech Ref Sec 24 Change Log 2026-08-29 + Primer CURRENT APP STATE 2026-08-29] - orig: provisional (code fix written, NOT YET TESTED LIVE — Charlie's
             build/test step still pending)
AREA:        Save (Rack + Disk) / chain row bypass state / block handles —
             saveSequenceDetected (sysex-handler.js) / saveCurrentPatchToSlot
             (capture-scan.js)
FINDING:     Root cause of Charlie's "everything goes weird after ANY save"
             bug report (chain blocks can't be enabled/disabled, AMP shows
             wrong/dead state, some chain actions turn on multiple blocks) —
             present since at least v1.3.31, reproduced from Charlie's
             session-2026-08-29-070012.log. handleBulkTfxData's isSave check
             (saveSequenceDetected && slotNum===saveSequenceSlot) is the ONLY
             gate on running the post-save recovery block (REQU_CHAIN_MAP
             resend + the requestAllBypass it triggers, which is what
             refreshes stale block handles/bypass after the hardware
             reassigns handles on a save — see the existing comment at
             sysex-handler.js ~line 187). saveSequenceDetected is armed ONLY
             by handleSaveArm, which fires on an INCOMING short CMD 0x04
             broadcast (<=10 bytes, no embedded name) — the real shape of a
             HARDWARE FRONT-PANEL save's arm signal. A SOFTWARE-initiated
             save (saveCurrentPatchToSlot, i.e. the app's own Save to
             Rack/Disk) never produces that broadcast — the only CMD 0x04
             traffic it ever sees is the long, name-bearing echo of its own
             step-3 bank-index write (>10 bytes), which handleCmd04 correctly
             routes to handlePatchNameEnumReply instead, not handleSaveArm.
             Net effect: saveSequenceDetected stayed false for the entire
             life of every software-triggered save, so its bulk broadcast
             (confirmed in the log arriving mid-sequence, BEFORE the actual
             commit/CMD 0x02 write even goes out — apparently provoked by the
             CMD 0x03 dirty-flag clear) always fell into the "NOT A SAVE"
             branch and never refreshed the chain map / bypass state —
             exactly matching every symptom reported.
EVIDENCE:    Charlie's log, 07:02:32-33: "Bulk data: 1352b slot=11 (C4)
             saveSeq=false saveSlot=-1 isSave=false" during a real "Saving
             current patch to C4" sequence — contrast with a genuine chain-
             map-triggering event (e.g. 07:00:14, 07:01:07, 07:01:42) where
             "requestAllBypass: queried 11 bypass flags across 10 blocks"
             always follows. No "Post-save: re-reading chain map..." line
             (sysex-handler.js's isSave-only log) appears anywhere after the
             07:02:32 save. Post-save (07:02:37 on), "Amp bypass state
             unknown yet, ignoring click" and a burst of 8 identical
             sendBypassWrite 0x78 ACTIVE calls (07:02:37-07:02:48) with no
             matching confirm broadcasts — consistent with stale/unrefreshed
             blockBypass and block handles after the hardware reassigned them
             on save.
CONFLICTS:   none — this is a gap, not a contradiction of a documented fact.
FIX APPLIED: sysex-handler.js — extracted the arming logic out of
             handleSaveArm into a shared armSaveSequence(slot, source)
             function. capture-scan.js — saveCurrentPatchToSlot now calls
             armSaveSequence(slot, 'Software save') itself, right before it
             sends anything, so its OWN bulk broadcast (whenever it lands,
             including the early one seen in the log) is correctly recognized
             as isSave and runs the existing chain-map-refresh/bypass-requery
             recovery. Version bumped 1.3.58 -> 1.3.59.
NEXT:        CHARLIE TO TEST LIVE: Save to Rack (and Save to Disk) repeatedly,
             back-to-back, then immediately try toggling several chain blocks
             (incl. AMP) and confirm they respond correctly and show true
             state, with no multi-block cross-talk. This is an
             INTERMITTENT/cumulative-feeling bug per Charlie's report ("more
             of these are being used more real world one after the other") —
             per Primer's own rule, needs MORE THAN ONE clean pass before
             being called TESTED LIVE, not just one green save/toggle cycle.
